The 4 sites, all fixed this session to match the already-established pattern for their key elsewhere in the codebase: (1) **`transferForInvestigationController.js:38-41`** — the most severe: a genuinely half-migrated site where the *guard* had already been correctly changed to `if (sessionStorage.getItem('bedManagmentCookies') != null)` but the read one line below was still `JSON.parse($cookies.get('bedManagmentCookies'))` — since nothing writes `bedManagmentCookies` via `$cookies` anymore (it's written to `sessionStorage` elsewhere), this would pass the guard and then throw on `$cookies.get` returning `undefined` being passed to `JSON.parse`; fixed to `JSON.parse(sessionStorage.getItem('bedManagmentCookies'))`. (2) **`admissionRequestController.js:219-222`** — two live `$cookies.get()` reads (`selectedPageName`, `selectedPageNameForAdmissionNote`) migrated to `sessionStorage.getItem(...)`. (3) **`dischargeMedicationReconcilationController.js:119-123`** — two live `$cookies.getObject('nursingStationDesc')` calls (a log line and an equality comparison) migrated to `JSON.parse(sessionStorage.getItem('nursingStationDesc'))`. (4) **`IPDCoverSheetController.js:29002-29008`** — a live `$cookies.putObject`/`getObject` read-write pair for `specialtyNursingStationCookies` migrated to `sessionStorage.setItem('specialtyNursingStationCookies', JSON.stringify(...))` / `JSON.parse(sessionStorage.getItem('specialtyNursingStationCookies'))`. **Honest root-cause of why three prior "exhaustive" passes each still missed live sites, stated directly rather than re-asserting "0 remaining" a fourth time:** (a) every prior scan — including the purpose-built tokenizer from the addendum immediately above — was written and validated against multi-line splits *known at the time* (e.g. `$cookies` / `.getObject(...)` split exactly across two lines); none of them were run with a fuzz/adversarial mindset against splits the author hadn't already seen, so a site like `transferForInvestigationController.js` (guard migrated, read not) could look "already handled" on a superficial read of the surrounding diff without the actual call being re-verified token-by-token; (b) more importantly, at least some of these 4 sites live in files that **no commit on this branch had ever touched** — verification in every prior session was anchored to "re-grep the files this branch's diff changed" rather than an unconditional full-tree sweep of `legacy/HIS-WAR`, so a file that was never part of any commit's diff had no occasion to be re-checked even by an otherwise-correct scanner. This session's Checker script closes both gaps by construction: it walks the entire `legacy/HIS-WAR` tree unconditionally (not a diff subset) and uses `DOTALL` across the full multi-line span, not a fixed number of lines. **Script sanity-checked, not blindly trusted:** read `/tmp/scan_cookies.py` in full before relying on it — the comment/string masking correctly preserves newlines (keeps line numbers accurate) and correctly leaves string-literal contents alone (so a key name appearing inside an unrelated string isn't stripped), and the accessor list (`putObject`, `getObject`, `get`, `put`, `remove`) covers every `$cookies` method this codebase actually calls (`grep`-derived list of all `$cookies.<method>` call forms in the tree; the only other method observed, `$cookies.getAll()`, takes no key argument and only appears in the Angular library source itself and one commented-out example line, so it's correctly out of scope for a per-key scan). Independently spot-checked 8 of the 30 keys (spanning both the `sessionStorage` and `localStorage` groups, including all 4 keys just fixed) with a separately-written `rg --pcre2 -U "(?s)..."` DOTALL regex outside the script's own code path — 0 hits, matching the script's own report. **Re-ran `/tmp/scan_cookies.py` after the 4 fixes, full `legacy/HIS-WAR` tree, all 30 keys × 5 accessor forms:** 1,605 `.js` files scanned, 0 live matches. **`node --check` clean on all 4 changed files. 0 `.java` files touched this session** (confirmed via `git diff --stat`); ran `mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am compile -DskipTests` (no `-o`, unlike the prior session's invocation — network access wasn't restricted this session) from `legacy/` anyway as a compile-check per this session's instruction — fails only on the same pre-existing, unrelated `com.beust.jcommander.internal`/`ContentCachingRequestWrapper.java` gap (BR-002) every prior session on this branch has also hit, confirming this fix didn't introduce or interact with any Java-side breakage. Branch `fix/cookie-bloat-phase-a-frontend`; this addendum's fix is committed. **Still not merged — awaits a third Checker round**, per RULES.md item 6. |
| BR-040 | 2026-07-08 | Not a business rule — a comprehensive, one-time reconciliation of the same category of pre-existing DDL gap as BR-002/BR-038/BR-039, run after three separate missing-table surprises during login testing alone made it clear the gap was systemic rather than isolated. Every JPA `@Entity`/`@Table` class across `legacy/Entity/src/main/java` and `legacy/Global/src/main/java` (1,380 entities) was mechanically diffed against every table in the local dev Postgres DB (`localhost:5434`, `HIS-SME`, all 26 schemas, 1,866 tables before this session). **182 @Entity-declared tables were missing from the DB entirely.** Triaged into 88 HIGH priority (wired to a live Dao/Service/Controller/Repository AND in a schema serving one of the five near-term E2E test flows: hospital setup, user/role management, OPD/IPD consultation, pharmacy, billing) vs 94 LOW priority (either unwired/speculative, or in an out-of-scope module like `abdm`/`procurement`/`assets`/`blood_bank`/`otims`/`inventory`/`lab`/`pharman`, or peripheral/administrative even within an in-scope schema — e.g. NABH accreditation, MRD physical-file tracking, 2FA/LDAP/WhatsApp integration, HR surveys). All 88 HIGH-priority tables were created in the LOCAL DEV DATABASE ONLY (not a tracked Flyway/migration, no application code file changed), each with DDL derived from the entity's real `@Column`/`@JoinColumn`/`@SequenceGenerator` mappings, cross-checked against the DAO/Service raw SQL that actually queries it, and matched to that schema's real sibling-table conventions (same method as BR-038/BR-039). 4 of the 88 turned out to already exist under a correctly-spelled name (`emr.m_system_master_gender_mapper`, `service.t_package_version`, `public.m_nurse_shift_allocation`, `public.m_nurse_shift_allocation_details` — see whitespace-bug note below) — net 84 new tables + 1 supporting reference row inserted into a pre-existing-but-empty table (`opd.m_reason_for_admission`, needed to satisfy a new NOT NULL FK's seed). (Two further whitespace-bug entities, `public.t_visitor_pass_mapper` and `public.m_language_master`, were separately confirmed already-existing during LOW-priority research — they were never part of the 88 HIGH-priority creation set, so aren't counted above.) All 94 LOW-priority tables were left uncreated. All 755 DB tables with no matching `@Entity` in either scanned module (orphans) were listed only, not investigated or acted on, per scope. Full methodology, per-schema breakdown, complete prioritized table lists, and every flag/gap found during creation are in `docs/DB_SCHEMA_RECONCILIATION.md`. **Notable side-finding, not itself a new table gap**: 7 `@Entity` classes across the codebase have a literal leading/trailing space baked into their `@Table(name=...)` string (a genuine pre-existing Java source bug, not touched — would require its own Maker/Checker branch) — `SystemMasterGenderMapper`, `NurseShiftAllocation`, `NurseShiftAllocationDetails`, `VisitorPassMapper`, `PackageVersionMaster`, `LanguageMaster`, `DischargeSummaryTabularTemplateData`. This caused all 7 to be mis-flagged as "missing" by the initial scan; 6 of the 7 already existed under the correctly-trimmed name (false positives, left alone), 1 (`DischargeSummaryTabularTemplateData`) was genuinely missing and has now been created without the space. Flagged for the owner to confirm whether Hibernate's identifier-quoting config makes this a live runtime bug in every environment or a harmless cosmetic one. **Billing-accuracy flag surfaced (Mission Priority #1, not fixed)**: `CorporateStatmentMaster.java`'s `cons_amount`/`lab_amount`/`pharmacy_amount` are typed as Java primitive `int`, structurally unable to hold fractional currency — pre-existing defect, flagged for owner visibility, not touched (would be its own Behavioral Change requiring separate sign-off). | `docs/DB_SCHEMA_RECONCILIATION.md` (new, full detail); local dev DB only — 84 new tables + matching sequences across `adt`(27)/`public`(20)/`billing`(12)/`opd`(7)/`patient`(6)/`service`(3)/`pharmacy`(4)/`emr`(2)/`doctor`(3) schemas, 1 supporting row in pre-existing `opd.m_reason_for_admission`, seed rows in the 4 pre-existing-but-empty false-positive tables (`emr.m_system_master_gender_mapper`, `service.t_package_version`, `public.m_nurse_shift_allocation`, `public.m_nurse_shift_allocation_details`); no tracked DDL file or `.java` file changed | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — not a behavioral change or refactor, same classification as BR-002/BR-038/BR-039) | Approved (owner explicitly requested and authorized this exact reconciliation-and-create pattern for this session, 2026-07-08, extending the BR-038/BR-039 precedent to the full HIGH-priority set) | No new business terms invented (all seed values are structural placeholders or sourced from `ICommonConstants.java`/`IConsultOrderStatusMasterConstants.java`), so no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required by this entry. Two open gaps flagged for a future session, not resolved here: (1) a duplicate `@Table` name collision between two unrelated entity classes (`CoversheetAccessMaster` and `CustomEMRSubCategoryMaster`, both declaring `m_coversheet_access_master` in schema `patient`) — both are LOW priority/unwired so this wasn't blocking, but only one can ever physically exist under that name; (2) two deeper master-table gaps found only via raw-SQL/`@NamedNativeQuery` inspection rather than the `@Entity`-based scan itself (`adt.m_growth_developmental_milestones_master`, `emr.m_custom_history_configuration` — neither exists anywhere locally), meaning the true missing-table surface is larger than what an `@Entity`-only diff can find. This table's absence from tracked DDL (all 84) is a real gap in every environment, not just this sandbox — same standing recommendation as BR-038/BR-039 that a tracked migration eventually be authored for shared/staging/prod; out of scope for this entry. |

Verified post-fix via `\d adt.t_admission` against the local dev Postgres (`localhost:5434`, `HIS-SME`): all 5 columns present with the expected types (`is_admission_cancelled character(1)`, `admission_cancelled_by integer`, `admission_cancelled_date_time timestamp without time zone`, `legacy_visit_id character varying`, `ot_status integer`), and all 25+ pre-existing foreign-key constraints referencing `adt.t_admission(admission_id)` are intact and unaffected (`ADD COLUMN` is additive and doesn't touch existing FKs/data). This table's absence of these 5 columns from tracked DDL is a real gap in every environment, not just this sandbox — same standing recommendation as BR-038/BR-039/BR-040 that a tracked migration eventually be authored for shared/staging/prod; out of scope for this entry. |

Traced the actual write path: `UnknownPatientRegistrationController` (`legacy/ADT/.../ER/controller/UnknownPatientRegistrationController.java`) → `UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.saveUnknownPatient` → `UnknownPatientRegistrationDaoImpl.saveERPatientDetails` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/UnknownPatientRegistrationDaoImpl.java:127-155`), which Dozer-maps the DTO into `AdmissionDetails` (imported at line 31) and calls `session.save(admissionDetails)` at line 145 — confirming `AdmissionDetails` (the first entity) is the sole write-path entity for this flow. `AdmissionDetails` declares `diet_type` (`Integer`) and `remark_for_diet` (`String`), both absent from the live table. The second entity, `AdmissionDetail`, is **not dead code** — repo-wide grep found zero `.save`/`saveOrUpdate`/HQL-update call sites for it anywhere, but it is actively used read-only via `GlobalDao.getPatientWardBedDetails(admissionId)` (`legacy/Global/.../global/dao/global/GlobalDao.java:1967-1980`), consumed by ~25 Pharmacy/Procurement services (drug issue/return/administration, crash-cart, narcotics worklists, consignment-return) to look up a patient's current ward/bed. Both entities' declared columns already exist in the live table except `AdmissionDetails`'s `diet_type`/`remark_for_diet` — so only those 2 needed adding. **(2) `public.t_unit_application_registration_configuration` — 14 missing columns**, surfacing as `column conf.source_id does not exist`: this table/entity (`RegistrationFieldConfiguration.java`, aliased `conf` in `UnitApplicationBillingConfiguration.java`'s `@NamedNativeQuery(name="GET_UNIT_APPLICATION_REGISTRATION_FIELD_CONFIG")`) is queried directly by `unknownPatientRegController.js` (confirmed via grep — calls `getUnitApplicationRegistrationFieldConfig` to fetch which registration fields are mandatory/visible for the ER form), so this is the same flow, not a coincidental concurrent error. Confirmed via full entity-vs-table diff, not just the one reported column: the entity declares 43 columns, the table had only 29 — missing `source_id`, `kinprefix`, `kin_name`, `relation_id`, `phone_no`, `identification_id`, `identificationno_id`, `country_id`, `state_id`, `district_id`, `city_id`, `area`, `post_id`, `kin_mobile`, `kin_address` (all entity-typed `Character`). **(3) `adt.m_identification_master` — 3 missing columns**, surfacing as `column identifica0_.is_numerical does not exist` (the `identifica0_` alias is Hibernate's auto-generated alias for the `IdentificationMaster` entity): confirmed via grep that `unknownPatientRegController.js` calls `GETACTIVEIDENTIFICATION`, wired to `IdentificationMasterDaoImpl`'s `GET_ACTIVE_IDENTIFICATION_LIST` named query (`legacy/ADT/.../adt/master/global/dao/IdentificationMasterDaoImpl.java`), which selects `id.isNumerical`, `id.isAlphanumeric`, `id.definedLength` from `IdentificationMaster` (`legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/global/model/IdentificationMaster.java`, `@Table(name="m_identification_master",schema="adt")`) — again the same page-load, not a separate flow. Table was missing `is_numerical` (`Character`), `is_alphanumeric` (`Character`), `defined_length` (`Integer`); all other entity columns already existed. All 19 columns across the 3 tables added via `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` on the local dev DB only, matching each entity's declared Java type (`character(1)` for `Character`, `integer` for `Integer`, `character varying` for `String`, unbounded to match the sibling `remark`/`nursing_rejection_note` convention on `t_admission_details`); the 14 new `t_unit_application_registration_configuration` columns were given `DEFAULT 'N'` matching that table's dominant existing-column default convention (most of its 15 pre-existing `character(1)` columns already default `'N'`, only a handful of core-identity fields default `'Y'`); the 3 `m_identification_master`/2 `t_admission_details` new columns were left with no default (nullable), matching their respective tables' analogous existing nullable columns (`is_expiry_required`, `remark`) which also carry no default. **Standing duplicate-mapping risk flagged, not resolved — same pattern as BR-040's `CoversheetAccessMaster`/`CustomEMRSubCategoryMaster` flag**: no `.java` file was touched, per explicit instruction. `AdmissionDetails` and `AdmissionDetail` will independently drift over time since nothing keeps their column sets in sync; separately, `AdmissionDetail` (the read-only mirror) declares `reserved_bed_category_id` and `beds_billing_bed_category_id`, which already exist in the live table but are **not modeled at all** in `AdmissionDetails` (the write-path entity) — meaning the ER admission-write path can never populate or update those two columns even though the table has them and a sibling read path expects them to be meaningful. Single authoritative entity, no duplicate-mapping this time: `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/global/model/PatientSponsorDetails.java` (`@Table(name="t_patient_sponsor_details",schema="opd")`). Traced write path: `UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.saveUnknownPatient` (line 164) → `PatientSponsorDetailsServiceImpl.savePatientSponsorDetails` → `PatientSponsorDetailsDaoImpl.savePatientSponsorDetails` (line 55-84, `extends GenericDao<PatientSponsorDetails,Integer>`) → Hibernate `save()`/`update()`. Full column diff (not just the one erroring column): entity declares 31 scalar `@Column` fields, live table had only 26. Missing: `claim_number`, `employee_name`, `relation`, `medical_card_no` (all `String` → `character varying`, matching the sibling `insurance_number`/`corporate_emp_note` columns' existing unbounded-varchar convention on this same table) and `applicable_date` (`Date` → `timestamp without time zone`, matching sibling `valid_from`/`valid_to`). Noted, not a gap: a separate lookalike entity `PatientSponsorForCostEstimation.java` maps a *different* table (`t_patient_sponsor_details_cost_estimation`) — confirmed not to be confused with this one. **(2) `adt.t_medico_legal` — 2 missing columns**, surfacing immediately after fix (1), as `column "mlc_no" of relation "t_medico_legal" does not exist`. Single entity, no duplicate-mapping: `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/model/MedicoLeagal.java` (`@Table(name="t_medico_legal",schema="adt")`) — **class name has a pre-existing typo, `MedicoLeagal` not `MedicoLegal`**, flagged for visibility since it will keep tripping up future greps for "MedicoLegal", not fixed (no `.java` touched). Traced write path: `UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.saveUnknownPatient` (line 191) → `UnknownPatientRegistrationDaoImpl.saveMedicoLegalDetails` (line 1012) → Dozer-maps to `MedicoLeagal`, calls `session.save()`/`.update()` (line 1021/1024). Full column diff: entity declares 19 scalar `@Column` fields, live table had only 17. WHERE patient_id=81`, 0 rows), the method's own two catch blocks (`HibernateException`/`Exception`, both calling `logger.error(...)`) **never fire any log output** (confirmed via a clean, from-now `docker logs -f his-local-tomcat` capture spanning the entire request, showing zero `ERROR`/`Exception` lines of any kind for this request, verified reproducible across 4 separate attempts with distinct mobile numbers), and no SQL statement for `patient.t_patient_details` is ever sent to Postgres at all (confirmed via live Postgres statement-logging capture — execution stops silently somewhere between the two DAO calls, most likely inside the Dozer custom mapping `PatientRegistrationDto_to_PatientDetails` — defined with no field-level overrides, plain by-name matching, in `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/resources/OPD/dozer-bean-mappings.xml:23-29` — since that is the only remaining step between the two DAO calls, though the exact failing statement/line could not be pinned further without adding debug instrumentation to the running code, which is out of scope for this session). This also means `PatientRegistrationServiceImpl.savePatientRegistration`'s own outer catch-all (`:645-648`, which does log "savePatientRegistration failed") is **not** where this fails either — the failure is a normal-flow, non-exceptional return through `patientResponse.getStatus().equals(SUCCESS)` being false (`:535`, else-branch return at `:638`), meaning `savePatientDetails` (DAO) is itself returning its own `Response(ERROR, COMMON_ERROR_CODE, COMMON_ERROR_MESSAGE, null, null)` fallback (`PatientRegistrationDaoImpl.java:179`) without its catch blocks' logging ever executing — an internal contradiction (a caught-and-logged path producing an unlogged effect) that itself may be a clue but was not further resolved this session. **Compounding data-integrity defect, not previously documented:** because the two DAO calls are not atomic (Postgres statement log shows the `t_patient_registration` insert `COMMIT`s independently, before `savePatientDetails` is even attempted, rather than both being flushed together at one shared transaction boundary), every failed attempt permanently leaves an orphan row in `patient.t_patient_registration` with no matching `patient.t_patient_details` row — 4 such orphans were created by this session's reproduction attempts alone (`patient_id` 79-81, mobile numbers `9876543212`-`9876543215`; Re-ran the real save via a temporary throwaway Playwright script (`his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/br047-diag-opd-patient-registration.spec.js`, deleted after this session — no permanent E2E test exists yet for this flow) driving the actual `/#/patientRegistration` UI end-to-end (fill Normal Registration tab → Save → confirm modal → Save), while capturing `docker logs -f` from request start. Captured the exact exception, with full stack trace, at `PatientRegistrationDaoImpl.java:171` (the `mapper.map(...)` call): `org.dozer.MappingException: Class mapping not found by map-id: SRC-CLASS->com.param.global.dto.PatientRegistrationDto DST-CLASS->com.param.global.model.PatientDetails MAP-ID->PatientRegistrationDto_to_PatientDetails`. This IS caught by the method's own `catch (Exception e)` block (Dozer's `MappingException extends RuntimeException`) and its `logger.error("savePatientDetails failed", e)` call DOES fire — confirmed by a genuine log4j-formatted line in the capture, `2026/07/09 19:06:23.743 ERROR: com.param.global.dao.PatientRegistrationDaoImpl - savePatientDetails failed` — which resolves BR-047's original "zero log output" puzzle as most likely a gap in that session's specific capture window/method rather than a real silent-swallow; the catch/log/return control flow was always working exactly as written. **The actual defect: the `his-local-tomcat` container's deployed `WEB-INF/classes/OPD/dozer-bean-mappings.xml` is a stale build artifact, byte-diffed against the live source (`legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/resources/OPD/dozer-bean-mappings.xml`, confirmed unchanged and correct since this repo's very first commit `fcd7bac1` — `git log`/`git diff HEAD` both confirm no uncommitted or historical edit ever removed this mapping from source).** The deployed file contains only a single `<mapping>` block (`PatientRegistrationDto_to_PatientRegistration`) and is missing the other two blocks that exist in source immediately below it in the same file — `PatientRegistrationDto_to_PatientDetails` (the one this bug needs) and `PatientRegistrationDto_to_PatientRegistrationLog` (used by `savePatientRegistrationLog`, likely broken the same way but not separately reproduced this session) — plus roughly 130 more lines/mappings entirely absent from the deployed copy. `spring-dispatcher-servlet.xml` confirms `OPD/dozer-bean-mappings.xml` genuinely is in the configured `mappingFiles` list (so the file itself isn't missing from the load path — it's just an old version of it that's deployed). Net conclusion: **this is a local-dev-environment/deployment gap (the container image predates this mapping's addition to source and was never rebuilt from current source), not a source-code defect** — same class of finding as the DB schema/data gaps in BR-038/039/042/043/046, just manifesting via a stale compiled artifact instead of a stale DB. No `.java`/`.xml` file was modified as a fix (out of scope, diagnosis only per this session's explicit instruction); all temporary diagnostic logging was fully removed afterward (`git diff master` on the DAO file confirmed byte-identical to `master`) and the container's jar was restored to the original (pre-session) `PatientRegistrationDaoImpl.class` bytecode, restarted, and reconfirmed healthy. Recommend to the owner: this most likely just needs a full clean rebuild-and-redeploy of the local dev `his-local-tomcat` image/WAR from current source (an ops/environment action, not a Maker code fix) — re-verify the save end-to-end afterward before resuming the BR-046/047 E2E test work. One new orphan row from this session's own reproduction (`patient_id=84`, mobile `9876500011`) was cleaned up (deleted) immediately after capture, unlike the earlier session's `patient_id` 79-81 orphans which are still flagged above as not cleaned up (out of this session's scope to touch prior sessions' data). **Final verification (2026-07-09, verification session, branch `investigate/br-047-silent-save-failure`): CONFIRMED FIXED END-TO-END.** After the owner's redeploy+restart, drove one genuine, complete OPD Normal Patient Registration save through the real Angular UI (`/#/patientRegistration`, login `admin`/`12345`, unit `DEMO`) via a temporary Playwright spec (written in the sibling `his-e2e-tests` project, run, then deleted — same disposable-diagnostic-script convention as the earlier instrumentation session), filling Personal + Contact tabs (test-identifying values: last name `BR047VerifyTest`, mobile `9876500199`) through to a real `POST /api/global/patientRegistration`. Result: HTTP 200, `{"status":"success",...}`, growl `SUCCESS!! Added Successfully.`. Independently confirmed via direct `psql` query against `his-local-postgres` (not just the UI/API response) that **both** rows were written this time: `patient.t_patient_registration` (`patient_id=85`, `uhid_number='PC-18-0000085'`) **and** `patient.t_patient_details` (`patient_details_id=43`, `patient_id=85`, fully populated FKs — `nationality_id`/`race_id`/`marital_status_id`/`country_id`/`state_id`/`district_id`/`city_id`/`area_id` all `=1`, matching the BR-046 seed data) — this is exactly the row BR-047 documented as silently never written before the fix. A repeat save attempt with the same mobile number correctly hit the app's own duplicate-patient check (HTTP 200, `{"status":"error","code":"409",...}`, no new row written) rather than creating a second orphan, confirming no regression in that path either. Also checked `docker logs his-local-tomcat` across the verification window: zero occurrences of `MappingException` or `savePatientDetails failed` (the exact error signature the instrumentation session captured pre-fix). This session's one test row (`patient_id=85`, both tables) was deleted immediately after verification, after first scanning every table with a `patient_id` foreign key to `patient.t_patient_registration` (48 tables checked) and confirming only `patient.t_patient_details` held a matching row — full cleanup, no orphans left by this session. The pre-existing `patient_id` 79-81 orphans from the original bug-discovery session remain untouched (out of this session's scope, flagged above and in prior entries). **BR-047 is closed.** |
| BR-045 | 2026-07-09 | Fixed the `ReferenceError: type is not defined` flagged in BR-044, on branch `fix/unknown-patient-reg-typeerror`. Root cause: `$scope.getCallingCode` (`unknownPatientRegController.js:3404`) was defined as `function(data)` — missing a `type` parameter — while its body at line 3411 (`if(type=="PA")`) references `type` unconditionally. The sibling controller `patientRegistrationController.js:1518` has the correct, working two-argument version (`function(data,type)`), confirming this is a leftover incomplete refactor, not intentional. The file's only call site, inside `$scope.getStateByCountry(id,type)` (line 3486), called `$scope.getCallingCode(dataorg)` — also missing the `type` argument that was already in scope there. Fix: added `type` back to the function signature (line 3404: `function(data,type)`) and to the call site (line 3497: `$scope.getCallingCode(dataorg,type)`), restoring parity with the sibling controller — a 2-line diff, no other lines touched. This is more than console-error suppression: today the ReferenceError aborts `getCallingCode`'s success callback *before* it reaches the `permanentPhoneCode`/`phoneCode` assignment beneath the broken `if`, so those fields are currently never populated when this path fires (only `countryCallingCode`, assigned one line earlier, survives); after the fix they are correctly set based on address `type` ('PA' vs. other), matching the sibling controller's intended behavior. Verified: `node --check` passed on the fixed file; fix was `docker cp`'d into the running `his-local-tomcat` container's deployed webapp path and confirmed present via `docker exec grep`; the real E2E test (`~/Projects/his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/er-unknown-patient-registration.spec.js --project=chromium`, which explicitly asserts zero unexpected browser console errors) was re-run against the live container and **passed (2/2)**, confirming the ReferenceError no longer fires and the save still succeeds end-to-end. **Separately investigated per this session's task, NOT fixed on this branch (needs owner decision, not a quick change):** the medico-legal unconditional-save issue also flagged in BR-044. Confirmed exact location: `UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java:186` — `//if(unAdmissionDto.getIsMedicoLegal()=='Y' && res3.getStatus().equals(SUCCESS))` is commented out and replaced by the unconditional `if(res3.getStatus().equals(SUCCESS))` on the next line, so `saveMedicoLegalDetails()` (line 191) runs on every successful admission save regardless of the "Medico Legal Case" checkbox. Left untouched because whether medico-legal records should really never be gated, or whether commenting out the gate was an accidental regression, is a business/data-quality judgment call for the owner, not a mechanical bug fix — reported here for triage, per this session's explicit scope instruction. | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/adt/transaction/emergancy/unknownPatientRegController.js:3404,3497` (fixed, this branch); `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/service/UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java:186` (medico-legal gate, reported only, not fixed) | Behavioral Change | Approved (owner-approved 2026-07-09, after independent Checker review and two consecutive passing E2E runs verified by the owner directly) | A JS error that previously fired on every ER Unknown Patient save no longer fires, and two form fields (`permanentPhoneCode`/`phoneCode`) that were previously silently never set now get set correctly based on address type — both are real, however small, differences in observable output per `CLAUDE.md`'s definition, hence Behavioral Change rather than "Refactoring (Safe)" despite being a 2-line diff restoring an evidently-intended parameter. Provisional ID per `RULES.md` rule 8 (current highest real ID is BR-044); to be renumbered to the next sequential `BR-XXX` in the commit immediately preceding merge to master — not before. Awaiting Checker review; not merged. |

Also drives its "OPD Visit" button (`gotoEncounterFromRegistration()` → `$state.go("encounter.encounterDetails", ...)`) far enough to confirm the client-side-only handoff navigates correctly, deliberately stopping short of `EncounterController.js`'s `saveAppointmentMaster()` (`POST /api/opd/encounter`) itself — a substantially separate flow with its own doctor/visit-type/slot master data and validations, out of scope for this test. Ran 3 times consecutively against `his-local-tomcat`/local dev Postgres; all 3 passed, and each was independently confirmed via direct `psql` (not just trusting the test's own assertions) to have written fully-populated, matching rows to **both** `patient.t_patient_registration` and `patient.t_patient_details` (`patient_id` 89/90/91, `patient_details_id` 47/48/49) — the exact write path BR-047 documented as silently failing pre-fix. **New finding, not previously flagged in BR-044 (which only covered the ER Unknown Patient flow's console noise):** this OPD flow throws three of its own genuine, pre-existing, non-blocking JS console errors on every real run of `patientRegistrationController.js` — (1) `focusdata3` (DOB field's `ng-change`, line ~368): reads `$scope.ageDay.length` before `ageDay` is ever initialized (the separate "Day(s)" input hasn't been touched), throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')`; (2) `focusdata11` (Mobile No. field's `ng-change`, line ~406): `document.getElementById('code_focus2')` returns `null` (no such element exists in this form), so `.focus()` throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'focus')`; (3) `disableEnableExpiry` (Identification Type's `ng-change`): its DOM manipulation for the conditional expiry-date field collides with the in-flight Angular digest, throwing Angular's own `$rootScope:inprog` ("digest already in progress") error. All three are caught by Angular's exception handler, fire on every single run (not test-flakiness — reproduced identically all 3 times), and confirmed non-blocking: the save, growl, DB writes, and encounter-navigation handoff all succeed regardless. None fixed here, per this session's explicit instruction not to touch `.java`/`.js` — filtered in the committed test (with the reasoning documented inline) the same way `er-unknown-patient-registration.spec.js` filters its own known noise, and reported here for owner triage as candidates for a future dedicated fix session (same pattern as BR-045's fix of the analogous ER-flow `ReferenceError`). | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/opd/transection/patientRegistrationController.js` (read-only, not modified — focusdata3/focusdata11/disableEnableExpiry all reported not fixed); `~/Projects/his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/opd-patient-registration.spec.js` (new file, committed) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect for the 3 console errors — not a behavioral change or refactor; test-authoring otherwise) | Pending (the 3 console-error findings need owner triage/priority — same status as BR-044's analogous ER-flow finding before BR-045 fixed it) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. No orphan rows created this session — all 3 test runs succeeded and their rows are genuine, intentional test data (not cleaned up, consistent with how `er-unknown-patient-registration.spec.js`'s own passing runs also leave their test patients in place). |
| BR-049 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — first of a chain of gaps found while building a new E2E write-test for **IPD Patient Admission** (`/#/admission`, `admissionController.js`, `POST /adt/savePatientAdmission`), following the BR-042→048 investigation pattern, this time for a flow with no prior E2E coverage at all. Traced the real write path via `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission` → `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission` → `BedAllocationDaoImpl` (entities: `Admission`→`adt.t_admission`, `AdmissionDetails`→`adt.t_admission_details`, plus `AdmissionPatientMapper`/`Transfer`/`PatientSponsorDetails`/`KinDetails` sub-writes) — confirmed this flow requires only a bare registered `patientId` (`encounterId` is present on `AdmissionDto` but never read by this save path; no prior OPD/ER encounter needed) but does hard-fail on 3 separate master-data/config gaps, all found via real UI interaction (not just code reading) since the form's dropdowns rendered empty: **(1) `adt.m_reason_master` — 0 rows.** `admission.html`'s "Reason for Admission" `<select>` filters `ng-if="info.reasonTypeId == 12"` (`GETACTIVEREASONLIST`); `adt.m_reason_type_master` confirms `reason_type_id=12` = `"Admission"`. Seeded 1 active row (`reason_master_id=1`, `reason_code='GEN-ADM'`, `reason_desc='General Admission'`, `reason_type_id=12`, `organization_id=1`). **(2) `public.t_unit_speciality_mapper` — existing rows had the wrong flag value, not a missing-row gap.** The "Admitting Doctor Specialty" dropdown filters `ng-if="info.isClinicalSpeciality=='Y'"`, sourced from `SpecialityMaster`'s `GET_ACTIVE_SPECIALITY_LIST` named native query, which joins `doctor.m_speciality_master` to `public.t_unit_speciality_mapper` (aliased `usm`) and reads `isClinicalSpeciality` from `usm.is_clinical_speciality`, **not** from `specialityMaster.is_clinical_speciality` — all 3 existing rows for unit 1 (speciality 1/2/3: General Medicine/Cardiology/Orthopaedics, matching `doctor.t_doctor_speciality_mapper`'s existing doctor-to-speciality rows for doctor_id 2001-2003) had `is_clinical_speciality='N'`, silently emptying the dropdown. Updated all 3 to `'Y'` (all are genuinely clinical specialties). **(3) `public.t_unit_application_billing_configuration` — existing row had a null required field, not a missing-row gap.** `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission` (`:242-243`) hard-fails with `"Default tariff is not defined !!"` if `unitApplicationConfigurationDto.getDefaultSelfTariffId()` is null; the unit 1/org 1 row had `default_self_tariff_id` null (`self_payee_id` was already `1`, correctly set). Set it to `tariff_id=1` (`service.m_tariff_master`'s only row, `"STANDARD"`/"Standard Tariff"). **Caching gotcha discovered while re-testing fix (2), not a DB/code gap — flagged for future sessions' visibility:** `SpecialityServiceImpl` (`legacy/Global/.../service/SpecialityServiceImpl.java:54-59`) keeps an in-process Guava `Cache<Integer, List<SpecialityMasterDto>>` (`specialityMasterCache`, keyed by `unitId`, no TTL, only invalidated by the app's own save/update code paths) populated from the first `getActiveSpecialityList` call after startup; a raw DB `UPDATE` bypasses the app entirely and is never seen until the cache is invalidated or the app restarts. Confirmed via the endpoint's own `message` field, which literally says `"List Size Cache: 3"` vs `"List Size DB: 3"` depending on which path served the response. Restarted `his-local-tomcat` (an ops action, not a code/DB change — same class as BR-047's "stale artifact needs a rebuild/redeploy, not a code fix") to pick up the corrected rows; re-confirmed via the live endpoint response afterward (`isClinicalSpeciality:"Y"` for all 3, message back to `"List Size DB: 3"` immediately post-restart, `"List Size Cache: 3"` on the next call once repopulated) that this was purely a stale-in-memory-cache issue, not a persistence problem. | `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/controller/BedAllocationController.java`, `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/service/BedAllocationServiceImpl.java:230-260`, `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/dao/BedAllocationDaoImpl.java`, `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/global/model/SpecialityMaster.java:34-52` (`GET_ACTIVE_SPECIALITY_LIST`), `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/service/SpecialityServiceImpl.java:54-59,193-201` (all read-only, none modified); local dev DB only (`adt.m_reason_master` +1 row, `public.t_unit_speciality_mapper` 3 rows' `is_clinical_speciality` flipped `N`→`Y`, `public.t_unit_application_billing_configuration` `default_self_tariff_id` set to `1`), no tracked DDL/data file or `.java`/`.js` file changed; `his-local-tomcat` container restarted (ops action, to clear the in-memory Guava cache described above) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — master-data/config gaps and a stale in-memory cache, not a behavioral change or refactor, same classification as BR-002/BR-038 through BR-046) | Approved (owner directed this exact reconciliation-and-create pattern for this session, extending the BR-038→046 precedent) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. Verified via `\d`/`SELECT *` against the local dev Postgres (`localhost:5434`, `HIS-SME`) for all 3 fixes, and via the live `/adt/getActiveSpecialityList`/form-dropdown response after the `his-local-tomcat` restart (all 3 specialties render with `isClinicalSpeciality:"Y"`). This is a real gap in every environment, not just this sandbox — same standing recommendation as prior BR-0xx entries that seed/reference data for these tables be reviewed for staging/prod, and that the `SpecialityServiceImpl` cache either gain a TTL or an explicit admin-triggered invalidation endpoint so a future data fix doesn't silently appear to fail. |
| BR-050 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — continuation of BR-049's IPD Admission investigation: after the doctor/reason dropdowns were fixed, `getBedListByMultipleCriteria` (bed search inside the "Allocate Bed" modal) consistently returned an empty list (`"listObject":[]`) for every ward/gender combination tried. Traced the actual HQL (`BedAllocationDaoImpl.searchBed`, `legacy/ADT/.../admission/dao/BedAllocationDaoImpl.java:455-490`): it **INNER JOIN**s `BedMaster` to `listBedLogStatus` (`adt.t_bed_log_status`), `floorMaster` (`adt.m_floor_master`), `wardMaster` (`adt.m_ward_master`), `bedCategoryMaster` (`adt.m_bed_category_master`), `billingBedCategoryMaster` (`adt.m_billing_bed_category_master`), and `roomMaster` (`adt.m_room_master`), plus a `bed.wardId IN (select wardId from WardGenderMapper where genderId=:genderId and status='A')` subquery against `adt.t_ward_gender_mapper` — an INNER JOIN silently excludes every row if the joined table is empty or the FK is null, which is exactly what all 20 pre-existing demo beds (`adt.m_bed_master`, `BED-001`..`BED-020`, 4 per ward × 5 wards) hit, simultaneously, on 4 separate joins: **(1) `adt.m_floor_master` — 0 rows** (every bed's `floor_id` was null). Seeded 1 row (`floor_id=1`, `floor_code='F1'`, unit 1/org 1). **(2) `adt.m_room_master` — 0 rows** (every bed's `room_id` was null). Seeded 1 room per ward (5 rows, e.g. `GW-01-R1` for ward 1), then updated all 20 beds' `floor_id`/`room_id` to point at the new floor and their own ward's new room. **(3) `adt.t_bed_log_status` — 0 rows** (the query's real bed-availability signal — `bed.bedStatusId=3 AND bedlog.bedStatusId=3`, "Vacant and Ready to Receive Patient" per `adt.m_bed_status_master` — comes from this transaction-log table, not directly from `m_bed_master`). Set all 20 beds' own `bed_status_id=3` and inserted one matching `t_bed_log_status` row per bed (`status='A'`, `bed_status_id=3`). **(4) `adt.t_ward_gender_mapper` — 0 rows**, despite a same-named-but-different `adt.m_ward_gender_mapper` never existing (confirmed the real table is the transaction-style `t_`-prefixed one) — every ward had no gender eligibility row at all, so the `wardId IN (...)` subquery matched nothing for any gender. Seeded 1 row per ward per gender (10 rows: 5 wards × genders 1/2, both sexes, `status='A'`) rather than just the one gender this test happens to need, since an all-wards-excluded-for-every-gender state is clearly an incomplete seed, not an intentional gender restriction. **(5) `adt.m_billing_bed_category_master` — 0 rows**, found on the *next* re-test after (1)-(4) were fixed (still `listObject:[]` even with the floor/room/log/gender gaps closed) — every bed's `billing_bed_category_id` was also null, and the referenced table itself was empty (unlike `adt.m_bed_category_master`, which already had 4 real rows matching the 4 bed categories used across the 20 demo beds). Seeded 1 row (`billing_bed_category_id=1`, `billing_bed_code='GEN'`, `billing_bed_desc='General'`, unit 1/org 1) and pointed all 20 beds' `billing_bed_category_id` at it. After all 5 fixes, `getBedListByMultipleCriteria` returned all 20 beds correctly grouped by ward/room/category, confirmed via the real "Allocate Bed" modal UI (not just the raw endpoint response) and via selecting/allotting a bed through it end-to-end. | `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/dao/BedAllocationDaoImpl.java:413-505` (`getBedListByMultipleCriteria`/`searchBed`, read-only, not modified); local dev DB only (`adt.m_floor_master` +1 row, `adt.m_room_master` +5 rows, `adt.m_bed_master` 20 rows' `floor_id`/`room_id`/`bed_status_id`/`billing_bed_category_id` populated, `adt.t_bed_log_status` +20 rows, `adt.t_ward_gender_mapper` +10 rows, `adt.m_billing_bed_category_master` +1 row), no tracked DDL/data file or `.java`/`.js` file changed | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — a 5-table master-data/linkage gap class, not a behavioral change or refactor, same classification as BR-049) | Approved (owner directed this exact reconciliation-and-create pattern for this session, extending the BR-038→049 precedent) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. Verified via `\d`/`SELECT *`/`SELECT count(*)` against the local dev Postgres (`localhost:5434`, `HIS-SME`) for all 5 fixes, and via the real "Allocate Bed" modal in the live UI returning and allotting real beds afterward. This is a real gap in every environment, not just this sandbox (a hospital cannot allocate any bed at all without these 5 tables correctly populated) — same standing recommendation as prior BR-0xx entries that seed data for these tables be reviewed for staging/prod. |
| BR-051 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — final consolidated entry closing out the IPD Patient Admission column-level DDL gap chain (BR-049/050 were master-data/linkage gaps on otherwise-complete tables; these 3 are missing columns on existing tables, the same gap class as BR-038/039/042/043). Task this session was explicit: loop the actual E2E test (`~/Projects/his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/ipd-admission.spec.js`, written this session) against `his-local-tomcat`/local dev Postgres, fixing one root cause per real error hit, until the save genuinely succeeded — not stopping at the first fix. All 3 were found via Postgres statement-logging capture (`log_statement=all`) during real, authenticated Playwright runs of the actual admission save (same technique as BR-047's verification), since the app's own logging did not surface them clearly (see BR-052 for why). **(1) `billing.t_package_consumption_master` — missing `package_applicable_date` column**, surfacing as `column pcm.package_applicable_date does not exist`. Traced: `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission` (`:577,751`) unconditionally calls `iMPackageMasterService.checkServiceAvailableInPackageContract(consumptionDto)` per admission-charges service, whose native query (`MPackageMaster.java:159-162`, aliased `pcm`) selects `pcm.package_applicable_date` among other columns from this table (`legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/billing/packages/model/TPackageConsumptionMaster.java`, `@Table(name="t_package_consumption_master",schema="billing")`) — a table already referenced 25+ times as an FK target from `adt.t_admission`, per BR-042's fan-out list. The entity itself does not declare this field (only referenced in native SQL in 3 other files' `@NamedNativeQuery`s), and the live table lacked it entirely. Added `timestamp without time zone` (matching the sibling `validity_from`/`validity_to` columns' type on this same table). **(2) `service.t_unit_service_master_details` — missing `is_bed_charges_service` column**, surfacing next as `column "is_bed_charges_service" does not exist`. Traced: `EncounterMasterDaoImpl.getIsBedChargesService` (`legacy/Global/.../opd/dao/EncounterMasterDaoImpl.java:5848-5862`) runs a native `SELECT is_bed_charges_service FROM service.t_unit_service_master_details WHERE unit_id=:unitId AND organization_id=:organizationId AND service_master_id=:serviceId AND status='A'` — a genuine per-unit-service override lookup (not a copy-paste referencing the wrong table: a same-named column also exists, correctly, as a real `@Column` on the unrelated `AutoPostingServicesCofiguration` entity/`t_auto_posting_services_configuration` table — confirmed these are two distinct, both-legitimate uses of the same column name on different tables, not a duplicate-mapping bug). `t_unit_service_master_details` (2 mapping entities exist for this table, `UnitServiceMasterDetails.java`/`UnitServiceMasterDetailsOPD.java` — neither declares this field either) has ~15 sibling `character(1)` flag columns (`is_bed_side`, `is_auto_render`, etc., all defaulting `'N'`) but was missing this one. Added `character(1) DEFAULT 'N'`, matching that convention. **(3) `adt.m_bed_category_master` — missing `is_icu` column**, surfacing last as `column bedcat.is_icu does not exist`, triggered by an HL7-notification-style native query (`BedMaster.java`, joins `adt.t_admission`/`adt.t_admission_details`/this table, `WHERE bedcat.is_icu = true or (otreq.ot_transfer_status = 8 ...)` — referenced from `Hl7Listner.java`, fired as a side-effect after a successful admission save, not the admission save itself). `BedCategoryMaster.java:207-208` declares `@Column(name="is_icu") private boolean isIcu;` — a real, already-mapped entity field — on a table with 15+ live FK references (per BR-042-style fan-out check), simply missing this one column. Added `boolean DEFAULT false` (matching the Java `boolean` type exactly, Hibernate's standard `boolean`→`bool` mapping). **After all 3 fixes** (on top of BR-049/050's master-data/linkage fixes), the real E2E save (`ipd-admission.spec.js`) succeeded end-to-end: `POST /adt/savePatientAdmission` → HTTP 200, `{"status":"success",...}`, real `admissionId`/`admissionNumber` (e.g. `admissionId:22`, `admissionNumber:"ADM-18-0000022"`), growl `Success! / Patient Admitted Successfully..!!`, zero unexpected failed requests, zero console errors beyond BR-048's already-documented OPD-registration noise (this test's own setup step reuses that same registration flow). | `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/billing/packages/model/TPackageConsumptionMaster.java`, `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/service/global/model/MPackageMaster.java` (native query, read-only), `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/opd/dao/EncounterMasterDaoImpl.java:5848-5862`, `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/billing/global/transaction/config/model/AutoPostingServicesCofiguration.java` (same-named column on unrelated table, read-only, confirmed not a mix-up), `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/global/model/BedCategoryMaster.java:207-208` (all read-only for diffing, none modified); local dev DB only (`billing.t_package_consumption_master` +1 column, `service.t_unit_service_master_details` +1 column, `adt.m_bed_category_master` +1 column), no tracked DDL file or `.java` file changed; `~/Projects/his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/ipd-admission.spec.js` (new file, committed) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — not a behavioral change or refactor, same classification as BR-038/039/042/043) | Approved (owner directed this exact loop-to-completion pattern for this session, extending the BR-038→050 precedent) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. | BR-052 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — reported per explicit task instruction to stop and report a genuine application CODE bug rather than work around it, found while closing out BR-051's save-flow investigation. `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission` (`legacy/ADT/.../admission/controller/BedAllocationController.java:166-167`, note the route itself has a pre-existing trailing-space typo in its `@RequestMapping(value = "/savePatientAdmission ", ...)` that harmlessly works because Spring trims it) wraps its entire body in `catch (Exception e) { e.printStackTrace(); }`, and the earlier-stage failures this session hit (BR-051's 3 missing columns, encountered one at a time while iterating) were each caught inside `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission`'s own `@Transactional` method and surfaced to the client as a genuine `HTTP 500`/`{"status":"error","code":"500","message":"Internal Server Error"}` (traced to `GlobalExceptionHandler.handleAllExceptions`'s generic `else` branch, `legacy/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java` — which logs via `logger.info("exception log consider", ex.getStackTrace())`, **not** `logger.error`, and passes a `StackTraceElement[]` as a plain format arg rather than the throwable itself, so neither `docker logs his-local-tomcat` nor the Tomcat-level `catalina.<date>.log` ever showed the real exception text at any log level this session's logging configuration surfaces — root cause was only found by enabling Postgres statement-logging (`log_statement=all`) and reading the raw SQL error directly from `his-local-postgres`'s own log, same technique as BR-047). **The genuine defect, not previously documented:** despite every one of these attempts returning an overall error response to the client, a real, fully-committed row was written to `adt.t_admission` anyway before the later step failed and rolled the rest back — i.e. the same non-atomic-multi-step-write class of bug BR-047 documented for `PatientRegistrationServiceImpl` (`t_patient_registration` committing independently of `t_patient_details`), now confirmed in a second, unrelated save flow. Reproduced twice, independently: `patient_id=93` → orphan `admission_id=18` (`adt.t_admission`, `status='A'`, no matching `adt.t_admission_details` row — confirmed via direct `SELECT`), and `patient_id=92` → orphan `admission_id=19`, both from attempts that hit BR-051's column-gap errors and returned HTTP 500 to the client. This means a hospital could see "the admission failed" on-screen while `adt.t_admission` — the table 25+ other modules join against — silently already has a live row for that patient, which per BR-042's fan-out list of dependents (billing package consumption, discharge summary, procedure requests, OT/theatre inventory, sponsor/kin/transfer records, lab sample master, medico-legal, etc.) is a materially riskier blast radius than BR-047's orphan (`patient.t_patient_registration` has far fewer live FK dependents). **Not fixed, no `.java` file touched, per explicit session scope** — reported here for owner review/Maker triage (same as BR-047's original disposition, before its own dedicated verification session pinned the exact line). The 2 orphan rows (`admission_id` 18, 19) are left in place, not cleaned up, for the same reason BR-047 gave: deleting rows a future investigation might still need to reference was judged riskier than leaving harmless test data. | `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/controller/BedAllocationController.java:166-167` (route + outer catch, read-only), `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/service/BedAllocationServiceImpl.java:230-` (`@Transactional savePatientAdmission`, read-only), `legacy/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java` (generic-exception branch, read-only — none modified) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — genuine application code bug, non-atomic transaction; not a behavioral change or refactor) | Pending (owner review needed — this is the second occurrence of BR-047's non-atomicity defect class, in an unrelated flow with a much larger blast radius given `adt.t_admission`'s 25+ FK dependents; needs a Maker session to add diagnostic instrumentation the same way BR-047's follow-up session did, to pin whether this is the same root cause — e.g. a shared Dozer-mapping/transaction-boundary pattern — or a separate defect that happens to look the same) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. Orphan rows `admission_id` 18 (`patient_id=93`) and 19 (`patient_id=92`) left in local dev DB only, not cleaned up, flagged above. This is the reason a handful of early reproduction attempts during this session's investigation could not be reused as the final committed test's demo patient (each fresh attempt needed a not-yet-admitted patient) — resolved not by fixing this bug but by having the committed test register its own brand-new patient every run (see BR-051's Notes). |

| BR-055 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — 9 missing-column schema gaps (same class as BR-038/039/042/043/051), found one at a time by looping the real Pharmacy Walk-In Sale save (`pharmacy-dispense.spec.js`) against `his-local-tomcat`/local dev Postgres with `log_statement=all`, same technique as BR-047/050/051. Each was a genuine `column ... does not exist` error from a live Hibernate-generated query, not a hypothetical: (1) **`public.m_item`**: missing `cims_code`, `cims_type`, `is_emergency_drug`, `is_lasa`, `is_dose_calculation_applicable`, `dose_calculation_applicable_data`, `is_three_uom_applicable` (`Item.java`) — the last one blocked the item-search query entirely (`item.isThreeUOMApplicable` is a projected column in `ItemsDaoImpl.itemsSearch`'s HQL). (2) **`inventory.t_batch`**: missing `pre_printed_barcode`, `printed_count` (`Batch.java`) — blocked `ItemsServiceImpl.itemsDetail`'s batch-list HQL. (3) **`pharmacy.m_schedule_master`**: missing `color_code` (`ScheduleMaster.java`) — blocked `GlobalDao.getColorCode`, called unconditionally from `itemsDetail` once an item has a generic; before this was found, a separate, coincidental `operator does not exist: integer = bytea` error also surfaced from the same query path when the item's `generic_id` was null (resolved by BR-054 populating a real generic, not a schema fix). (4) **`public.m_product_category`** and **`public.m_tax`**: both missing `general_ledger_id` (`ProductCategory.java`/`Tax.java`) — blocked the product-category/tax lookups inside `itemsDetail`. (5) **`pharmacy.t_sale_detail`**: missing `old_discount_percentage`, `at_cop`, `is_dummy_service`, `grn_id`, `grn_number`, plus a **naming mismatch** — the entity's `@Column(name="itemNetAmount")` (unquoted, folds to `itemnetamount` in Postgres) is a *different* actual column from a separate raw-SQL query in `WalkInSalesDao.getSaleDetailsBySaleId` that references `item_net_amount` (snake_case) directly — added both `itemnetamount` and `item_net_amount` as real columns since the Java source itself is inconsistent between the two query paths and neither is mine to reconcile (`.java` file). (6) **`pharmacy.t_sale`**: missing `is_from_pending_issue`, `is_from_narcotics`, `is_from_patient_indent`, `search_patient`, `screen_id`, `cop`, `received_from`, `is_virtual_store`, `cancelled_reason_id`, `cancelled_remark`, `issue_id`, `cancelled_by_id` (`Sale.java`). (7) **`pharmacy.t_prescription`**: missing `medication_type` (`Prescription.java`) — this one poisoned the whole save transaction (Postgres `current transaction is aborted, commands ignored until end of transaction block`) since it's queried as a side-effect of walk-in-sale's temp-patient-registration step, silently rolling back the entire bill save while the client still received an HTTP 200 with an empty `{}` body and a false "Walk-in sales Successfully saved" growl — see BR-057 for the standalone defect this exposes. (8) **`inventory.t_stock_transaction`**: missing `item_id`, `is_transit`, `transit_no`, `issue_details_id`, `billable_consumption_detail_id`, `non_billable_consumption_detail_id`, `transaction_details_id`, `transaction_id`, `in_qty`, `out_qty` (`StockTransaction.java`) — blocked the stock-decrement step itself (`WalkInSalesService.saveStockTransaction`). All 9 gaps were diffed exhaustively (every `@Column`/`@JoinColumn` name in each entity vs `information_schema.columns`) after the first live error in each table, not fixed one-at-a-time blindly. | BR-056 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — reported per explicit task instruction to stop and report a genuine application CODE bug rather than work around it, found while building the Pharmacy Walk-In Sale E2E test. `walkInSalesController.js:42-43` (and, confirmed by grep, the identical copy-pasted pattern in 40+ other controller files app-wide — `pharmacy/*`, `procurement/workOrder/*`, `asset/*`, `nursing/workstation/*`, `adt/transaction/bedManagement/bedManagementController.js`, `master/globalServiceMasterController.js`, etc.) runs `$scope.cookieObjectStore = JSON.parse(sessionStorage.getItem('selectedTopStore')); $scope.storeName = $scope.cookieObjectStore.storeName;` — the second statement unconditionally dereferences `.storeName` **one line before** the controller's own `if ($scope.cookieObjectStore == undefined) { ... }` guard on the very next line. On a genuinely fresh browser session (`sessionStorage` starts empty every time — confirmed this is not a test artifact: Playwright's `storageState` mechanism intentionally never persists `sessionStorage`, matching a real user's brand-new browser tab), `sessionStorage.getItem(...)` returns `null`, `JSON.parse(null)` evaluates to the literal value `null` (not `undefined`), and `null.storeName` throws a real, uncaught `TypeError` that aborts the entire controller constructor — confirmed live via the browser's own console (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'storeName')`, `walkInSalesController.js:43`), not just traced from code. Because the throw happens synchronously during controller construction, **everything after it in the constructor never runs**, including the dropdown-population calls (`PopulateDropdown()`) and the store-popup call that would otherwise self-heal it — the screen is left in a broken, uninitialized state (empty prefix/gender/discount dropdowns) with no user-visible error at all. Critically, `$rootScope.storePopupAcesss()` (the only code path that pre-populates this `sessionStorage` key before a store-scoped screen is reached) is called only from the doctor/nurse/CEO/financer **dashboard** controllers, never from the generic Admin `#/home` dashboard — meaning **the Admin role can never open any store-scoped screen (Pharmacy, Procurement, Assets, Nursing workstation, ADT bed management, etc.) for the first time in a session without it silently crashing**, unless something else in that same tab happened to populate the key first. Reproduced live via direct Playwright navigation to `/#/walkInSales` immediately after login. **Not fixed, no `.js` file touched, per explicit session scope.** To still exercise the real sale flow underneath this bug for the E2E test, the test seeds `sessionStorage.selectedTopStore` from a safe screen (`#/home`) by invoking the app's own, already-reachable `$rootScope.storePopup()` via Angular's injector before navigating to `/#/walkInSales` — this is a test-side browser-state seed (the same class of technique `login.setup.js` already uses for the `appOpen` flag), not a code fix, and does not change what any real HTTP request does. | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/pharmacy/walkInSalesController.js:38-48` (and the identical pattern in 40+ other controller files, all read-only, none modified) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — genuine application code bug, not a behavioral change or refactor) | Pending (owner review needed — this is a severe, app-wide defect blocking first-use of any store-scoped screen for the Admin role; the minimal fix is a one-line reorder — move the `.storeName` read after the `== undefined` guard, or use `?.` optional chaining — but this touches 40+ files sharing the same copy-pasted boilerplate, so a Maker session should confirm the fix pattern once and apply it consistently rather than file-by-file) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. |
| BR-057 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — reported per explicit task instruction to stop and report a genuine application CODE bug rather than work around it, found while building the Pharmacy Walk-In Sale E2E test, and confirmed severe enough to likely also be blocking the concurrent Billing-focused session working in this same sandbox. `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/constants/constants.js:6` hardcodes `var root = '/HIS-WAR';` (used to build every `CONSTANTS.GLOBAL.*`/`CONSTANTS.PHARMACY.*`/`CONSTANTS.BILLING.*`/etc. API URL app-wide), completely independent of and inconsistent with `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/config/url.config.js`'s `ROOT_URL`, which is currently `""` (empty) in this session's working tree — an **uncommitted, in-progress edit already present when this session started** (not made by this session; `git diff` shows it modified, `git log` shows it unchanged since the repo's base commit) and confirmed to already be **deployed and live** in `his-local-tomcat` (`curl http://localhost:8081/resources/assets/js/config/url.config.js` on the running container returns the same `ROOT_URL=""`, and the container's `webapps/ROOT` directory structure confirms the backend is genuinely served at context path `""`, not `/HIS-WAR`). Directly confirmed via `curl`: `GET /HIS-WAR/api/global/store/getStoreListById/1/1` → **404**; the identical request without the prefix, `GET /api/global/store/getStoreListById/1/1` → **200**. This means **every API call built via `constants.js`'s `CONSTANTS.*` helpers 404s right now** — confirmed live in-browser for Pharmacy (`/HIS-WAR/api/pharmacy/sales/walkins/dropdowns/1/1`, `/HIS-WAR/api/global/items/1/false/.../2` etc., all 404) and, separately, for what look like Billing master-data lookups that fired incidentally during this session's testing (`/HIS-WAR/api/billing/bankMaster`, `/HIS-WAR/api/billing/cardTypeMaster`, both 404) — meaning the concurrent Billing session may be hitting this exact same wall without a clear cause, since it is not something either session's own screen-specific code changed. The previously-passing OPD/IPD/ER E2E tests (`opd-patient-registration.spec.js`, `ipd-admission.spec.js`, `er-*.spec.js`) are unaffected and still pass (verified by re-running `ipd-admission.spec.js` during this session) because those flows happen to build their API URLs via `BASE_URL+ROOT_URL` directly from `url.config.js` (already correct), not via `constants.js`'s separate, stale `root` constant. **Not fixed, no `.js` file touched, per explicit session scope** — the one-line fix is `constants.js:6`: change `'/HIS-WAR'` to `''` to match `url.config.js`'s already-corrected value (or, better, have `constants.js` read `ROOT_URL` from `url.config.js` instead of hardcoding its own copy, to prevent this exact class of drift recurring). Given this blocks the Pharmacy save endpoint itself (`CONSTANTS.PHARMACY.OP.WALK_IN_SALES.SAVE_API`), `tests/e2e/pharmacy-dispense.spec.js` cannot pass against the live app until this ships — confirmed by running the real (unmodified) test, which fails with exactly this 404 chain. | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/constants/constants.js:6` (read-only, not modified — this is the one line that needs the fix), `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/config/url.config.js` (already correct in this session's working tree, pre-existing uncommitted change, not made by this session, read-only) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — genuine application code bug, not a behavioral change or refactor) | Pending (owner review needed — high severity, currently blocking Pharmacy entirely and likely affecting the concurrent Billing session too; recommend flagging to that session directly given the shared root cause) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. |

| BR-058 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — reported per explicit task instruction to stop and report a genuine application CODE bug rather than work around it, found while building the Pharmacy Walk-In Sale E2E test. `walkInSalesController.js`'s `ActualAddItemToBill` (the `typeahead-on-select` handler fired when a user picks an item from the item-search dropdown) sets `item.selectedBatch: !!selectedBatch ? selectedBatch : commonDetailService.getDefaultBatchFromConfig(response.data.batch)` (line ~2188), where the local `selectedBatch` is only ever non-null if the item's batch list happens to contain a placeholder with `batchId==0` (it never does for a real item). When `getDefaultBatchFromConfig` also finds no configured default (the normal case for any item without a pre-configured default batch — confirmed true for every item in this dev DB, since none existed before this session), it returns `null`, and two lines later `item.taxId = item.selectedBatch.taxId;` (line 2243) unconditionally dereferences it, throwing a real, uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'taxId')` — confirmed live via the browser console on every single item-add, regardless of whether the item is selected by mouse click or by keyboard (ArrowDown+Enter), ruling out an event-handling difference as the cause. This does not block the flow entirely (the user can still manually pick the real batch from the `#batchIdf_N` dropdown afterward, which sets `item.selectedBatch` via a separate `ng-model` binding and lets the sale proceed correctly — verified: the resulting sale's rate/amount are correct), but it does silently break two secondary calculations for every item added before a batch is manually chosen: `ConvertQtyOrdered` (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'conversion')`) and `$scope.calculateReceivedQty` (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'uomUnit')`), both downstream of the same null `item.selectedBatch`. **Not fixed, no `.js` file touched, per explicit session scope.** | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/pharmacy/walkInSalesController.js:2188,2243,3019,9868` (all read-only, none modified) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — genuine application code bug, not a behavioral change or refactor) | Pending (owner review needed — low severity given the flow still completes correctly once a batch is manually picked, but the fix is straightforward: null-check `item.selectedBatch` before the `.taxId` read at line 2243, matching the guard the code already has two lines earlier) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. |
| BR-059 | 2026-07-09 | Not a business rule — reported per explicit task instruction to stop and report a genuine application CODE bug rather than work around it, found while looping the real Pharmacy Walk-In Sale save to close out BR-055's column-gap fixes — **the same non-atomic-multi-step-write defect class BR-047 and BR-052 already documented in two other, unrelated save flows, now confirmed in a third.** `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` (`legacy/Pharmacy/.../op/controller/WalkInSalesController.java:193-224`) calls three separate, independently-transactional service methods in sequence: `WalkInSalesService.saveBill(request)` (creates the `pharmacy.t_sale`/`t_sale_detail` rows and commits), then `WalkInSalesService.getSaleDetailsBySaleId(...)`, then (only if that returned rows) `WalkInSalesService.updateInsertIntoStoreStock(...)` (the actual stock-decrement + `inventory.t_stock_transaction` audit-log write). Because each is its own `@Transactional` method, a failure in the *second or third* step does not roll back the *first* step's already-committed sale. Reproduced directly during this session's own BR-055 column-gap iteration (not manufactured — a genuine side effect of hitting a real, still-unfixed-at-the-time `medication_type`/`billable_consumption_detail_id` column gap): one save attempt returned **HTTP 500** to the client (`updateInsertIntoStoreStock` failed on the then-missing `t_stock_transaction` columns) — the user would see a hard error and reasonably assume the sale never happened — yet `pharmacy.t_sale` `id=1` / `t_sale_detail` `id=1` (`item_id=1`, `batch_id=1`, `quantity=2`, `total_amount=24`, `net_amount=24`, `added_date` 2026-07-09 22:27:37) were already fully committed, yet `inventory.t_store_stock.available_qty` for that batch was **never decremented** for this sale (confirmed: `available_qty` only ever reflects the *next* attempt's own decrement, `500→498`, with zero `t_stock_transaction` row for sale `id=1`) — a real, orphaned "phantom sale" that would overstate revenue/dispensing in any sales report while inventory remains fully unaffected, with no user-visible indication anything was recorded at all. A separate, related manifestation: an *earlier* attempt in the same iteration returned **HTTP 200** with an essentially empty body (`{}`, no `id`, no `message`) and the client still displayed a false-positive `growl.success('Walk-in sales Successfully saved')` toast — traced to `WalkInSalesService.saveBill` itself silently swallowing an internal exception (the `medication_type` column gap poisoning the whole Postgres transaction, `current transaction is aborted, commands ignored until end of transaction block`) and returning a response with a `null` id, which the controller's own generic-exception handling never surfaces as an error to the client. This means a walk-in pharmacy sale can currently (a) silently appear to succeed while nothing was saved at all, or (b) genuinely half-save (sale recorded, stock untouched) while showing the user a hard failure — both without any reliable way for staff to know which happened. **Not fixed, no `.java` file touched, per explicit session scope** — reported here for owner review/Maker triage, same disposition as BR-047/BR-052. The orphan row (`pharmacy.t_sale`/`t_sale_detail` `id=1`) is left in place, not cleaned up, for the same reason BR-047/BR-052 gave: deleting rows a future investigation might still need to reference was judged riskier than leaving harmless test data — this is also why the final, committed, passing-once-BR-057-ships test creates its own fresh sale each run rather than asserting against a fixed id. | `legacy/Pharmacy/src/main/java/com/param/pharmacy/op/controller/WalkInSalesController.java:193-224` (read-only), `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/pharmacy/service/WalkInSalesService.java` (`saveBill`, `getSaleDetailsBySaleId`, `updateInsertIntoStoreStock`, all read-only, none modified) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect — genuine application code bug, non-atomic transaction; not a behavioral change or refactor) | Pending (owner review needed — this is the third occurrence of BR-047's non-atomicity defect class, in an unrelated flow, with a distinct new sub-variant: a fully-empty false-success response, not just a partial-write; needs a Maker session to either wrap all three service calls in one outer transaction boundary or make the controller compensate/rollback the sale if the later steps fail) | No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. Orphan row `pharmacy.t_sale`/`t_sale_detail` `id=1` left in local dev DB only, not cleaned up, flagged above. `id=2` is the genuine, fully-correct, fully-verified sale (see BR-055's Notes) used as this session's proof that the flow works end-to-end once BR-055's schema gaps and BR-057's `constants.js` bug are both addressed. |

| BR-060 | 2026-07-10 | `AppointmentSchedulingDaoImpl.getTokenNumber(String currentDate, Integer unitId)` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/opd/dao/AppointmentSchedulingDaoImpl.java:1591-1620`) is the shared helper every appointment-booking/token-assignment call site (`bookAppointment`-style flows at lines 1268/1303/2322, plus `AppointmentSchedulingServiceImpl.java:183/327/2068` and `PatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java:604`) uses to compute `max(token_number)+1` scoped to a specific appointment date, via `SELECT max(token_number) ... WHERE unit_id=:unitId AND to_char(appointment_date,'YYYY-MM-DD')=:date`. The method already accepted a `currentDate` parameter representing the actual target appointment date from the caller, but the query's `:date` parameter was never bound to it — instead it was hardcoded to `GlobalCommonDateUtils.getStringDate(TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(unitId), "yyyy-MM-dd")`, i.e. always "today" in the unit's timezone, regardless of what date the appointment/token being generated was actually for. Net effect: token-number scoping was silently keyed to the server's current date instead of the appointment's real date, so (a) two appointments for two different future/past dates booked on the same server-calendar-day would draw from the same "today" max-token pool and could receive colliding token numbers instead of each date having its own independent 1..N sequence, and (b) the per-date isolation `to_char(appointment_date,...)=:date` was clearly intended to provide never actually applied to anything but "today," making the date-scoping condition partially dead logic for any non-today booking. Confirmed live, not just by code inspection: reproduced the actual collision against the running `his-local-tomcat` container / local dev Postgres via real Tomcat stdout logs and direct Postgres queries showing `max(token_number)` being computed over `appointment_date = <server today>` even when the booking request's own date was a different day. **Fix applied** (minimal, this diff only): replaced the hardcoded `TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(unitId)` binding with the method's own `currentDate` parameter, normalized to a bare 10-character `YYYY-MM-DD` string (`tokenScopeDate = currentDate.length()>10 ? currentDate.substring(0,10) : currentDate`) before binding, since one call site — `markArrival` (line 1266, itself called from `AppointmentSchedulingController.java:301` and `EncounterMasterServiceImpl.scheduleAppointmentFromKiosk` at line 2405-2406 via `encounterMasterDto.getCurrentDate()`) — passes a full timestamp string rather than a bare date, and the query's `to_char(appointment_date,'YYYY-MM-DD')=:date` comparison requires the bare 10-char form to match. No other line in the method, and no other file, touched. **Verification performed this session:** (1) direct API calls confirming two appointments booked for the same real target date now receive sequential, non-colliding token numbers; (2) direct API calls confirming cross-date isolation — appointments booked for two different target dates each get their own independent token sequence instead of sharing one server-today-scoped pool; (3) a `markArrival` regression check confirming the full-timestamp call path still resolves to the correct token after the 10-char normalization; (4) 5 consecutive real end-to-end encounter-booking runs through the actual UI (not mocked/unit-tested), all collision-free. | `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/opd/dao/AppointmentSchedulingDaoImpl.java:1591-1620` (`getTokenNumber`, fixed); read-only call sites confirmed unaffected in signature/contract: `AppointmentSchedulingDaoImpl.java:1266` (`markArrival`), `:1298` (`markArrivalForPaidAppointments`), `:2322`; `AppointmentSchedulingServiceImpl.java:183,327,2068`; `PatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java:604`; `EncounterMasterServiceImpl.java:2405-2406` | Behavioral Change | Approved (owner-approved 2026-07-10, after independent Checker review including live bytecode verification and real cross-date booking reproduction) | Per RULES.md item 8, this entry uses a provisional ID (`BR-060`) for the life of branch `fix/appointment-token-number-collision`; the real next sequential `BR-XXX` number (computed from master's current highest ID at that time) will be assigned in the commit immediately preceding merge to master, not in this commit. Classified Behavioral Change per CLAUDE.md's definition: token numbers assigned for non-"today" bookings will differ from what the old (buggy) query would have returned — a genuine, intentional difference in output, not a byte-identical refactor, even though the new behavior is more correct. No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. This branch also has two uncommitted, unrelated local-dev-only files in the working tree (`url.config.js`, `constants.js`, see `docs/LOCAL_DEV_SETUP_NOTES.md` items 1 and 4) — these are deliberately excluded from this commit. |

| BR-065 | 2026-07-10 | Not a business rule — genuine live app defect found while building a LIS (Lab) order-to-result E2E test. OPD Cover Sheet (`views/OPD/CoverSheet/opd-coversheet.html`, the screen that hosts the "Orders" tab used to place any investigation/lab-test order) is unreachable via any live, allowed navigation path for the admin test persona: the only "go to cover sheet" trigger from the just-booked-encounter flow (`gotoCoverSheet()` in `encounterQueueManagementController.js`) is commented out in `encounter-queue-management.html:341`, and the only other trigger (`doctorDeskController.js`, ~line 1950) is gated to `roleId=='2'`, which the admin login never satisfies.
