| BR-072 | 2026-07-10 | **BR-052 fix, this session — owner directive: "one big save is essential", consolidate into a single database transaction, not compensating rollback logic.** Investigated `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission` (`legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/service/BedAllocationServiceImpl.java:230-455`) firsthand by reading the live code (not by trusting any prior claim): the `@Transactional` boundary was already correctly singular around the entire ~10-step save sequence (admission header, patient details, referral, transfer, sponsor details, waiting list, deposit, medico-legal, visitor pass) — so no boundary move was needed. The actual defect was that a blanket `catch (Exception e) { }` (completely empty) wrapped the whole method body: any exception thrown by any nested call was silently discarded and the method returned a normal `Response(ERROR,...)`, so no exception ever reached Spring's transaction interceptor and a mid-sequence failure committed anyway. Three further swallow points in the same call chain had the identical effect: `BedAllocationDaoImpl.savePatientAdmission` (empty catch after the `t_admission` insert), `BedAllocationDaoImpl.saveTransfer` (empty `HibernateException`/`Exception` catches after the `t_transfer` insert), and `UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.saveMedicoLegalDetails` (logged then swallowed). Also independently checked `BedAllocationDaoImpl.savePatientDetails` — already correctly rethrows via `catch(HibernateException){throw he;} catch(Exception e){throw e;}`, no fix needed there. Confirmed no nested call in the nine-call chain opens an independent physical transaction (`grep` for `REQUIRES_NEW` returned nothing). **Fix** (4 catch blocks changed): each of the 4 locations now does `throw new ApplicationException(e.getMessage(), e)` (`in.param.exception.ApplicationException`, confirmed via `javap` to extend `RuntimeException` and expose a `(String, Throwable)` constructor) instead of swallowing — unchecked, so it propagates through the `@Transactional` proxy (triggering rollback) and reaches `GlobalExceptionHandler`, the project's single centralized `@ControllerAdvice`, already registered for `ApplicationException.class`. **Verified live, both directions, via a deliberate-failure-injection test I ran myself:** added a temporary unconditional `throw new RuntimeException(...)` at the top of `BedAllocationDaoImpl.saveTransfer` (fires after the admission-header insert already executed in the same transaction), rebuilt the ADT jar (`mvn -pl ADT -am package -DskipTests`), `docker cp`'d it into the running `his-local-tomcat` container, restarted, and ran `ipd-admission.spec.js` against it: the client received `status:"error"` (HTTP 200 with an error body, the platform's existing exception-response shape) instead of a false success; independently confirmed via direct `psql` against `his-local-postgres`/`HIS-SME` that `adt.t_admission`/`adt.t_transfer` row counts were **unchanged** (77/5050 and 15/15, identical to the pre-test baseline) — zero orphan rows, full rollback of the earlier admission-header write. Reverted the injection (confirmed via `grep` that no trace remained and the diff returned to exactly the 3-file/4-catch-block fix), rebuilt and redeployed the clean fix, then ran `ipd-admission.spec.js` twice more: both runs' actual save assertions passed (HTTP 200, `status:"success"`, real `admissionId`/`admissionNumber`, growl "Patient Admitted Successfully..!!"), each independently DB-verified via `psql` (`admission_id` 36 and 37, unit-18 numbering `ADM-18-0000036`/`037`, each with a matching `t_transfer` row, `transfer_id` 16/17 — no orphans, `t_admission` count correctly incremented 77→79). Both runs' single remaining failure was the identical, already-documented, pre-existing, owner-approved-as-out-of-scope `500 .../getUnitApplicationBillingConfigList` (BR-044/BR-061's `vaccinationChartHide` type-mismatch defect, unrelated to this fix, fires on every page load regardless of admission outcome) — this spec's strict zero-failed-requests assertion means it cannot show a fully green Playwright result until BR-061's remaining gap is separately fixed, but the actual save/rollback behavior this task targets was confirmed correct and reproducible across both runs. | `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/dao/BedAllocationDaoImpl.java` (`savePatientAdmission`, `saveTransfer`); `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/admission/service/BedAllocationServiceImpl.java` (`savePatientAdmission`); `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/service/UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java` (`saveMedicoLegalDetails`) | Behavioral Change | Pending (owner review needed — this session cannot self-approve; a save that previously could silently commit a partial admission and report either false success or a generic error now correctly rolls back the entire multi-step save and reports a real error) | Real BR number assigned at commit time per this task's explicit instruction (current master's highest ID was `BR-071` immediately before this commit). Maker-Checker (RULES.md item 6) still applies before this branch merges to master — not merged. Scope note: this fix addresses exactly the 4 swallow points in `savePatientAdmission`'s own call chain; other unrelated raw-Hibernate manual-commit patterns exist elsewhere in the ADT module but are not called from this chain and were deliberately left untouched. No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. |
| BR-073 | 2026-07-10 | **BR-059 fix, this session — owner directive: "one big save is essential", consolidate into a single database transaction, not compensating rollback logic.** Investigated `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` (`legacy/Pharmacy/src/main/java/com/param/pharmacy/op/controller/WalkInSalesController.java:198-248`) firsthand: unlike BR-052, this genuinely was 3 separate physical transactions — the controller method itself is plain `@RequestMapping` (not `@Transactional`), and calls `WalkInSalesService.saveBill` (`@Transactional(rollbackOn=CustomException.class)`), then `.getSaleDetailsBySaleId` (`@Transactional`), then conditionally `.updateInsertIntoStoreStock` (`@Transactional`) as 3 sequential, independently-proxied calls, each opening and committing its own transaction. Compounding this, `WalkInSalesService.saveBill` (swallowed and returned `null` on any `Exception` from `createSale`) and its private helper `createSale` (3 further swallow points, including one wrapping virtually the entire method body) all caught and discarded exceptions instead of propagating them, so even a failure inside the *first* call's own transaction wouldn't reliably roll back either — same "excessive local try/catch silently swallowing errors" pattern as BR-052. Independently confirmed `getSaleDetailsBySaleId` and `updateInsertIntoStoreStock` already correctly rethrow (`catch(Exception e){throw e;}`), no fix needed there. **Fix**: added a new orchestrating method, `WalkInSalesService.saveBillComplete` (`@Transactional(rollbackOn = Exception.class)`, broader than `saveBill`'s own `CustomException`-only rule since the goal is rollback on any failure in the consolidated chain), that calls `this.saveBill(request)` → `this.getSaleDetailsBySaleId(...)` → `this.updateInsertIntoStoreStock(...)` via self-invocation — bypasses each method's own `@Transactional` proxy so all 3 execute inside `saveBillComplete`'s single physical transaction instead of 3 independent ones. `IWalkInSalesService` gained the matching interface declaration. `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` now calls `saveBillComplete` once instead of the 3 separate calls; the conditional `globalBillingService.postServicesIntoExistingBill` call (a different bean/module, only invoked when `request.isAddToBill()==true`) was deliberately left outside this transaction boundary — not one of the 3 methods this task named, and expanding into the Billing module unilaterally was out of scope. Also fixed the 4 swallow points (`saveBill`, `createSale` x3) to rethrow instead of silently logging/discarding. Compiled clean (`mvn -pl Global,Pharmacy -am compile`). **Verified live, via a deliberate-failure-injection test I ran myself:** added a temporary unconditional `throw new RuntimeException(...)` at the top of `updateInsertIntoStoreStock` (fires after the sale header + sale detail rows already executed within the same transaction, via `createSale`), rebuilt the Global jar, `docker cp`'d it into `his-local-tomcat`, restarted, and ran `pharmacy-dispense.spec.js`: the client got a broken/error response (HTTP 200 with an empty, unparseable body — a pre-existing platform quirk on this specific exception path, not this fix's concern, but clearly not a successful save); independently confirmed via direct `psql` against `his-local-postgres`/`HIS-SME` that `pharmacy.t_sale`/`t_sale_detail` row counts were **unchanged** (10/12 both, identical to the pre-test baseline) — zero orphan rows, full rollback of the earlier sale-header/detail writes. Reverted the injection (confirmed via `grep` that no trace remained, diff back to exactly the 3-file fix), rebuilt/redeployed clean, then ran `pharmacy-dispense.spec.js` twice more: both runs' actual save assertions passed (HTTP 200, real sale `id`, growl "Walk-in sales Successfully saved"), each independently DB-verified via `psql` (sale `id` 14 and 15, each with exactly one matching `t_sale_detail` row, quantity 2 — no orphans, `t_sale`/`t_sale_detail` counts correctly incremented 10→12). Both runs' single remaining failure was the identical, already-documented, pre-existing, owner-approved-as-out-of-scope `500 .../getUnitApplicationBillingConfigList` (BR-044/BR-061, unrelated to this fix) — this spec's strict zero-failed-requests assertion means it cannot show a fully green Playwright result until BR-061's remaining gap is separately fixed, but the actual save/rollback behavior this task targets was confirmed correct and reproducible across both runs. **Follow-up, later same-day session — closed one additional swallow point in the same call chain that this entry's own fix did not touch:** re-investigated `WalkInSalesService.savePatient` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/pharmacy/service/WalkInSalesService.java:2655`, called from inside `createSale` for the self-payee walk-in path — confirmed live via `psql` that `pharmacy.t_temp_patient_registration` gains a matching row on every standard walk-in sale in this test environment, i.e. this is not a rare branch) — it had the identical swallow-and-return-generic-error shape as the other 4 points already fixed above (`catch(Exception e){logger.error(...);} return new Response<>(ERROR,...)`), just not enumerated in the original investigation. Fixed identically to this entry's established style: `throw e;` instead of swallowing (kept the plain-rethrow style already used above, rather than introducing a different wrapper type, for consistency within this file). **Verified live, independently, via a second deliberate-failure-injection test targeting this specific method:** recorded a fresh baseline (`pharmacy.t_sale`/`t_sale_detail`/`t_temp_patient_registration` all 16), injected `if (true) throw new RuntimeException("BR-059-SAVEPATIENT-DELIBERATE-FAILURE...")` immediately after `iPatientRegistrationDao.savePatientRegistration(dto)` succeeds (i.e. after a real patient row is already inserted in the same transaction), rebuilt/redeployed the Global jar, re-applied the two local-dev-only frontend patches, and attempted a real sale: `docker logs` confirmed the real injected exception (not a generic message) propagated cleanly all the way to `WalkInSalesController`'s own catch, and direct `psql` immediately after showed all three tables **unchanged** (16/16/16) — zero orphan patient/temp-registration/sale rows, full rollback of the already-inserted patient row. Reverted the injection (`git diff` against the prior commit on this branch showed only the intended one-line `savePatient` fix, no injection trace), rebuilt/redeployed the clean combined fix (this entry's `saveBillComplete` change plus the `savePatient` fix together), then ran `pharmacy-dispense.spec.js` twice more: both runs' save assertions passed identically to the baseline described above, and `psql` confirmed exactly 2 new rows across all three tables (16→18), with the same single pre-existing unrelated `getUnitApplicationBillingConfigList` 500 as the only remaining spec failure. Also independently re-ran this entry's own original baseline (clean fix, no injection) once before starting this follow-up, as a sanity check that the already-committed `saveBillComplete` consolidation still works after a fresh container restart — it did, matching this entry's original claims and corroborating that the original verification above was genuine, not fabricated. | `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/pharmacy/service/IWalkInSalesService.java` (new method declaration); `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/pharmacy/service/WalkInSalesService.java` (`saveBill`, `createSale`, `savePatient`, new `saveBillComplete`); `legacy/Pharmacy/src/main/java/com/param/pharmacy/op/controller/WalkInSalesController.java` (`saveBill`, now calls `saveBillComplete`) | Behavioral Change | Pending (owner review needed — this session cannot self-approve; a save that previously could silently commit a partial sale now correctly rolls back the entire chain) | Real BR number assigned at commit time per this task's explicit instruction (current master's highest ID was `BR-071` immediately before BR-072/BR-073 were drafted this session; BR-072 is BR-052's companion entry, drafted in this same session but committed, if at all, from a separate branch). Maker-Checker (RULES.md item 6) still applies before this branch merges to master — not merged. Scope note (`postServicesIntoExistingBill`): deliberately left outside the new transaction boundary, flagged here for explicit owner/architect decision on whether it should also be folded into a wider atomic boundary in a follow-up. | BR-062 | 2026-07-10 | **Root cause found and fixed this session.** Investigated Nursing Vitals save (`#/vitals` → `#/addNewVitals` → `VitalAddController.saveVitals()` → `POST /global/vital` → `VitalsController.saveVitals` → `VitalServiceImpl`/`VitalDaoImpl`). Two specific risks were flagged going in and both were conclusively resolved as non-issues via live testing, not just re-reading the code: (1) the frontend always sends `admissionId:""` (empty string) against a backend DTO field (`TVitalDto.admissionId`) typed `Integer` — live-proven harmless: a real POST with `admissionId:""` sailed through Spring/Jackson deserialization and the full service/DAO call chain with no admissionId-related error at any point (confirmed by the request failing much later, deep inside `VitalDaoImpl.saveVitals`, at an unrelated line — see below); Jackson evidently coerces the empty string to a `null` Integer, and `null` is the semantically correct value for a non-admitted OPD patient's admission id anyway, not a data gap. (2) the save URL (`BASE_URL+ROOT_URL+GLOBAL+SAVE_VITALS` → `/global/vital`) omits the `/api` prefix its sibling `getVitalsList()` call uses — live-proven correct as written: `VitalsController` is mapped at class level to `/global` (no `/api`), confirmed by a real `POST http://localhost:8081/global/vital` reaching the controller and returning HTTP 200 (not a 404); the `/api`-prefixed sibling call is a separate, pre-existing dead/broken call unrelated to save. **The actual, previously-undiagnosed blocking bug**, found only by driving the real UI end-to-end: `VitalDaoImpl.saveVitals` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/VitalDaoImpl.java:54`) called `LocalTime.parse(dto.getVitalTime())` completely unguarded, while the same method's two other date-ish fields two lines below (`createdDate`, `updatedDate`) both have a `!= null && !isEmpty()` guard with a `TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(dto.getUnitId())` fallback. `vitalObj.vitalTime` defaults to `""` in `VitalAddController.js`'s `initVital()` and nothing in the UI ever auto-populates it (the Time field is a free-text clockpicker input, not required, no default), so every real-world save that leaves Time blank — the common case — hit `DateTimeParseException: Text '' could not be parsed at index 0`, caught by the controller's try/catch and returned as an HTTP-200-wrapped `{"status":"error","code":"500","message":"Text '' could not be parsed at index 0"}`, i.e. every single Vitals save silently failed with no visible client-side error (confirmed live: `public.t_vital` had zero rows before this fix, on an environment with otherwise-active Nursing usage). **Fix** (`VitalDaoImpl.java:54-56`, 2 lines added): added the same null/empty guard as the two sibling fields, falling back to the current time (derived from the identical `TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(dto.getUnitId())` call already used twice more in this method) when `vitalTime` is blank — mirrors existing precedent in the same method, no new date-utility introduced. Verified live: patched `.class` deployed into the running `his-local-tomcat` container's jar (same `jar uf` technique as the BR-047 diagnostic session, restarted cleanly), then 3 consecutive full Playwright runs (fresh OPD-registered patient each run, Time field deliberately left blank matching the real bug condition) all passed end-to-end — real `POST /global/vital` → HTTP 200 `{"status":"success","message":"Added Successfully."}` — each independently reconciled via direct `psql`: `t_vital_id` 5/6/7, correct `patient_id`, `vital_time` correctly defaulted to real current time, matching `t_vital_details` row with the entered observation value. Also regression-checked the untouched branch (a real Time value supplied, e.g. `"14:30:00"`) still parses and saves correctly (`t_vital_id=4`, `vital_time=14:30:00`), confirming no change to the pre-existing working path. | BR-061 | 2026-07-10 | **Root cause found and fixed this session.** `$scope.saveBillings()` (`newOPDBillingController.js`) branches on `$scope.requestStatusObject` to decide whether to save the bill or show a discount-confirmation popup: `if($scope.requestStatusObject != null || != undefined){ if(reqStatus==1){...} else if(reqStatus==2){...} }` — with no final `else`. `requestStatusObject` is initialized to `{}` (an empty object, not `null`) at controller load and is only ever reassigned by the async discount-request-status check (`getDisRiqFlag`-gated, itself only triggered by the manual UHID-search/patient-select flows) — which never fires on the direct-from-encounter navigation path this test (and the real "register → book encounter → bill" flow) uses. So `requestStatusObject` stays `{}` (non-null, but with `reqStatus` `undefined`) for the whole session: `{} != null` is true, so the code enters the `if`, but `reqStatus` matches neither `1` nor `2`, so **neither branch fires and the function returns having done absolutely nothing** — no save call, no popup, no warning, no console error. The Save button looks clickable and enabled the entire time; the click is simply a no-op. Confirmed live via a debug Playwright script that dumped `$scope.requestStatusObject`/`$scope.getDisRiqFlag` immediately before and 6s after the click: `{}` / `false` in both cases, `$scope.settleobj` still `null` after the click (proof `saveBillngNew()` was never invoked). **Fix** (`newOPDBillingController.js`, `saveBillings()`, ~16 lines added): added the missing `else` to the `reqStatus==1`/`reqStatus==2` chain, mirroring the exact fallback the sibling `requestStatusObject == null` branch a few lines below already uses for the equivalent "no active discount request" case — check `finalBill=='Y' && cashSettlement=='N'` and call `saveBillngNew()` directly, else show `#saveConfirmPopup`. Verified: after the fix, clicking Save now reliably fires `POST /api/billing/saveBill`. **Three further schema/deployment gaps surfaced and fixed once the save request started actually reaching the server** (found via `log_statement=all` Postgres statement-logging capture of the exact failing SQL, same technique as BR-051), all local dev DB only, no `.java` file touched: (1) `t_order_details.is_dummy_service`/`is_hidden_service` were `character varying(50)` storing the literal text `"false"`/`"true"`, while every sibling boolean-semantic column on the same table (`is_package`, `markup_status`, `debit_note_service`, `is_consumed`) and the JPA entity (`OrderDetailsMaster.java`, `private Boolean isDummyService`) are genuine SQL `boolean` — the mismatched two caused `operator does not exist: character varying = boolean` in both a raw-SQL comparison (`BillingMasterDaoImpl.matchBillAmounts`, silently swallowed by its `catch(HibernateException){}`, always returning `false` — surfaced to the user as `"Dispute in orders count, refreshing orders !"` on every final bill regardless of the real order count) and an HQL comparison (`BillingMasterDaoImpl.getOrderedServicesByEncounterId`'s `orderDetails.isDummyService=false`, which cannot be worked around with SQL-text quoting since HQL types the literal against the entity's declared `Boolean` property — this ruled out a code-side fix short of changing the entity's Java type everywhere, a much larger and riskier change). Fixed at the root by `ALTER TABLE t_order_details ALTER COLUMN is_dummy_service/is_hidden_service TYPE boolean USING (...::boolean)` (all existing rows were the literal text `'false'`, cleanly castable), matching the JPA mapping and the table's own sibling-column convention — the lower-blast-radius fix given 150+ existing call sites across Billing/Global/LIS already treat both columns as booleans. (2) `billing.t_bill_payee_details` was missing 4 columns declared on its entity (`BillPayeeDetails.java`): `cr_net_amt` (`double precision`) — actively populated via `billPayeeDetailsDto.setCrNetAmount(0.00)` in `BillingMasterServiceImpl.saveBill()`'s payee-details-save step, confirmed a genuinely distinct field from the already-present `cr_amount` (both independently read/written elsewhere) — plus `paid_amount` (`double precision`), `payment_entitlement_id` (`integer`), `priority_id` (`integer`), all part of the same INSERT. Same DDL-drift gap class as BR-038 through BR-051. (3) `m_application_configuration` was missing `public_url` (`character varying`), read by `UnitApplicationBillingConfigurationDaoImpl.getPublicUrl()` — called from `BillingMasterController.saveBill()` **after** the bill is already committed, to build an SMS/email report link; this is a genuine live reproduction of the non-atomicity concern flagged for this session (see below), not the collectPayment-specific instance but the same defect class: the bill (`billing_master_id=6056`, confirmed via direct `psql`) was durably committed while the client simultaneously received an HTTP 500 because this unrelated, non-critical downstream step threw. **Non-atomicity (`saveBillService`/`collectPayment`) — reported only, not fixed, per instruction.** Confirmed both architecturally and live: `BillingMasterServiceImpl.saveBillService` (`@Transactional`, line 618) and `.collectPayment` (`@Transactional`, line 1876) are two independently-`@Transactional` methods, called sequentially from `BillingMasterController.saveBill()` (itself not `@Transactional`) — Spring's default `REQUIRED` propagation gives each its own separate database transaction. Live proof of the failure mode this produces: in the run immediately before fix (3) above, `billing.m_billing_master` id 6056 (encounter 120, `net_amount`/`total_self_payable`=400, matching the two line items exactly) was already committed and visible via direct `psql` query at the exact moment the client received `HTTP 500` — i.e. the money-critical write had already durably succeeded while the overall request was reported to the user as a failure, because a later, unrelated step (SMS-link generation in this instance) threw inside the same handler after the first transaction had already committed. This is the same "commit-then-fail-on-a-later-unrelated-step" shape as the specifically-named `saveBillService`→`collectPayment` split (same defect class as BR-047/BR-052/BR-059), just caught via a different concrete downstream call in this session's reproduction; a `collectPayment`-specific failure was not separately fault-injected (would require deliberately re-breaking a step just fixed), but the transactional-boundary analysis plus this live proof together confirm the underlying architectural risk is real and reachable in the standard OPD final-bill flow, not theoretical. **Verification:** 3 consecutive full Playwright runs (`billing-invoice.spec.js`, `--project=chromium --workers=1`, fresh patient/encounter each run) all passed end-to-end (registration → encounter booking → add tariff-priced service → Save → real `POST /api/billing/saveBill` → HTTP 200 `{"status":"success",...}` with a real `billId`). Each of the 3 runs' resulting bill independently reconciled via direct `psql` against `billing.m_billing_master` (`net_amount`/`total_self_payable`) vs `sum(t_order_details.net_amount/self_payable)` for that encounter's 2 line items (`OPD Registration` ₹100 + `General Consultation` ₹300 = ₹400, exact match, not just within the server's own `matchBillAmounts` +/-1 tolerance) — billing_master_id 6059/6060/6061, all exact. | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/billing/transaction/newOPDBillingController.js` (`saveBillings()`, fixed, ~16 lines added); `legacy/Billing/src/main/java/com/param/billing/global/transaction/dao/BillingMasterDaoImpl.java` (`matchBillAmounts`, `getOrderedServicesByEncounterId` — read-only for diffing, reverted to original after an interim fix attempt, ultimately unmodified); `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/OrderDetailsMasterDaoImpl.java` (read-only for diffing, reverted to original, ultimately unmodified); `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/billing/global/transaction/model/BillPayeeDetails.java`, `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/global/model/OrderDetailsMaster.java` (both read-only for diffing, neither modified); local dev DB only (`t_order_details.is_dummy_service`/`is_hidden_service` type change varchar→boolean; `billing.t_bill_payee_details` +4 columns; `m_application_configuration` +1 column), no tracked DDL file changed | Behavioral Change | Approved (owner-approved 2026-07-10, after independent Checker review confirming diff minimality, 2 additional consecutive passing E2E runs, and DB-verified monetary reconciliation) | Per RULES.md item 8, this entry keeps its provisional ID (`BR-061`) for the life of branch `fix/billing-save-button-hang`; the real next sequential `BR-XXX` number (computed from master's current highest ID, `BR-060` as of this session) will be assigned in the commit immediately preceding merge to master, not in this commit. No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. Two unrelated, uncommitted local-dev-only files already present in the working tree (`url.config.js`, `constants.js`, see `docs/LOCAL_DEV_SETUP_NOTES.md` items 1 and 4) were deliberately left out of this commit — not committed, not reverted. Two pre-existing, unrelated, out-of-scope errors remain on every page load (`m_application_configuration`'s `vaccinationChartHide` boolean/string type mismatch and the `getUnitApplicationBillingConfigList` 500 it causes) — already documented as out-of-scope in BR-044's session notes, confirmed here to not block the bill save. Did not merge to master. |
| BR-069 | 2026-07-10 | **Root cause found and fixed this session, follow-up to BR-065.** `OPDCoverSheetController.js`'s `$scope.checkAccessCover(id)`/`$scope.checkAccessCoverSubTab(id,headerId)` (~line 12745, gate every OPD Cover Sheet tab including Orders via `ng-if` in `opd-coversheet.html`) call `.filter()`/iterate on `$scope.mappedDataCoversheetByUserss`, which is parsed straight from `localStorage.getItem('mappedDataCoversheetByUser')` with no null-guard. That localStorage value is itself `JSON.parse(response.data.listObject[0].tabAccessIds)` from `GET /api/opd/getMappedCoversheetDataForUser`, which reads `public.t_user_unit_mapper.tab_access_ids` — confirmed live via direct `psql` that this column is genuinely `NULL` for **every** row in `t_user_unit_mapper` in this dev DB (all 7 rows: user_id 1/2/2001-2005, admin and non-admin alike), not an admin-specific gap. So `mappedDataCoversheetByUserss` is `null` for any user today, and every cover-sheet tab-access check hard-crashes with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'filter')`, confirmed live via a debug Playwright probe reading the crash directly off the live `$scope`. Considered seeding a real `tab_access_ids` config row for the admin test persona instead (as the session's task explicitly allowed), but rejected it: (a) it wouldn't be a general fix — every other user in the DB would still crash; (b) the one existing UI that writes this column (`userRoleManagementMasterController.js`'s tab-access-management screen) explicitly restricts itself to configuring Doctor/Nurse-role users only (`growl.warning("Access only for doctor or nurse")`), so fabricating a full clinical-tab-access config for the Admin persona would not reflect how this mechanism is actually meant to be used in production — a code-side null-guard is the more correct, general fix. **Fix** (2 functions, 4 lines added): both functions now return `true` (fail-open — treat "no config row" as "no restriction configured", i.e. show the tab) when `$scope.mappedDataCoversheetByUserss` is null/empty, before doing any `.filter()`/iteration on it. Same defensive-default class as BR-061/BR-062's fixes: nothing currently succeeds for any user on this path (100%-reproducible crash for every row in the table), so a fail-open default cannot regress an existing working case, and it unblocks the entire OPD Cover Sheet (not just Orders) for every user in the current dev DB. **Verified live**, not by inspection alone: a debug Playwright probe drove a real patient registration → OPD encounter booking, manually set `sessionStorage.cookieEncounterObject` (mirroring `gotoCoverSheet()`'s own construction, since the real UI navigation triggers to this screen are separately blocked — see BR-065's own notes and BR-066 below) and navigated to `/#/OPDCoverSheet`, then called `scope.checkAccessCover(6)` (the Orders tab's real gate id) directly off the live, running `$scope` — confirmed `mappedDataCoversheetByUserss` was genuinely `null` (the real live condition, not a simulated one) and `checkAccessCover(6)` returned `true` instead of throwing. The Orders tab does not yet visibly render in the DOM after this fix alone — a second, independent crash (BR-066, below) interrupts the same digest cycle later in controller construction, before Angular finishes applying the `ng-if` bindings; full end-to-end tab visibility is verified together with BR-066's fix. | `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/opd/transection/OPDCoverSheetController.js:12745-12772` (`checkAccessCover`, `checkAccessCoverSubTab`) | Neither (Pre-existing Defect fix — restores intended default-access behavior, not a new business rule or calculation change) | Approved (owner-approved 2026-07-10, after independent Checker review verifying diff minimality, live behavior via direct scope inspection, and the tab_access_ids NULL claim via direct psql) | Per RULES.md item 8, this entry uses a provisional ID for the life of branch `fix/opd-coversheet-tabaccess-null-crash`; the real next sequential `BR-XXX` number will be assigned in the commit immediately preceding merge to master. | BR-068 | 2026-07-10 | Not a business rule — two combined pre-existing gaps found while building the Growth Chart (Nursing, `#/growthChart`) E2E test, per this session's explicit scope (EHR workflow target: Growth Chart). **(1) Schema gap, materially blocking, larger than the master-data gap alone:** `public.m_growth_chart_master` was missing the `gender_id` column entirely, even though `GrowthChartMaster.java`'s JPA mapping (`@Column(name="gender_id")`) and, more importantly, the actual live query path (`GrowthChartDaoImpl.getGrowthChartTransaction`'s raw SQL, the only implementation backing `/api/global/growthChart/patient/details`) both reference `growth_chart.gender_id` directly — confirmed via `psql \d+ public.m_growth_chart_master` showing no such column, meaning every growth-chart-master read/write path (list, save, by-id, exists-check, and the patient-transaction fetch) would fail live with `column "gender_id" does not exist` regardless of whether master data existed. **(2) Master-data gap, same class as BR-067's LIS finding:** `m_height_unit`, `m_weight_unit`, `m_circufereance_unit` (all 3 unit lookup tables the master row's FKs require) and `m_growth_chart_master` itself were all 0 rows — the 3 `INNER JOIN`s in every read query (`GET_GROWTH_CHART_LIST`, the live `getGrowthChartTransaction`) mean zero rows would ever surface even once (1) was fixed. **Fixed the schema gap** (`ALTER TABLE public.m_growth_chart_master ADD COLUMN gender_id integer` + FK to `m_gender_master(gender_id)`, mirroring the existing FK-naming/nullability convention of the table's other 3 unit FKs) — purely additive (Expand-only), no existing column altered or dropped, no other session's data touched. **Seeded the master-data gap, structural only**: 1 row each in `m_height_unit` ('Cm'), `m_weight_unit` ('Kg'), `m_circufereance_unit` ('Cm'); 1 placeholder `m_growth_chart_master` row (org 1/unit 1 "DEMO", `age_in_months`=6, `gender_id`=1 "Male", referencing the 3 new unit rows). Per this session's explicit task scope and the same caution as BR-067's follow-up: `weight_from`/`weight_to`/`height_from`/`height_to`/`circumference_from`/`circumference_to` were deliberately left `NULL` — populating them would mean fabricating a clinical "normal range" claim (e.g. inventing a WHO-percentile-like band for a 6-month-old), a business/clinical judgment call, not mechanical gap-filling against an already-established shape. Programmatically md5sum'd every file under `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/` (3,356 local / 3,372 deployed) and `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/resources/` (17/17) against the equivalent path in the running `his-local-tomcat` container. **Result: found zero NEW instances of the anticipated pattern (deployed stale/behind source, safe to fix by redeploying source).** Instead found the opposite and more serious problem — the container is running substantially *ahead of* git with uncommitted, unreviewed, in places completely untracked code, discovered in 5 parts. **(1) 76 JS/CSS mismatches under `resources/`:** 53 match `git stash@{1}` exactly (the BR-056-class `sessionStorage`/`cookieObjectStore` null-guard candidate fix, 57 files) and 1 matches `git stash@{2}` exactly (`OPDCoverSheetController.js`, the BR-066 `stopAllRecordings` typeof-guard fix) — both stashes are leftover, `git stash push -u`'d during a 2026-07-10 session per that session's own log entry, and were evidently `docker cp`'d into the container for live verification before being stashed rather than committed, so the container kept the fix while `master` did not. The remaining 22 files show the identical defensive-guard pattern (adding a `return;`/`typeof`/`|| {}` guard around a `sessionStorage`-derived null) but match no surviving stash at all — orphaned, unrecoverable from git, same root cause (an earlier live-verification `docker cp` whose source-side change was later lost by a `git reset`/checkout instead of a stash). None of these 76 are safe to "fix" by redeploying source over them — doing so would silently revert unreviewed-but-apparently-working defensive fixes; none are safe to commit-and-keep either, since they were never Maker-Checker reviewed or owner-approved. `url.config.js`/`constants.js` differences were confirmed to be exactly the documented, intentional local-dev-only substitution in `docs/LOCAL_DEV_SETUP_NOTES.md` and correctly excluded from all of the above. **(2) `src/main/resources` (17/17 compared): `jdbc.properties` mismatch is a serious secrets-hygiene finding**, not a stale-file bug — the deployed copy carries real plaintext DB credentials and API keys for half a dozen past QA/SME hosts (commented and live), with the one *active* (uncommitted) block pointing `jdbc.url` at a **remote** QA Postgres host, not localhost — contradicting the "local dev Postgres" framing used throughout recent session-log entries; source correctly holds only `CHANGE_ME_SET_LOCALLY_NOT_IN_GIT` placeholders per BR-020. `log4j.properties` has `org.hibernate.sql` at `TRACE` in the container vs `ERROR` in source — likely a leftover debug setting, low severity. **(3) 18 deployed files under `resources/assets/css` and `resources/assets/images/services_img` (`New_Coversheet_CSS/*`, `theme*.css`, service SVGs, `choices.min.js`) have never existed anywhere in `git log --all` — fully untracked.** Follow-up check of the container's `index.html` (outside this task's literal `resources/` scope, but necessary to interpret finding (3)) shows it has an untracked `<link>`/`<script>` block, commented "HIS UI MODERNISATION THEME START (Sprint 1)" / "Phase 2: Local dev mode flag", loading these files plus `resources/assets/js/config/local-dev-mode.js` — none of which exists in source's `index.html`. This is a live, direct violation of `CLAUDE.md`'s mission ordering (UI/version upgrade is explicitly Phase 4, last, only after Phases 1–2 are proven). **(4) The referenced `local-dev-mode.js` is confirmed NOT currently active** — it does not exist at the deployed path (`ls` exit code 2) so the script tag 404s silently exactly as its own comment predicts, and no deployed JS anywhere references its `LOCAL_UI_DEV_MODE` flag. However, a file of that exact name does exist in the repo, only under `legacy-codewhale-attempt/devops/` — the parallel Angular 18 rewrite attempt a 2026-07-03 session explicitly flagged as "unreliable/corrupted...not to be built upon." Its content sets `LOCAL_UI_DEV_MODE = true` and its own comment states this "bypasses OAuth/JWT and creates a local session with SuperAdmin permissions for UI development" — an authentication-bypass mechanism. Currently inert (confirmed, not exploitable as deployed today), but the wiring attempt itself, sourced from the explicitly-distrusted directory, is a serious finding on its own regardless of activity status. **(5) Two trivial git-hygiene items, no functional impact:** `assets/.DS_Store` (junk, not deployed) and a duplicate case-variant pair `MathJax.js`/`mathjax.js` (identical content, macOS case-insensitivity artifact) — `MathJax.js` isn't deployed but isn't loaded locally either (`index.html` loads MathJax from a CDN, not the local file), so no behavior is affected either way. **Jar-freshness check (separate task instruction): PASSED.** Extracted and `diff -rq`'d every class file (ignoring only `META-INF` manifest timestamps) in the deployed `ADT-0.0.1-SNAPSHOT.jar`/`Global-0.0.1-SNAPSHOT.jar` against a fresh `mvn -pl ADT,Global -am package -DskipTests` of current `master` HEAD — zero class-level differences, confirming BR-052/BR-059/BR-062's Java fixes are genuinely live in the running container. **No fix applied and no container/source file touched by this session** — every one of the 5 findings above carries real risk in at least one direction (reverting a working-but-unreviewed fix, deploying unreviewed code without the Maker-Checker sign-off `RULES.md` item 6 requires, or destabilizing the container the concurrent BR/E2E session is actively using), so per `CLAUDE.md`'s explicit instruction to stop and flag rather than proceed on an assumption when a request's premise turns out to conflict with project rules, this entry reports findings only. | `his-local-tomcat` container state (`resources/`, `src/main/resources`, `index.html`, `WEB-INF/lib/*.jar`); no application source file in `legacy/` or `new/` modified | Neither (discovery/report — no rule defined, no code or config changed) | Pending | Recommends a dedicated future session — ideally after the concurrent E2E/DB session completes, to avoid disturbing its in-flight work on the shared container — to: (a) for each of the 76 file-level drifts, either push it through a real Maker-Checker + owner-approval cycle and commit it, or revert the container to match clean `master`, restoring container/source parity as the baseline invariant; (b) rotate the plaintext external DB/Razorpay/mail credentials found live in the deployed `jdbc.properties`, regardless of whether they're still valid; (c) get an explicit owner ruling on the untracked "UI Modernisation Sprint 1"/"Phase 2 local-dev-mode" injection into `index.html` before anyone builds on it further, given both the Phase-4-last ordering rule and the auth-bypass code its Phase 2 flag would activate if ever fully wired up. No E2E test was re-run per the task's self-verify instruction, since no fix was made to verify. |
| BR-076 | 2026-07-11 | Not a business rule — full-scale (all 1,660 tables) structural reconciliation of `docs/staging_schema_reference.sql` vs. the live `his-local-postgres`/`HIS-SME` dev DB, extending BR-075's proven 4-table method to every table. Wrote `scripts/br076_schema_reconcile.py`: parses every `CREATE TABLE` in the reference file (paren-depth-aware column splitter, handles `numeric(p,s)`, `CHECK(...)`, quoted/spaced identifiers e.g. `service."t_service tarrif stattime manager"`) into schema/table/column/type/nullable/default, queries `information_schema.columns` for the live equivalent, and diffs. Two parser bugs were caught and fixed mid-run before trusting the output: (1) a table-name regex that didn't allow quoted identifiers containing spaces silently dropped 1 of 1,660 reference tables; (2) the live-side type normalizer wasn't routed through the same alias/strip logic as the reference-side one, manufacturing ~110 false "mismatches" (e.g. `time` vs `time without time zone` counted as different when they're the same type). Also identified and normalized away a reference-file-generator artifact — `varchar(10485760)`/`bpchar(10485760)` (43 occurrences) is a sentinel this dump tool emits for "no explicit length", confirmed against 3 live spot-checks that the equivalent live column is genuinely unbounded `character varying` — treating it as a real 10MB length constraint would have manufactured 42 more false mismatches. **Results: reference 1,660 tables / live 1,566 tables.** (a) **127 tables entirely missing locally**, by schema: public 71, abdm 11, lab 9, adt 7, procurement 6, emr 4, inventory 4, opd 4, blood_bank 2, otims 2, patient 2, sso 2, doctor 1, pharmacy 1, service 1 — verbatim `CREATE TABLE` DDL extracted from the reference file into `docs/br076_missing_tables.sql` (127 statements, byte-identical to source, not hand-retyped). (b) **432 missing columns across 168 existing tables** — billing 42 cols/20 tables, public 139/42, lab 53/16, doctor 40/6, procurement 21/11, patient 20/14, pharmacy 20/8, otims 23/7, opd 19/7, inventory 17/13, adt 14/10, kitchen 12/5, service 7/5, emr 4/3, blood_bank 1/1 — generated as additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` statements (exact type/default from the reference, deliberately omitting `NOT NULL` even where the reference has it, so no ALTER can fail against existing rows) in `docs/br076_missing_columns.sql`. (c) **531 columns across 310 tables with a type disagreement — flagged only, nothing altered.** Full detail in `docs/br076_diff_report.json`'s `category_c_type_mismatches`; the dominant pattern by far is `char` (reference) vs. Postgres's distinct internal `"char"` 1-byte type (live) — 292 of the 531, spread across many tables, almost certainly a systemic, long-standing local-schema convention rather than anything from tonight, but genuinely a different type OID and not something this task should silently reconcile either direction. Remainder is a long tail: `character varying`/`text` boundary differences (~90), `time` vs `timestamp` (17, a real type-family difference, not a formatting artifact), `numeric(10,2)` vs `double precision` (16, real precision-semantics difference), `character varying(200)` vs `bytea` (7, worth a specific look — a length-bounded text column on the reference side reported as raw binary live), plus assorted length-only varchar differences. (d) **33 tables exist locally but not in the reference — flagged only, nothing deleted.** Names strongly suggest test/backup artifacts, not features: 6 dated-suffix backups (`*_bkp`, `*_030321`, `*_080421`, `*_090420`, `*_211220`, `*_021019`), 11 `*_deolali`-suffixed tables (looks like a specific unit/site's test seed set — `m_generic_deolali`, `m_store_deolali`, etc.), plus `public.temp_nurse_assignment`, `public.t_aws_credentials`, `public.cname`; full list in `docs/br076_diff_report.json`'s `category_d_local_only_tables`. **Stopped before Step 3 (applying (a)/(b) as DDL) and everything after it — did not execute, did not run E2E, nothing in this DB was touched.** Reason: this task's own framing (a Python script that structurally modifies up to 559 objects — 127 tables + 432 columns — across 15 schemas of the shared dev DB) is exactly what RULES.md item 6 defines as "a database migration script," which that file requires Maker-Checker on — a separate AI/model, different session — "before a human ever sees it," and RULES.md is explicit that it wins over any conflicting instruction. This same task's own instructions separately said, in this exact session, not to delegate to any subagent for any reason. Those two requirements cannot both be satisfied inside one session, so per CLAUDE.md's explicit instruction for exactly this situation ("say so explicitly and stop — do not proceed on the assumption the owner meant to override it"), this entry reports the completed, verified, read-only analysis and the ready-to-apply-but-not-yet-applied DDL, and stops there — same posture as BR-074.

## BR-076 — Final Application Confirmation (2026-07-11)
DDL genuinely applied to the local dev DB, owner-executed directly: 58 missing sequences created (extracted from docs/staging_schema_reference.sql, 3 sequences absent from that dump created with standard defaults matching the file's own convention), 121 missing tables created (table count confirmed via direct query: 1,566 → 1,676), 432 missing columns added across 168 tables (2 column-name parsing bugs found and fixed during application — split identifiers `updated_by`/`wing_code` mangled by a quoting artifact in the original generation, isolated instances, no others found on a full re-scan). Zero genuine errors on final application (all remaining "already exists" messages were expected/idempotent). E2E regression check: 14/14 passed, zero regressions. Phase 1 (schema reconciliation) is now complete.
| BR-078 | 2026-07-11 | **Codebase-wide sweep for the exception-swallowing-defeats-@Transactional-rollback pattern first found in BR-052/059/061/072/073.** Root cause (same as those four): a `catch` in an `@Transactional` method that either returns an ERROR-status `Response`/`CommonResponse` object or only logs, instead of re-throwing — Spring's declarative rollback only fires on an exception actually propagating out of the method, so the DB write(s) already completed earlier in that same method commit even though the method "failed." Scanned every `@Transactional`-annotated method (structural brace-matched, comment-aware) across the whole `legacy/*/src/main/java` tree for this exact catch shape: found 762 candidate catch blocks across 135 files. After excluding 31 that were inside `/* */`-commented-out (non-compiled) code, individually reviewing every remaining shape variant (log-only, empty, multi-catch `HibernateException`+`Exception` chains, sentinel-value returns), and fixing all confirmed genuine instances, **715 catch blocks across 123 files were converted to `throw new ApplicationException(<var>.getMessage(), <var>)`**, matching BR-052/059/061's exact established pattern. Two auto-fix passes handled the bulk mechanically (return-ERROR-Response fallthrough, and log-only/empty catch falling through to a bare `return null;`); each was verified compiler-clean via `mvn -pl <module> -am compile -DskipTests -o` and any newly-unreachable trailing `return` statement (Java definite-reachability rule, same mechanical consequence as BR-006/BR-007/BR-009/BR-010) was deleted only after the compiler itself flagged it as unreachable — never assumed. The remaining ~140 non-mechanical shapes (multi-catch chains, sentinel-value returns like `return "error";`/`return 0;`/`return false;` masquerading as real data, and genuinely ambiguous cases) were each read individually in context and fixed only when the try body contained a real DB write or the return value was consumed as if valid. **Two fixes are flagged for specific owner attention beyond the standard pattern:** `LoanInOutService.approveLoanInOut` and `WitnessApprovalService.updateWitnessApproval` both had a per-item loop where a DB-write failure (batch/stock allocation) was silently logged-and-continued, but the item's own status field was set to "approved"/"closed" a few lines later **unconditionally, regardless of whether the write actually succeeded** — the same partial-completion data-integrity defect class as BR-052's original finding, just discovered inside a loop instead of a single save path. Fixing these (now throws, aborting the whole batch on any single item's failure) is a bigger behavioral change than the other 713 fixes: previously a batch of N items could partially succeed with some items silently mismarked; now one bad item aborts and rolls back the whole batch call. | 123 files under `legacy/{ADT,Billing,Global,Entity,HIS-WAR,Inventory,Procurement}/src/main/java` — full list is the two-commit diff on `fix/exception-swallowing-codebase-sweep` (`cd770f6d`, `4a6f4f8d`); largest concentrations: `UserMasterServiceImpl.java` (45), `EncounterMasterServiceImpl.java` (~45), `AppointmentSchedulingServiceImpl.java` (39), `PatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java` (~32), `UnitServiceMapperServiceImpl.java` (18) | Behavioral Change | Pending | **Deliberately left unfixed, reviewed individually (not a sample — every one of the ~35 remaining live candidates was read in context):** (1) `ScheduledLogsScheduler.syncSchedulingLogs`/`syncPathalogyReport` (14+ sites) and `MessageQueueServiceImpl.getMessageFromQueqe` (5 sites) — per-item best-effort resilience in scheduled external-HTTP-sync/notification-queue jobs; each item's own DB state (`markScheduleLogsCompleted`/`markScheduleLogsError`) is set explicitly based on the real per-item outcome inside the try, so there is no BR-052-class partial-completion risk, and one bad row's HTTP timeout genuinely should not abort every other pending row's sync. (2) `BillingMasterServiceImpl.collectPayment` (2 sites) and `AppointmentVitalMapperDaoImpl.saveAppointmentVitalMapper` (1 site) — this is the exact scenario BR-008 already ruled on for this file: catches around a downstream MyLife-sync push that runs *after* the real save (payment/vitals) already committed successfully; matches BR-008's Option-1/2 precedent exactly (keep the true-success response, only the sync notification can fail) — left exactly as BR-008 specified, no change needed. (3) `GlobalOrganizationMasterServiceImpl.saveOrganizationMaster`/`GlobalUnitMasterServiceImpl.saveUnitMaster` (3 sites) — same downstream-non-critical-sync shape as (2), except the OTIMS/EMR publish calls these particular catches guard are entirely commented out already, so the guarded code cannot currently throw at all; left alone as dead-code-adjacent, flagged for whoever eventually removes the commented publish calls. (4) `CustomSynchEventListener.onApplicationEvent` — a Spring `ApplicationListener`, `void`, writing only to the async sync-log audit table; letting a listener exception propagate risks disrupting the publisher thread for an audit-only side effect, standard defensive pattern for this component type. (5) `Msg91MessageSendNew.msg91Postdata`/`ProductionReleaseEmailNotificationConfigDaoImpl` — SMS/release-notification sends with no DB write in the guarded path. (6) `PaymentRecieptMasterServiceImpl.savePaymentReciept`'s card-amount-validation catch — not `@Transactional` at all (annotation is commented out on this specific method) and the catch is a deliberate `try{throw new Exception();}catch{return validationError;}` idiom used as intentional control flow for a business-rule violation, not an unexpected-failure swallow — exactly the "non-exceptional business rule violation" carve-out this task's own instructions named. (7) `SlotMasterDaoImpl`'s guarded catch is inside a `finally` block's own resource-cleanup (`statement.close()`) — standard, correct pattern, not a swallow. (8) `SlotMasterServiceImpl.getSlotsNew`'s inner catch guards a `SimpleDateFormat.format()` call only (display formatting, no DB write). (9) `UserMasterServiceImpl.createOrganizationFromWeb`'s inner AWS-subdomain-provisioning catch is a deliberate retry-with-alternate-subdomain block, not a swallow. **Root-cause verification incident during this session, documented for the record:** this session's own shared (non-worktree) working directory was live-edited by a concurrent JS null-guard sweep session throughout (see BR-077); a mid-session `git branch` switch by that other session (twice) briefly moved this session's uncommitted Java edits onto the wrong branch pointer, recovered cleanly both times via `git show stash@{0}:<path>`/re-checkout scoped only to this task's own file list, zero JS files touched or lost either time. Separately, and unrelated to the branch-switch incident: a `mvn package` build run partway through this session picked up a **transiently different, uncommitted version** of `PatientRegistrationDaoImpl.java` that existed on disk in the shared working directory for a few minutes (traced via `javap` bytecode/line-number inspection against the deployed jar's actual behavior, which didn't match either this branch's committed source or the current working-tree source) — the resulting jar was deployed and briefly caused a real E2E regression (OPD patient registration returning `status: error`) that was NOT present in this branch's actual committed code. Diagnosed by decompiling the deployed class and comparing its bytecode line-number table against the committed source line-by-line; resolved by confirming the working tree was byte-identical to `HEAD` (`git diff HEAD` empty) and rebuilding+redeploying fresh. **This is the reason to treat "restart+E2E-pass in a shared non-isolated working directory" as necessary but insufficient — verify the actually-deployed jar's checksum/bytecode traces back to the exact source under test, not just "the build succeeded."** Final state: full project compiles clean (`mvn -pl '!HIS-WAR' compile -DskipTests -o`, offline, every module except HIS-WAR, which has the pre-existing unrelated `com.beust.jcommander.internal` dependency gap already tracked as BR-002 and reconfirmed present on a clean pre-session checkout); 14/14 E2E tests pass (two showed single-run flakiness during the full-suite pass — `ehr-growth-chart.spec.js` and `billing-invoice.spec.js`/`vitals-save.spec.js` on different runs — each confirmed passing in isolated re-run, consistent with resource-contention/timing flakiness rather than a code regression, since a different single test flaked on each of 3 consecutive full-suite runs rather than the same one repeatedly). Not merged to master — needs Maker-Checker (RULES.md item 6) and owner sign-off given this touches core save-path exception handling broadly, plus explicit review of the two LoanInOutService/WitnessApprovalService partial-completion behavioral changes flagged above. |

Found and closed a genuine, pre-existing structural master-data gap, same class as BR-046/053/054/067/070/079: the pre-existing `RAD-CXR`/"Chest X-Ray" service row (`service.m_service_master` id=13, present in the DB before this session) had `speciality_id`/`sub_speciality_id` both NULL and no matching rows in `service.t_unit_service_master_details` or `service.m_service_base_price_master` — the Cover Sheet's service-search query (`SEARCH_SERVICES_BY_NAME_AND_CODE`) inner-joins all three, so the service could never appear in search results at all. Fixed (full SQL in `docs/br080_ris_order_seed.sql`, transactional, zero errors): added a new `doctor.m_sub_speciality_master` "Radiology" row (id=3, under the existing "General Medicine" speciality=1, same placement pattern BR-070 used for lab's "Hematology"), linked service_master_id=13 to it, and added the missing `t_unit_service_master_details`/`m_service_base_price_master` rows (base_price=400, matching — not inventing — the rate already present on service 13's pre-existing tariff row). A second, distinct gap surfaced only via live testing after the above fix: the search then worked, but selecting the service and saving triggered a growl "Service is not mapped with the tariff..!!" and silently stopped the order (traced to `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.getSerivceRateGlobal`/`EncounterMasterDaoImpl.getBasePriceByServiceTariffMaster`) — the pre-existing generic tariff row (`unit_service_tariff_id=13`, no `patient_type_id`/`billing_bed_category_id`) does not satisfy that query's exact-match WHERE clause, same gap class BR-070 already found and fixed for CBC (its `unit_service_tariff_id=18`); added a patient-category-specific tariff row (`patient_type_id=3` "Staff", `billing_bed_category_id=1`, matching this project's standard E2E test patient) to close it. This session's own first attempt at that new tariff row omitted `effective_date`, which defaults to NULL (no column default) — the same query's `to_char(effective_date,'YYYY-MM-DD') <= :effectiveDate` filter never matches a NULL, so the row silently failed to match until traced live and corrected (`docs/br080_ris_order_seed.sql` includes the corrected single-pass version). **Separately found and reported, NOT fixed (out of this session's scope):** a genuine, deterministic, pre-existing app-level JS defect, same class as BR-065/066/069 (`OPDCoverSheetController.js`). The controller's `getVitalList` success handler (~line 9380) unconditionally calls `$scope.setForthTabLevel(12)` on every Cover Sheet page load, which calls `$scope.onClick()` (~line 7234); `onClick()` reads `$scope.patientViatalList.length` with no null guard, but `$scope.patientViatalList` is only ever assigned inside several *separate* vitals-graph API success callbacks elsewhere in the same controller — for a freshly registered patient with no vitals history yet (i.e., any brand-new patient, not RIS-specific), that assignment never fires before `onClick()` runs, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')` on every single page load. Reproduced deterministically on all 5 live runs during this session (2 investigation runs + 3 required consecutive-pass verification runs) — not flaky. Filtered as known/documented noise in the new test's console-error assertion (matching this suite's existing precedent for other OPDCoverSheetController scope errors), and reported here for owner/dev triage rather than fixed, since a Cover Sheet controller behavior change belongs to a dedicated JS-bugfix branch, not this master-data-and-test branch. **Verification:** ran the new test 3 consecutive times, all passed; independently confirmed via direct `psql` that each run produced a real, correctly-shaped `public.t_order_details` row (`is_investigation_order='Y'`, `service_id=13`, `status='A'`, `net_amount=400.00`) — order_details_id 631 (initial investigation run), 637, then 640/643/646 (the 3 required consecutive verification runs). USG Abdomen (`service_master_id=14`) has the identical structural gap (also NULL speciality/sub-speciality, no detail/price rows, and its own pre-existing tariff row has `visit_type_id=2` and no patient/bed-category match either) — flagged, deliberately **not** fixed, out of scope for this single-test task. | `docs/br080_ris_order_seed.sql` (new); `doctor.m_sub_speciality_master` (1 new row), `service.m_service_master` (1 row updated: `speciality_id`/`sub_speciality_id`), `service.t_unit_service_master_details`/`m_service_base_price_master`/`m_unit_service_tariff_master` (1 new row each, all `service_master_id`=13) — all `his-local-postgres`/`HIS-SME`, local dev only; `~/Projects/his-e2e-tests/tests/e2e/ris-radiology-order.spec.js` (new); reported-not-fixed: `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/opd/transection/OPDCoverSheetController.js` (~lines 7225-7234, 2880-2897, 9370-9382) | Neither (Discovery-plus-mechanical-seed for the master-data fix, same discipline as BR-079 — no schema or clinical/business-judgment value invented; Pre-existing Defect for the reported-not-fixed JS bug, same class as BR-002/BR-065/BR-066) | Pending | Follows Expand-only discipline (RULES.md item 1) — every DB change is additive, nothing altered or dropped, no column type/name changed. No new business terms invented, no `docs/GLOSSARY.json` addition required. This is a Phase 3 (data) + E2E-test task; the reported `OPDCoverSheetController.js` bug is a Phase-independent JS defect recommended for a future dedicated null-guard/bugfix session (natural continuation of BR-077's sessionStorage/localStorage sweep, but for plain in-memory `$scope` variables instead of Web Storage). |
| BR-081 | 2026-07-11 | Not a business rule — built the first genuinely working Blood Bank E2E write-operation test: registers a New Donor from the Donor Registration screen (`#/donorRegistration`), the module's simplest self-contained write flow (no precondition chain on an existing patient/encounter/order, unlike the Blood Bag Issue/Transfusion Acceptance flow investigated and set aside as a second candidate — that flow requires an already-issued blood bag to exist first, itself dependent on donor screening → bag collection → component separation → issue, out of scope for one test). Also discovered (documented in the test, not a blocker): the Local Address country/state/district/city/area `<select>`s are pre-populated at page load to the logged-in unit's own default location — since the test's target address matches that default, reselecting the same option fires no browser `change` event, so `setPostCode()` (which auto-fills Zip Code from the selected area) never runs; the test fills Zip Code directly instead of relying on that auto-population, which a real user would also have to do in this exact common case (own-unit-address donor). **Separately found and reported, NOT fixed (out of this session's scope):** a genuine, deterministic, pre-existing app-level JS defect, same class as BR-065/066/069/080. `donorRegistrationController.js`'s `getDefaultRaceList` success handler (~line 495) unconditionally reads `response.data.listObject[0].raceId` with no null/length guard. `RaceMasterDaoImpl.getDefaultRaceList`'s `GET_DEFAULT_RACE_LIST` named query filters `raceMaster.isDefault='A'`, but `m_race_master.is_default`'s actual value domain is `Y`/`N` (confirmed live: the one seeded race row has `is_default='N'`) — so the query can never match any row regardless of what's seeded, `listObject` is always empty, and this throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'raceId')` on every single Donor Registration page load. Reproduced deterministically on every run this session. Doesn't block the save flow (`raceId` isn't required by `storeDonorDetails()`'s own validation and isn't even rendered as a field on this view) — filtered as documented noise in the new test's console-error assertion, reported here for a future dedicated JS-bugfix session rather than fixed, since closing it needs either a query-condition code fix or an owner decision on which race is "default" (a business/data judgment), both out of scope for this master-data-and-test branch. **Verification:** ran the new test 5 times total across this session (2 back-to-back consecutive pairs, both 100% passing — flaky `page.goto` timeouts seen between some runs were traced to shared-machine resource contention, `load average` 34+ on a 16-core box from other concurrent sessions' work, confirmed unrelated to this test/flow via a direct `curl` health-check showing the app responding in 127ms during one such failure); independently confirmed via direct `psql` that every run produced a real, correctly-shaped `blood_bank.t_donor_registration` + `t_donor_details` row pair (`donor_number` sequentially `DN-1` through `DN-9`, `blood_group_id=1`, `donor_type_id=1`, `zip_code='411005'`, full local+permanent address hierarchy `1/1/1/1/1`) — `donor_id` 3–11 (plus the pre-fix investigation row, `donor_id=2`, deliberately left in place as evidence of the bug this session found and fixed, matching BR-080's precedent of leaving investigation-run rows undeleted). | `docs/br081_donor_registration_seed.sql` (new);
