## BR-084 — Inventory Store Indent E2E Test (2026-07-11)
Built a working E2E test for the Inventory Store Indent flow (Pharmacy Main Store → CSSD Store, reusing BR-082's precedent of pairing existing seeded stores where a true second store doesn't exist in this dev DB). Investigated `addNewStoreIndenting.js` live. Found and fixed 3 genuine master-data gaps via `docs/br084_store_indent_seed.sql` (Expand-only, additive): (1) `m_store_item_mapper` had zero rows for the test item in either store — same class of gap as BR-082; (2) `m_unit_of_measurement` had zero rows for the item, causing a client-side array-index crash in `AddItems()` — seeded the minimal structurally-correct 3-row UOM set (Purchase/Stock/Dispense, all mapped to the only existing UOM unit, 1:1 conversion, no fabricated packaging ratio); (3) `m_prefix_configuration` had zero rows for screen_id=2 (Store Indent), same discovery class as BR-081's donor-number gap — seeded one row. Genuine bug found and reported, NOT fixed: the UOM-array crash pattern (an unguarded `item.uomListdata[2].uomUnit` read) is a real, reproducible client-side defect independent of the data gap — worth a dedicated future null-guard fix, same class as the BR-056/077 sweep. Verified: 2 consecutive passing runs (owner-independently re-verified, indent ids 6, 7), each with a real `Store indent saved successfully` response.

## BR-085 — Kitchen/Dietary Diet Planning E2E Test (2026-07-11)
Built a working E2E test for the Kitchen/Dietary Diet Planning flow. Investigated `dietPatientRequestTransactionController.js` live. Found and fixed 5 genuine gaps via `docs/br085_diet_planning_seed.sql` (Expand-only, additive): (1) `adt.m_ward_master.is_ip_ward` was 'N' for every ward in the dev DB, blocking the Diet Order Patient List's IPD-only filter from ever matching a real inpatient ward — a structural data correction (all 5 existing wards are legitimately named inpatient wards, none ER/day-care-only), not a business-judgment call; (2) `adt.m_meal_preference_master` was completely empty, blocking the required "Diet Preference" dropdown — seeded one generic 'General' preference row; (3) `kitchen.m_diet_template` was empty (BR-079 deliberately left this for dietitian input) — seeded the structural container row only (diet_type + org/unit), no menu content; (4) linked the new template to the new meal preference; (5) seeded exactly 2 generic, structurally-necessary dish names ('Rice', 'Dal', no nutrition/value fields fabricated, same discipline as BR-079) and mapped both to all 3 existing meals. Verified: 2 consecutive passing runs (owner-independently re-verified).
## BR-086 — Linen & Laundry Linen Indent E2E Test (2026-07-11)
Phase 3 (BR-079) seeded Linen & Laundry with only a single new `m_store` row ("Linen Store"), no dedicated Linen master tables otherwise; this module's real structure hadn't been explored in any prior session. Investigated live: `add-new-linen-indent.html` / `addNewLinenController.js` (state `addNewLinenIndent`) / `LinenIndentController.java` / `LinenIndentService.java` / `LinenIndentDao.java` — a store-to-store indent flow, same shape as BR-082/084's precedent (Requesting Store → Issuing Store, add item(s), quantity, save). Built a working E2E test, `tests/e2e/linen-indent.spec.js` in the sibling `his-e2e-tests` repo.

**Master-data gap found and fixed** (`docs/br086_linen_indent_seed.sql`, Expand-only, additive, both inserts independently psql-verified): both `GlobalDao.getLinenUserStoreList` (fromStore/Requesting Store) and `GlobalDao.getLinenStoreList` (toStore/Issuing Store) filter `store.storeType.id = 5` ("Linen Store" type) — i.e. **both** sides of a Linen Indent must be Linen-type stores, not just one side as in some other modules. With only one such store existing (BR-079's "Linen Store", id=3), fromStore and toStore would always resolve to the same store, which the client's `validateSave()` explicitly blocks ("Please Select Different Stores"). Seeded a second Linen-type store, "Central Laundry Store" (id=6), plus a `m_user_store_mapper` row mapping admin (user_id=1) to the existing "Linen Store" (id=3) — the same class of gap as BR-053/081/082's per-module store-mapper findings. No item-side seeding was needed: item_id=3 (BR-082's "Sample Medical Equipment Item") already has `m_store_item_mapper` rows from BR-082, sufficient for this module's item-search query (which filters by unit_id only, storeId is never sent).

**Genuine bugs found live, reported, NOT fixed** (out of this session's scope — data/test-writing only, not application-code fixes):
1. `addNewLinenController.js`'s `searchNewItem()` hardcodes `assetTypeId: 2` ("Fixed") in its POST body regardless of module — apparently copy-pasted from the Assets Indent controller (same "Asset Category"/"Product Category" modal labels, verbatim). This means the Linen Indent "Add Item" search can only ever surface Fixed/Equipment-type items, never anything conceptually "linen" — there is no linen-specific item master anywhere in this dev DB at all. Structural, not just a data gap.
2. The same controller's `GetAssetCategoryList()` separately hardcodes `assetTypeId: 1` (`ASSET_CATEGORY_API + 1 + ...`) for populating the "Asset Category" filter dropdown shown in that same modal — inconsistent with finding (1)'s `assetTypeId: 2`, so the category dropdown always lists type-1 categories ("Pharmaceutical") that can never match what the search itself actually returns. Currently silent (the default "Select Asset Category" bypasses the filter), but the dropdown could never be used correctly as-is.
3. `addNewLinenController.js`'s `PopulateDropdown()` never auto-selects the Requesting Store (fromStore) to the logged-in user's own mapped store, unlike its sibling Asset Indent/Store Indent controllers, which both do `self.model.fromStore = self.model.fromStores.find(store => $scope.storeId == store.id)` after the dropdown response resolves. Confirmed live via Playwright: fromStore stays at the placeholder "Select Store" option after page load. Worked around in the test by explicitly selecting it (the field itself isn't disabled for a new indent), matching what a real user would also have to do.
4. Combined with (3): `ItemsDaoImpl.itemsDetailsFixedAsset`/`itemsDetails` call `query.setParameter("storeId", storeId)` with no explicit Hibernate type. When `storeId` is null (reachable whenever a user clicks "Add Item" without first manually picking a Requesting Store, per finding 3), Hibernate mis-infers the bind type and Postgres throws `ERROR: operator does not exist: integer = bytea`, a live HTTP 500 confirmed via `docker logs his-local-tomcat`. This is a shared DAO method also used by other item-picker modals, not Linen-specific, so likely a broader latent risk.
5. `LinenIndentService.saveLinenIndent()` (line 101) hardcodes `indentNo` to the literal string `"IND-001"` for **every** indent ever created, unlike sibling flows (Store Indent/Asset Indent/LPR/Donor Registration, BR-081/083/084 precedent) which generate a real sequential number via `m_prefix_configuration`. Confirmed live via psql: all 3 test runs produced rows with `indent_no='IND-001'` (ids 1, 2, 3). The live `linen_and_laundry.t_linen_indent.indent_no` column has no unique constraint (nullable, unconstrained) despite the JPA entity's `@Column(unique = true, nullable = false)` annotation — itself a DDL-vs-annotation mismatch, same class as BR-076's broader reconciliation findings — so this doesn't currently crash, but produces meaningless, duplicate indent numbers in production. Needs owner/product review; not something to silently generate a fix for.

**Verified:** 3 consecutive passing runs (indent ids 1, 2, 3), each with a real `Linen indent saved successfully` response and a non-zero id; independently re-verified via direct `psql` against `linen_and_laundry.t_linen_indent`/`t_linen_indent_detail` (from_store_id=3, to_store_id=6, item_id=3, quantity=3, matching the test's inputs for all 3 rows).

## BR-087 — OTIMS (Operation Theatre) Investigation — No Test Written This Session (2026-07-11)

Not a business rule and no application code or test written — this session was explicitly investigation-only, per owner instruction, ahead of building OTIMS's first E2E test in a future session. OTIMS was flagged out of scope by BR-079 (its schema was only listed, not seeded) and is the owner's stated top remaining priority module. Worked in an isolated git worktree (`investigate/otims` branch, not merged to master).

**Module structure found (three-tier split, consistent across the codebase's other modules but wider here):**
- `legacy/OTIMS/` — a *dedicated standalone Maven module* (own `pom.xml`), not folded into `Global`/`HIS-WAR` like every other module tested tonight. Holds the core workflow controllers: `OTReservationController` (case scheduling/booking), `PreOperativeDetailsController`, `IntraOperativeDetailsController`, `PostOperativeDetailsController`, `PostOperativeCareController`, `OTIMSDashboardController`, `StaffSchedulingController`, `EquipmentSchedulingController`, `ProcedureTemplateController`, plus checklist/notes controllers.
- `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/otims/` — master-data and secondary controllers: `OperationTheatreController`/`OperationTheatreTypeController`, `OperationTableController`/`OperationTableTypeController`, `ProcedureRoleMasterController`, `SurgicalCalendarMasterController`, `ResourceChargeConfigurationController`, `SubSequentProcedureConfigController`, `SurgeryResourceConfigController`, plus OT-completion-detail sub-controllers (implants, medication, checklist, notes, transfer request).
- `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/views/master/TheatreManagement/` — the **real** UI screen location (not `GlobalMaster/OTIMS/`, which is masters-only config). 37 view files, the largest three being `post.html` (177KB — OT Completion), `details-OT-billing-completion.html` (172KB), `New-OT-reservation.html` (159KB). Matching JS controllers under `resources/assets/js/controller/otims/` and `.../otims/global/`.

**Distinct screens/workflows found under TheatreManagement:** OT Manager Dashboard, OT Reservation (list + New OT Reservation booking), OT Reservation Cancellation, Pre-Operative, Intra-Operative, Post (OT Completion), Post-Operative Care (Recovery), OT Completion detail view, OT Billing Completion (list + detail), OT Checklist (+ details), Surgery Note, Anesthesia Note, Bill of Material (+ master), Resource Configuration, Subsequent Surgery Configuration, Procedure Configuration, Appointment Template Master, plus a full Implant Indent/Implant Return/Consignment sub-flow (patient-level implant issue/return/consignment tracking — a distinct inventory-adjacent slice, not part of the core surgery pipeline).

**Real stage sequence, confirmed via AngularJS `$stateProvider` param-chaining (`resources/assets/js/config/app.js`), not assumed:**
1. **`OTManagerDashboard`** (`/OTManagerDashboard`) — landing/worklist view.
2. **`NewOTreservation`** (`/NewOTreservation`, controller `NewOTreservationController.js`, **14,119 lines** — by far the largest single controller found across all 19 modules examined tonight, with entire multi-thousand-line function blocks duplicated verbatim 3× in the same file for what appear to be new/edit/view variants) — schedules an OT case: picks patient, procedure/service (loads a `ProcedureConfiguration` template's default checklist/consent/staff/equipment/service list), OT room + table, date/time, staff. Saves via `POST /api/otims/OTReservation/saveOTReservation` (`legacy/OTIMS/.../OTReservationController.java`), which in one call cascades writes across `otims.t_ot_reservation`, `otims.t_procedure_req_mst`, `otims.t_procedure_order_master`, and — per `OTReservationServiceImpl`'s injected DAOs — `t_procedure_order_consent`, `t_procedure_order_service`, `t_procedure_order_checklist`, `t_procedure_order_patient_education`, `t_procedure_order_staff`, `t_procedure_order_equipment`, `t_medication_details`, `t_implant_details` (9+ tables in one transaction). Local `try/catch` here correctly rethrows via `ServiceException`, not a silent swallow.
3. **`preOperative`** (`/preOperative`, controller **1,228 lines** — the smallest of the 5 stage controllers) — **hard dependency confirmed in code**: reads `$stateParams.resultCommonObj.proReqId` and calls `GET /api/otims/PreOperativeDetails/getProcedureOrderIdByProReqId` before it can do anything; cannot be entered standalone without an already-scheduled case. Saves via `POST /api/otims/PreOperativeDetails/savePreOperativeDetails` into `otims.t_pre_operative_details`, and forwards `resultCommonObj` to the next state.
4. **`intraOperative`** (`/intraOperative`, controller **1,501 lines**) — receives `intraCommonObj`/`intraDetailsObj`/`resultCommonObj` as forwarded state params; writes `otims.t_intra_operative_details` + `t_intra_operative_image`.
5. **`post`** (`/post`, controller **7,916 lines** — the OT Completion screen, matching `post.html`'s 177KB) — receives everything from steps 2–4 plus `postCommonObj`/`postDetailsObj`; this is where staff/equipment/consumables/implants/medication/medical-gas/CSSD-kit actually used in surgery get recorded, cascading into `otims.t_ot_completion_*` (9 sub-tables: staff, equipment, time_sheet, inv_consumable, inv_implants, medical_gas, note, operation_theatre, cssd_kit).
6. **`postOperativeCare`** (`/postOperativeCare`, controller **5,291 lines**) — Recovery Care, receives the full param chain plus `postCareCommonObj`/`postCareDetailsObj`; writes `otims.t_post_operative_care` + `t_post_operative_details`.
7. **`otBillingCompletionDetails`** (controller **7,027 lines**, matching `details-OT-billing-completion.html`'s 172KB) — a separate finalization step (not chained via state params like 3–6), reached from the OT Completion/Billing worklist once a case is closed out. `otims.t_procedure_req_mst` itself carries `ot_completion_date_time`/`ot_bill_completion_date_time` columns, confirming billing completion is tracked as a distinct terminal state of the same request row, not a separate entity.

So the real sequence matches the task's generic guess (schedule → pre-op → intra-op → post-op/recovery → billing) but with two corrections: (a) "post-op" in this codebase is actually **two** distinct stages — OT Completion (`post`, surgical resource reconciliation) then Recovery Care (`postOperativeCare`) — and (b) OT Billing Completion is a terminal status on the *same* `t_procedure_req_mst` row, not a separate downstream entity requiring its own new master data.

**Master-data completeness — confirmed via direct `psql` against `his-local-postgres`/`HIS-SME` (schema `otims`, 70 tables, all real row counts, not estimated):** Exactly as BR-079 predicted ("expect it to be largely or entirely unseeded") — **all 16 `m_`-prefixed master tables are empty except `m_procedure_role_master` (4 rows: Surgeon/Ass. Surgeon/Visiting doctor/Anaesthetist — no explicit "OT Nurse" role, worth flagging for whoever seeds staff roles next), and all 54 `t_`-prefixed transaction tables are completely empty (0 rows).** Specifically for the recommended first-slice tables: `m_operation_theatre_type` (0), `m_operation_theatre` (0, and its `theatre_type_id` FK means it depends on the type master first), `m_operation_table_type` (0), `m_operation_table` (0, depends on both `m_operation_theatre` and its own type master — a 4-level seeding chain just for one physical OT room+table, same "chain of gaps" shape as BR-082/083), `m_anesthesia_type_master` (0), `m_checklist_master`/`m_checklist_item_master` (0), `m_procedure_type_master` (0), `m_procedure_order_status` (0). `otims.t_procedure_req_mst` additionally has hard FKs to `adt.t_admission`, `opd.t_encounter_master`, `patient.t_patient_registration`, and `m_visit_type_master` — confirming an OT case must be raised against an *existing* patient encounter/admission, not a bare patient. Encouragingly, that prerequisite data already exists in volume from prior sessions' testing: 595 patients, 266 encounters, 128 admissions — so, unlike the OT-specific masters, this dependency needs no new seeding, only picking an existing reusable one (same reuse pattern as BR-053/081/082's patient/store reuse). No `otims`-specific `m_prefix_configuration`/screen_id numbering call was found in `OTReservationServiceImpl`/`OTReservationDaoImpl` (unlike every indent-style module tested tonight, BR-081/083/084/086) — case identifiers appear to be plain serial PKs (`ot_reservation_id`, `pro_req_id`, `procedure_order_id`), not formatted document numbers, so that particular recurring gap class may not apply here; needs re-confirming once a save is actually attempted.

**Recommendation — most tractable first slice: OT Case Scheduling only (`NewOTreservation` → `saveOTReservation`), *not* the full pipeline.** Reasoning, by the same judgment BR-079/LIS used for CBC order-to-result:
- It is the one stage every later stage hard-depends on (`preOperative` cannot even load without a real `proReqId`), so it must be built first regardless of how far a future session wants to go.
- Its own controller is the largest single file found across all 19 modules (14K lines) and its save cascades across 9+ tables — this is **already** a bigger single first slice than any other module's "first working E2E test" tonight (all of which were single- or dual-table indent/save flows), so scope discipline for the *first* test should stop exactly at "reservation saved successfully with a real `pro_req_id`/`procedure_order_id`," not attempt to also drive Pre-Operative in the same test.
- `preOperative` (1,228 lines, smallest stage controller) is the natural **second** slice for a follow-up session once scheduling works, giving a genuine two-stage "book a case → complete its pre-op checklist" pipeline without touching the two enormous 7,000+-line Completion/Billing screens, which should be deferred further still.
- Explicitly do **not** start with `post`/`otBillingCompletionDetails` (post.html/OT-billing-completion.html) — at 7,000+ lines and 170KB+ each, both dwarf every controller/view this project has built a first test against so far (the next-largest, `postOprativeCareController.js`, is itself 5,291 lines) and should only be attempted after scheduling + pre-op are proven stable, matching CLAUDE.md's "prove it with tests before going further" discipline.
- Before any test can be written, the same class of chained master-data seeding done in BR-082/083 will be needed: `m_operation_theatre_type` → `m_operation_theatre` → `m_operation_table_type` → `m_operation_table` (minimum one real OT room+table), plus `m_anesthesia_type_master`, `m_procedure_type_master`/`m_procedure_order_status`, and at least one `t_procedure_configuration` template (with its `t_procedure_config_*` child rows) for the service the test books — none of which exist today. `m_procedure_role_master` is already usable as-is (4 rows).

| `legacy/OTIMS/`, `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/otims/`, `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/views/master/TheatreManagement/`, `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/otims/` (docs-only investigation, no files under these paths modified) | Neither (investigation/documentation only, no schema, data, or code change of any kind — explicit "no seeding, no test" instruction for this session) | Pending | Not merged to master (branch `investigate/otims`, isolated worktree per this session's own instruction). Recommends a follow-up session scope itself to OT Case Scheduling alone (not the full pipeline) as the first E2E test, with Pre-Operative as the natural second slice once scheduling is proven. Flags the missing explicit "OT Nurse" role in `m_procedure_role_master` and the 4-level OT-room/table master seeding chain for whoever picks this up next. |

## BR-088 — Correlation-ID error response on Growth Chart patient search failure (2026-07-13, revised 2026-07-13)

**Root cause (owner-confirmed):** `patientSearchByMultipleCriteriaGrowthChart`'s three layers (DAO/Service/Controller) each follow the same structural defect — the success-path `return` sits inside a `try`, each `catch` block logs (or, at the Controller layer, doesn't even log) the real exception, and then an unconditional `return new Response(ERROR, ...)` sits *after* the try/catch, always carrying a hardcoded generic message (`COMMON_ERROR_MESSAGE` or `null`) regardless of what actually failed. The real exception is logged server-side at the DAO/Service layers (never at the Controller layer, which had a fully empty `catch`), but the client always receives the same generic `"Something went wrong!!"`-class message.

**Revised fix (2026-07-13 — supersedes this entry's original approach, same session's branch, not yet merged):** the original fix (below, kept for history) surfaced the raw `he.getMessage()`/`e.getMessage()` to the HTTP client, which this entry itself flagged as a leak risk in tension with the already-approved BR-003/BR-005 (`GlobalExceptionHandler` deliberately keeps its outer-boundary message generic for the same reason). The owner decided against that approach. **Current fix:** each `catch` block now generates a short correlation ID (`ErrorCorrelationUtil.newCorrelationId()`, an 8-hex-char UUID fragment prefixed `ERR-`), logs the real exception server-side tagged with that ID (`logger.error("[ERR-xxxxxxxx] patientSearchByMultipleCriteriaGrowthChart failed", e)`), and returns a generic client-facing message that carries only the ID (`"Something went wrong. Reference: ERR-xxxxxxxx"`, via `ErrorCorrelationUtil.clientMessage(...)`) — never the raw exception text. `ErrorCorrelationUtil` is a new shared static helper (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/utility/ErrorCorrelationUtil.java`), reachable from Global, ADT, and HIS-WAR (all three declare a Maven dependency on Global), used consistently by this entry, the ER-Discharge entry below, and the new GlobalExceptionHandler entry. No `try/catch` removed, no `logger.error` call removed, no HTTP status/response-shape change beyond the message string. Also added a class-level `logger` (`org.apache.log4j.Logger`, matching this codebase's established convention, e.g. `BillingBedCategoryMasterController`) to `UnknownPatientRegistrationController`, which had no logger declared at all — its `catch` block was previously completely empty (no log call whatsoever), which independently violates RULES.md's "no error may be silently swallowed, ever, anywhere."

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/utility/ErrorCorrelationUtil.java` (new shared helper)
- `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/UnknownPatientRegistrationDaoImpl.java` (`patientSearchByMultipleCriteriaGrowthChart`, DAO layer — the originally confirmed root-cause site)
- `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/service/UnknownPatientRegistrationServiceImpl.java` (same method, Service layer — same defect, one layer up)
- `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/ER/controller/UnknownPatientRegistrationController.java` (same method, Controller layer — same defect, plus a missing logger)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** (the `message` field's content differs on the error path for callers who inspect it — output is not byte-identical, per RULES.md's definition, even though status/code/flow are unchanged; and the message content itself changed again with this revision, from raw exception text to a generic message + correlation ID). Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)**.

**Notes:** Verified via `mvn -pl Global -am compile -DskipTests -o` and `mvn -pl ADT -am compile -DskipTests -o` (run from `legacy/`): both BUILD SUCCESS. Per RULES.md item 8, this uses a provisional ID for the life of this branch (`fix/backend-error-handling-detailed`, isolated worktree, not merged); the real next sequential `BR-XXX` number will be assigned in the commit immediately preceding merge. The security-leak concern originally flagged in this entry (raw `e.getMessage()` reaching the client) is resolved by this revision — see also the new GlobalExceptionHandler entry below, which applies the same correlation-ID pattern to the centralized handler's own generic-error branches for consistency.

## BR-089 — Correlation-ID error response on ER/IPD Discharge save failure (2026-07-13, revised 2026-07-13)

**Root cause (same defect class as BR-088):** `saveDischargeRequest`'s three layers (DAO/Service/Controller) all had the identical unconditional-generic-return-after-try/catch shape. The DAO layer (`DueForDischargeDaoImpl`) was the most severe instance found this session: both `catch` blocks (`HibernateException` and `Exception`) were **completely empty** — no `logger.error` call at all, meaning a failed discharge save (which can involve up to 3 DB writes: the discharge row, an admission-status update, and a conditional mortuary-request insert) was previously invisible both to the client *and* to server logs. None of the three files in this call chain (`DueForDischargeDaoImpl`, `DueForDischargeServiceImpl`, `DueForDischargeController`) had a `logger` declared anywhere in the class.

**Revised fix (2026-07-13 — supersedes this entry's original approach, same session's branch, not yet merged):** the original fix (below, kept for history) surfaced the raw `he.getMessage()`/`e.getMessage()` to the HTTP client, which this entry itself flagged as a leak risk. The owner decided against that approach in favor of the same correlation-ID pattern described in the Growth Chart entry above: each `catch` block generates a correlation ID via the new shared `ErrorCorrelationUtil` helper, logs the real exception server-side tagged with that ID, and returns only a generic message plus the ID to the client (e.g. `"Something went wrong. Reference: ERR-a3f9c1"`) — never the raw exception text. The class-level `logger` additions (`org.apache.log4j.Logger`, same codebase convention) to all three classes remain as before, since none had one; no `try/catch` removed, no HTTP status/response-shape change beyond the message string.

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/utility/ErrorCorrelationUtil.java` (new shared helper, same one used by the Growth Chart entry above)
- `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/discharge/dao/DueForDischargeDaoImpl.java` (`saveDischargeRequest`, DAO layer — previously fully silent, no logging at all)
- `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/discharge/service/DueForDischargeServiceImpl.java` (same method, Service layer)
- `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/discharge/controller/DueForDischargeController.java` (same method, Controller layer)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** (message-content-only, same reasoning as the Growth Chart entry above; status codes and control flow unchanged; message content changed again with this revision, from raw exception text to generic message + correlation ID). Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)**.

**Notes:** Verified via `mvn -pl ADT -am compile -DskipTests -o` (run from `legacy/`): BUILD SUCCESS. Per RULES.md item 8, provisional ID for the life of this branch (`fix/backend-error-handling-detailed`); real `BR-XXX` number assigned immediately before merge. The security-disclosure concern originally flagged in this entry (and in the Growth Chart entry above) is resolved by this revision — see also the new GlobalExceptionHandler entry below. **Scope note:** the task that produced this fix cited "BR-098" (CSSD `saveCleanedWorklist`) and "BR-091" (a Pharmacy/Billing `/api/billing/unit/bill/Report` receipt-PDF path) as two further priority targets; both were investigated this session and found to **already** correctly propagate `e.getMessage()` via a thrown `ServiceException`/`BillingException` at every layer (DAO/Service/Controller) — no fix was needed or made for either. Additionally, no entries numbered BR-088 through BR-102 exist anywhere in this manifest or `docs/BR_INDEX.md` despite the prior commit `14568290` ("Baseline: manual QA complete (TC-001–017), BR-088–102 logged, pre-upgrade state") claiming they were logged — that commit in fact only touched a stray `FETCH_HEAD` file. Flagging this discrepancy for owner awareness rather than guessing at what BR-091/097/098/102 were meant to contain.

**Broader scope discovery (flagged, not fixed — recommend a dedicated future sweep session, same precedent as BR-078's multi-part codebase sweep):** a structural scan of the exact defect pattern (try/catch with an unconditional generic `return new Response(...)` immediately after, where no `catch` returns or throws) across `legacy/CSSDMaster`, `legacy/Global`, `legacy/Billing`, `legacy/Pharmacy`, and `legacy/ADT` found **6,181 occurrences across 845 files** (0 in CSSDMaster, 0 in Pharmacy, 4,315 in Global, 254 in Billing, 1,612 in ADT) — roughly 9× the scale of BR-078's own historic 715-catch/123-file sweep, which itself took multiple commits/sessions to complete. This is far beyond what one no-subagent session can safely verify occurrence-by-occurrence (live-call-path tracing + per-file compile check + individual message-quality review, per this session's own instructions) without a real risk of mechanical, unreviewed changes. Only the two named, explicitly-prioritized call chains above (and the two investigated-and-found-clean chains, CSSD/Pharmacy-Billing) were fixed this session. A reasonably accurate character-level scanner script is committed at `scripts/find_swallowed_return_pattern2.py` on this branch, to save a future sweep session the trial-and-error this session went through building it — use this one, not `scripts/find_swallowed_return_pattern.py` (an earlier line-based heuristic attempt, kept in place only for transparency, that undercounted by roughly two orders of magnitude because it mishandles this codebase's dominant `"} catch (...) {"`-on-one-line style; it missed both of the confirmed-live occurrences fixed in this and the entry above before the character-level rewrite caught them). | `scripts/find_swallowed_return_pattern.py`, `scripts/find_swallowed_return_pattern2.py` (new, diagnostic tooling only, not part of the fix) | Neither (tooling/discovery, no application code) | Pending | Recommend the owner scope a dedicated sweep session for the 6,181-occurrence finding before any session attempts it, given its scale relative to BR-078's precedent. |

## BR-090 — GlobalExceptionHandler generic-error branches now return a correlation ID (2026-07-13)

**Context:** BR-003 and BR-005 (both Approved) already established that `GlobalExceptionHandler.handleAllExceptions`'s outer catch and "Broken pipe" branch should log the real exception and return a generic HTTP 500 body, rather than silently returning null. Those entries did not need to address message *content* leak risk because both branches already used a hardcoded constant (`ERR_500_MESSAGE`, "Internal Server Error"). Separately, the two entries above (Growth Chart, ER Discharge) originally surfaced raw `e.getMessage()` to the client, which the owner has now rejected in favor of a single consistent policy across the whole codebase.

**Fix:** applied the same correlation-ID pattern used in the two revised entries above to every genuinely-unexpected-error branch in `handleAllExceptions`: the outer catch (`Unhandled error in centralized exception handler`), the "Broken pipe"/aborted-connection branch, and the final `else` branch (the true catch-all, which returns either an `ErrorResponse("HIS-1001", ...)` or a generic `Response(...ERR_500...)` depending on whether the matched handler method itself returns a `ResponseEntity`). Each of these three branches now generates its own correlation ID via `ErrorCorrelationUtil.newCorrelationId()`, logs the real exception tagged with that ID (replacing the previous `logger.info("exception log consider", ex.getStackTrace())` call — which passed a `Throwable[]` as an SLF4J format arg rather than the exception itself — with a proper `logger.error(tag, ex)` call that actually captures the stack trace), and returns the client-facing generic message plus the ID via `ErrorCorrelationUtil.clientMessage(...)` in place of the hardcoded `ERR_500_MESSAGE`/`"Error while processing data"` strings. The existing `backLogService.createErrorLog(...)` persistence call in the `else` branch is untouched. **Explicitly NOT touched:** the two Bad Request (400) branches (`MethodArgumentNotValidException`/`HttpMessageNotReadableException`, `InvalidFormatException`) and the two Method Not Allowed (405) branches (`HttpRequestMethodNotSupportedException`, and the separate `handleMethodNotAllowed(TypeMismatchException)` method) — these are legitimately generic because they describe a malformed/unsupported *request*, not a server-side failure, so there is no real exception detail being withheld from the client and no case for a correlation ID.

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/java/com/param/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java` (`handleAllExceptions`: outer catch, broken-pipe branch, final `else` branch)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** (the response body's `message` field changes from a fixed string to a fixed string + a per-request correlation ID; HTTP status codes, response shape, and control flow are unchanged). Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)** — this is a further refinement of the already-Approved BR-003/BR-005 decisions, not a reversal of them; both branches keep the "log real error, return generic body" behavior BR-003/BR-005 established, only the exact generic string changes.

**Notes:** Could not get a clean isolated compile signal for HIS-WAR itself in this environment: `mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am compile -DskipTests -o` fails, but with the exact same errors (all in `ContentCachingRequestWrapper.java`, an unrelated file this session never touched, missing the `com.beust.jcommander.internal` dependency) as a build attempted against this branch's pre-change baseline (commit `d7b58e33`, verified via `git stash`/rebuild/`stash pop` — byte-identical error log) — this is the pre-existing, already-logged **BR-002** defect, not a regression introduced here. No compiler error in either run references `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` or the new `ErrorCorrelationUtil.java`, which is the strongest signal available in this environment that this change itself is syntactically and type-correct. Recommend the owner treat BR-002's resolution as a prerequisite for a genuine HIS-WAR build-verification signal on this or any future HIS-WAR change.
## BR-091 — OPD Cover Sheet crash on incomplete appointment data (2026-07-13)

**Context:** Confirmed live via browser console (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'split')`): `$scope.checkAppointment` in `OPDCoverSheetController.js` crashes whenever the active encounter lacks a full slot-based appointment record. Root cause confirmed as stated in the session task: `$scope.patientDetails.birthDate` (itself copied from `$scope.encounterDetailsObj.dob`) can be `undefined`/empty for such encounters, and line 2081 called `.split('/')` on it unconditionally, with a second chained `.split('')` immediately after on the same derived value (line 2083) that would also have thrown once the first guard was added.

**Fix:** wrapped both `.split()` calls (birth-date parsing → `$scope.birthYear`/`$scope.birthMonth`/`$scope.YearDifference`) in `if ($scope.patientDetails.birthDate) { ... }`. When the field is present, behavior is byte-identical to before (same statements, same order, now merely inside a truthy-guarded block). When absent, the function now skips only this birth-date-derived block and continues normally (`getAutoRenderedServicesByPatient`, `getTemplateCountByAdmissionOrEncounter`, etc. still run) instead of throwing and aborting the whole function. Downstream effect of skipping: `$scope.birthYear` stays `undefined`, so the existing `$scope.years` year-dropdown-filter loop below (unchanged) naturally produces an empty list rather than crashing — an acceptable degrade, not a new behavior path, since a missing birth date already made a real year dropdown impossible.

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/opd/transection/OPDCoverSheetController.js` (`$scope.checkAppointment`, lines ~2081-2085)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** (previously: uncaught exception aborting the function for encounters with no appointment/birth-date data; now: function completes, year-dropdown data is simply empty for that case). Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)**.

**Notes:** Verified via `node --check` on the modified file (no Java compile applies — this is a pure AngularJS/JS controller). Scope was kept to the minimal guard described in the task — no other logic in `checkAppointment` was touched, and behavior when `birthDate` is present is unchanged.

## BR-092 — Duplicate global-button-styleCss.js inclusion breaks sidebar bindings (2026-07-13)

**Context:** Confirmed live via browser console (`Uncaught SyntaxError: Identifier 'buttonStyles' has already been declared`; `document.querySelectorAll('script[src*="global-button-styleCss"]').length` returns 2 on the Home page). `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/global-button-styleCss.js` declares `const buttonStyles = {...}` at top-level/global scope, so loading the file twice via two separate `<script src="...">` tags throws a parse-time `SyntaxError` the second time it's parsed, since `const` (unlike `var`) cannot be redeclared in the same scope.

**Fix:** both inclusions were found in the same file, `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/index.html` (the single-page app shell), at line 420 (among the early utility-script includes, alongside `chart.js`/`fullcalendar.js`, before `config/app.js` and before any controller scripts) and line 1945 (near the very end of the script list, immediately after a leftover commented-out mistaken attempt — `<!-- <script src=".../global-button-style.js"></script> -->` — at line 1944, on a `.css.js`-typo'd path, suggesting the working line right after it was an accidental re-add rather than an intentional second inclusion). Removed the second (line 1945) inclusion, keeping the first (line 420) as the sole inclusion.

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/index.html` (removed one duplicate `<script src="resources/assets/js/global-button-styleCss.js"></script>` line)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** *(corrected 2026-07-13 per Checker review — originally misclassified below as Refactoring (Safe))*. Per RULES.md's own definition, a bug fix that changes output — here, from an uncaught parse-time `SyntaxError` that broke every ng-click binding declared after it (unresponsive sidebar navigation) to a working, responsive sidebar — is a Behavioral Change requiring owner sign-off, regardless of whether the new behavior is more correct. This entry was inconsistently classified relative to `BR-091` above, which correctly applies this same reasoning to an analogous crash-guard fix; the two now match. Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)** *(was: N/A/Refactoring-exempt)*.

**Notes:** Verified via `grep -c` that exactly one `global-button-styleCss.js` `<script>` reference remains in `index.html` (the sole rendered-page template chain referencing this script — no other JSP/HTML template in the module references it). No other syntax changes made to the surrounding markup.

## BR-093 — Missing $filter dependency breaks Local Purchase Request list refresh (2026-07-13)

**Context:** Confirmed live via browser console (`ReferenceError: $filter is not defined`) inside `GetItemList`, the Procurement Local Purchase Request module's item-search/list-refresh function. Save itself works; only the post-save list refresh (and the item search-by-name/search-by-code paths that reuse the same function) is broken.

**Fix:** located `GetItemList` in `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/procurement/purchase/addNewLocalPRController.js` (`addNewLocalPRController`, registered as `addNewLocalPR.Controller`). Confirmed `$filter('filter')(...)` is used twice inside `GetItemList` (item-name search and item-code search branches) and nowhere else guards or aliases it. Unlike the task's assumed convention, this controller is registered via a plain named function (`angular.module("myApp").controller("addNewLocalPR.Controller", addNewLocalPRController)`) with **no string-array injection list** — Angular resolves its dependencies by parameter name (implicit annotation), consistent with every other dependency already in this function's signature. Added `$filter` as a new parameter at the end of `addNewLocalPRController`'s parameter list; no array to update since none exists in this file.

**Files changed:**
- `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/procurement/purchase/addNewLocalPRController.js` (`addNewLocalPRController` function signature, line 4)

Classification: **Behavioral Change** *(corrected 2026-07-13 per Checker review — originally misclassified below as Refactoring (Safe))*. Per RULES.md's own definition, a bug fix that changes output — here, from an uncaught `ReferenceError` that aborted the post-save list refresh to a working item-search/list-refresh — is a Behavioral Change requiring owner sign-off, regardless of whether the new behavior is more correct. This entry was inconsistently classified relative to `BR-091` above, which correctly applies this same reasoning to an analogous crash-guard fix; the two now match. Owner Decision: **Approved (owner sign-off 2026-07-13)** *(was: N/A/Refactoring-exempt)*.

**Notes:** Verified via `node --check` (syntax) and confirmed via grep that this file has no `.controller([...])` array form and no `$inject` property anywhere, so the parameter-list edit is the complete fix — there is no second injection site to update.

## BR-094 — Investigation only, no fix made (2026-07-13)

**Context:** Confirmed instances: `{{recordTime}}` renders as literal (uninterpolated) text in the OPD Cover Sheet's "Summary" panel header (`views/OPD/CoverSheet/opd-coversheet.html:3726`), and `{{mappedData[41]}}` similarly (`opd-coversheet.html:13772`).

**Investigation (no fix applied):**
- Both variables are correctly assigned on `$scope` **inside the same controller function** (`OPDCoverSheetController.js`) that this exact template is bound to via the named ui-router state `OPDCoverSheet` (`$scope.recordTime` set at `OPDCoverSheetController.js:45907` etc.; `$scope.mappedData` populated at `OPDCoverSheetController.js:44196-44198` from the `getActiveButtonMaster` API response, called unconditionally at controller init). No scope mismatch or naming typo found — this rules out the task's first hypothesized cause.
- Byte-level check of both source lines confirms plain ASCII `{{...}}` — no HTML entities, no look-alike Unicode braces, nothing that would prevent Angular's default interpolation regex from matching.
- Confirmed via codebase-wide grep that no `$interpolateProvider.startSymbol/endSymbol` override exists anywhere in the app (only the AngularJS library's own internal default-symbol code) — rules out a custom-delimiter mismatch.
- Confirmed no isolate-scope directive or manual `$compile` call wraps either binding (no `.directive(` registration anywhere in `OPDCoverSheetController.js`) — rules out an isolate-scope-shadowing theory.
- Structurally compared the OPD copy of this "voice recorder" widget against the near-identical copy in `views/clinicalWorkStation/ipd-CoverSheet.html` (same `coversheet-summary-recorder` markup, same `{{recordTime}}` binding pattern) — no structural or quoting difference found that would explain OPD-only breakage.
- `$scope.mappedData[41]` would, per AngularJS's normal safe-undefined expression evaluation, render as an **empty string** (not literal `{{}}` text) even if the server's button-master data genuinely has no row for tabId 41 — so a master-data gap alone does not explain the reported symptom either.
- One unconfirmed, likely-unrelated lead flagged for owner awareness: `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/config/app.js` registers two ui-router states with the **identical URL** `/OPDCoverSheet` — `OPDCoverSheet` (controller `OPDCoverSheetController`) and `SpeechToText` (same `templateUrl`, different controller name) — a pre-existing state/url collision. Named-state navigation (which this app appears to use throughout) still resolves to `OPDCoverSheetController`, which does define both variables correctly, so this collision does not clearly explain the symptom, but it is a config smell worth cleaning up independently.
- This project's own prior session (BR-074, logged before this branch) found the live deployed container can diverge from git source by dozens of files; since no defect is reproducible in the current git source by static analysis, it is possible the browser-console-confirmed symptom reflects stale deployed markup/JS rather than a defect in this repository's current `opd-coversheet.html`/`OPDCoverSheetController.js`.

**No files changed.** Per this session's explicit instructions, stopping here rather than guessing at or forcing a change (e.g. deleting the `{{}}` expressions) without a confirmed root cause. Recommends a follow-up session: (1) re-verify the live container's deployed files against git HEAD for this template/controller (per BR-074 precedent) before further action, and (2) if still reproducible against current git source, inspect the live-rendered DOM (browser Elements panel, not just console) at the moment of the bug to see the actual compiled markup around these two bindings.

Classification: Neither (investigation only, no schema/data/code change of any kind). Owner Decision: Pending — needs owner/human triage on whether to schedule a live-container-vs-git-source reconciliation pass before a future session revisits this.

**Notes:** Could not get a clean isolated compile signal for HIS-WAR itself in this environment: `mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am compile -DskipTests -o` fails, but with the exact same errors (all in `ContentCachingRequestWrapper.java`, an unrelated file this session never touched, missing the `com.beust.jcommander.internal` dependency) as a build attempted against this branch's pre-change baseline (commit `d7b58e33`, verified via `git stash`/rebuild/`stash pop` — byte-identical error log) — this is the pre-existing, already-logged **BR-002** defect, not a regression introduced here. No compiler error in either run references `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` or the new `ErrorCorrelationUtil.java`, which is the strongest signal available in this environment that this change itself is syntactically and type-correct. Recommend the owner treat BR-002's resolution as a prerequisite for a genuine HIS-WAR build-verification signal on this or any future HIS-WAR change.

## BR-PENDING-vitals-celsius-default-and-placeholder-items — Two QA-reported defects, investigation only (2026-07-14)

**Note on numbering:** this session's task referred to these as "BR-094" and "BR-099" and the ui-router lead below as "BR-096" — those are labels from `MANUAL_QA_DEFECT_REPORT.md`, the same external, never-committed QA report already flagged in this manifest's top-of-file note. They do not refer to this manifest's own BR-094 (a different, already-logged investigation, see above). No renumbering of this manifest's existing entries was needed or attempted.

**1. Vitals form Temperature Celsius field shows -18 on load, auto-corrects when Fahrenheit is entered.**

Investigated the two places in the codebase that implement a Celsius/Fahrenheit vitals conversion:
- `VitalAddController.js` (Nursing "Add New Vitals" standalone screen, `views/Nursing/add-new-vitals.html`) — the only place in the entire codebase with a `(value - 32) * 5.0 / 9.0` / `value * 9.0 / 5.0 + 32` conversion formula (grepped for this formula shape across all of `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/`, zero other matches). `formatValue(value, scale)`'s `'C'` branch computes `Math.round((value - 32) * 5.0 / 9.0)` — for `value = 0`, this is `Math.round(-17.78) = -18`, an exact numeric match for the reported default. However, this function is **only ever invoked via `ng-blur`** (confirmed via grep for `formatValue(` across every `.html` view, not just this controller — the only two call sites are both `ng-blur` handlers in `add-new-vitals.html`, lines 38/41). Nothing in `VitalAddController.js` calls it automatically on load, on `$scope` init, or via a `$watch`/`$timeout`; the controller's own `getVitalsList()` call populates `$scope.vitalDetailsList` from `GET /api/global/vitals/{orgId}/{unitId}`, whose backing Hibernate named native query (`GET_VITAL_MASTER_LIST_FOR_NURSING`, `VitalMaster.java`) selects only `vital_name`/`unit`/`vital_id` from `emr.m_vital_master` — no observation/default-value column at all — so a freshly-loaded row's `observation`/`observation1` are `undefined`, not `-18`, per the current git source.
- The OPD Cover Sheet's own vitals tab (`OPDCoverSheetController.js`'s `validateTemperature`/`convertTemperatureUnit`/`calculateTemeperature` functions, `opd-coversheet.html` lines ~10793-10819) was also checked as a second candidate "Vitals form": its Celsius-labeled input (`vitalData.vitalValueShow`) only exists in the DOM once the unit dropdown (`unitT`) is manually switched by the user (`ng-if="vitalData.unitT == 'C'"`), and none of its conversion functions fire except via `ng-blur`/`ng-change` on user-driven interaction — no automatic-on-load path found here either.

**Conclusion: not reproducible in the current git source by static analysis.** The `-18` value is an exact match for what this codebase's one real conversion formula produces from a `0`/blank Fahrenheit input, and "auto-corrects when F is entered" is exactly consistent with that formula re-running with real data and overwriting a stale value — but no code path in either candidate screen invokes the formula without user interaction. Per this session's instruction, **no fix was made**, since forcing a change without a confirmed trigger would be guessing. Same class of open question as this manifest's own BR-094 (recordTime/mappedData) and BR-074 precedent: flagging live-container-vs-git-source drift as the most likely explanation for a future session with browser/live access to confirm — this session had no server or browser available to reproduce it directly.

**2. Placeholder text leaking into user-facing Item/Instrument Name fields.**

Searched all DB seed/migration SQL under `docs/` (this project's established seed-script convention — confirmed no separate Flyway/Liquibase migration directory exists; `docs/br0XX_*.sql` files, run manually against `his-local-postgres`, are the actual seeding mechanism used by every prior session). Did not touch or query the live database, per this session's instruction.

- `"Sample Medical Equipment Item (placeholder -- needs owner/procurement review)"` is an **exact match**, found at `docs/br082_asset_indent_seed.sql:44` — a deliberately-flagged placeholder row inserted into `public.m_item.name` by the BR-082 (Assets Indent E2E test) session, explicitly commented at seed-time as "flagged as a placeholder needing owner/procurement review" (line 30). Reused (not re-flagged) by BR-086's Linen Indent E2E test. **This is unambiguously a data issue** — the prior session that wrote it already self-documented it as a temporary test-data placeholder, not application code. Per this session's instruction, **no code change was made**; flagging for a future data-correction task: either replace this seed row's name with a real procurement-approved item name, or have the owner explicitly accept it as a permanent generic placeholder, before it is ever used outside test/E2E contexts.
- `"Scissors - Surgical"` / `"Forceps - Artery"` (`cssd.m_instrument_master`, `docs/br079_seed_masters.sql:120-123`) are real, plausible generic CSSD instrument names seeded by BR-079 alongside the module's other **explicitly-flagged** placeholder rows (the autoclave and wrapper-expiry rows in the same file both carry a `(placeholder -- ... needs owner review)` suffix; these two instrument rows do not). The exact QA-quoted string `"Scissors - Surgical (CSSD instrument, placeholder -- needs owner/procurement review)"` does not exist verbatim anywhere in git-tracked source — only the plain `"Scissors - Surgical"` name does. This is still a data-layer question (real vs. placeholder instrument master content), not a code defect; flagging the discrepancy between the QA report's exact quoted string and what's actually in the seed script for owner awareness, in case the live DB has since diverged from this seed script (same class of drift risk as finding 1 above and BR-074's precedent).

Classification: Neither (investigation only; no code, schema, or data change of any kind this session). Owner Decision: Pending — needs owner triage on (a) whether to schedule live-browser reproduction of the temperature default, and (b) a data-correction pass replacing the BR-082 placeholder item name before any non-test use.

**Notes:** No compile check applicable (no Java/JS files changed). Investigated in the isolated worktree `/tmp/legacy-modernization-batch3`, branch `investigate-fix/br094-096-linen`, not merged.

## BR-PENDING-speechtotext-opdcoversheet-url-collision-ruled-out — Confirmed and ruled out as cause of unrendered bindings (2026-07-14)

**Task:** confirm or rule out the `SpeechToText`/`OPDCoverSheet` `$stateProvider` url collision flagged as an unconfirmed lead in this manifest's own BR-094 entry (above). Investigation only, no fix — per this session's explicit instruction, given how much prior investigation into this specific symptom has already gone in circles.

**Collision confirmed to exist:** `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/config/app.js` registers `.state('OPDCoverSheet', { url: '/OPDCoverSheet', templateUrl: 'views/OPD/CoverSheet/opd-coversheet.html', controller: "OPDCoverSheetController", ... })` at line 13346, and `.state('SpeechToText', { url: '/OPDCoverSheet', templateUrl: 'views/OPD/CoverSheet/opd-coversheet.html', controller: "SpeechToText", ... })` at line 13371 — identical `url` and identical `templateUrl`, registered ~25 lines apart, differing only in state name and (nominal) controller.

**Ruled out as the cause of the unrendered `{{recordTime}}`/`{{mappedData[41]}}` bindings, for two independent reasons:**
1. **Navigation to this screen is by raw URL, not named state, and URL ties resolve to registration order.** Grepped the whole `webapp/` tree for every way this screen could be entered (`$state.go('OPDCoverSheet'...)`, `$state.go('SpeechToText'...)`, `ui-sref="OPDCoverSheet"`, `ui-sref="SpeechToText"`, `href="#/OPDCoverSheet"`) — the only match anywhere is a plain `href="#/OPDCoverSheet"` link in `views/common/side-bar.html:1073`. AngularJS UI-Router resolves an exact-duplicate URL pattern to whichever state was added to its internal matcher **first**; since `OPDCoverSheet` is registered before `SpeechToText` in `app.js`, direct/URL-hash navigation always resolves to `OPDCoverSheetController`, never `SpeechToText`.
2. **The `SpeechToText` state's controller doesn't exist as an Angular controller at all**, so it could never actually activate without a hard error. `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/opd/transection/SpeechToText.js` (828 lines) is a plain, non-Angular utility script for the browser's Web Speech API (global functions like `callvoice1()`, no `angular.module(...).controller(...)` registration anywhere in the file — confirmed via grep). If ui-router ever did try to instantiate the `SpeechToText` state, Angular's `$controller` service would throw (`Argument 'SpeechToText' is not a function, got undefined`), producing a hard, visible JS error — not the reported symptom, which is two silently-uninterpolated bindings with no crash. This state registration, and the loose script itself, appear to be leftovers from an abandoned attempt to wire voice dictation in as a separate routed state; the feature that actually shipped instead embeds `SpeechToText.js` directly via a `<script>` tag inside `opd-coversheet.html` itself (line 26414) — the same "voice recorder" widget this manifest's BR-094 entry already identified as structurally identical between OPD and IPD Cover Sheet.

**Conclusion: the collision is real but dead/vestigial routing config, definitively not the explanation for the unrendered bindings.** No fix attempted this session per instruction; this lead from BR-094 can now be considered closed/ruled-out rather than re-investigated in a future session. The unrendered-bindings symptom itself remains unexplained by static analysis (per BR-094's own conclusion) and still needs live-container-vs-git-source reconciliation to make further progress.

Classification: Neither (investigation only, no code change of any kind this session). Owner Decision: Pending — no action needed on the collision itself (recommend cleaning it up as config hygiene whenever `app.js` is next touched, but it is not blocking anything and not the cause of any known symptom); the unrendered-bindings root cause remains open per BR-094.

**Notes:** No compile check applicable (no files changed). Isolated worktree `/tmp/legacy-modernization-batch3`, branch `investigate-fix/br094-096-linen`, not merged.

## BR-PENDING-linen-laundry-investigation — Linen & Laundry module current-state investigation, no fix, no test (2026-07-14)

Read-only investigation, same style/discipline as BR-087's OTIMS investigation: structure map, confirm which of the 5 previously-known bugs (from BR-086) are still present in current source, plus additional findings surfaced along the way. **No test written, no code fixed, no data seeded this session**, per explicit instruction.

**Module structure:** `legacy/LinenLaundry/` is a dedicated Maven module (own `pom.xml`) with 9 sub-flow controllers under `com.param.linenLaundry`: `linenIndent`, `linenIndentApproval`, `linenIssue`, `linenIssueList`, `linenIssueAcceptance`, `linenReturn`, `linenReturnAcceptance`, `rejectedLinenAcceptance`, `sendToCondemnate`. UI lives under `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/views/Linen/` (50+ view files — considerably more screens than the module has real controllers for, see below) with matching JS under `resources/assets/js/controller/linenlaundry/`.

**BR-086's 5 bugs, re-confirmed against current source (all still present, none fixed since 2026-07-11):**
1. `addNewLinenController.js`'s `searchNewItem()` still hardcodes `"assetTypeId": 2` (line 154) — item search still copy-pasted from the Assets Indent controller, still can only surface Fixed/Equipment items.
2. The same controller's `GetAssetCategoryList()` still hardcodes `CONSTANTS.GLOBAL.ASSET_CATEGORY_API + 1 + ...` (line 115) — still inconsistent with finding 1's `assetTypeId: 2`.
3. `PopulateDropdown()` (lines 69-102) still never auto-selects `fromStore` to the user's own store: `indent.fromStore = indent.fromStores[0]` (line 76) runs *before* the dropdown API call, against the placeholder `defaultStores` list ("Select Store"); the `.then()` callback (lines 81-84) reassigns `indent.fromStores` (the list) from the real API response but never reassigns `indent.fromStore` (the selection) — so the placeholder object reference is what's left selected after the real store list loads. Confirmed via direct reading, not just re-stating BR-086's claim.
4. The null-`storeId` Hibernate/Postgres crash this enables (`ItemsDaoImpl.itemsDetailsFixedAsset`/`itemsDetails`) is server-side and was not re-triggered live this session (no server running / read-only investigation), but its precondition (finding 3) is confirmed still reachable.
5. `LinenIndentService.java:101` still hardcodes `new LinenIndent("IND-001", ...)` for every indent — confirmed via direct grep, unchanged since BR-086.

**New findings this session, beyond BR-086's original 5:**
- The task's "live HTTP 405 on a specific endpoint" detail (from the external `MANUAL_QA_DEFECT_REPORT.md`, per this session's numbering note above) could not be independently confirmed or precisely located via static analysis alone — no server was run this session to reproduce an actual HTTP status code, and no client/server HTTP-verb mismatch was found across the flows checked (`LinenIssuePendingIssue`'s `ApprovePending`/`PATCH`, `LinenIssueAcceptance`'s save/`PATCH` both correctly match their server `@RequestMapping(method = RequestMethod.PATCH)` counterparts). However, a very plausible source of *some* live error was found: **`RejectedLinenAcceptanceController.java`** has only its `/dropdowns` GET endpoint active — its `/search` (POST), `/{id}` (GET), and `/{id}` (PATCH, save) endpoints are all present in source but entirely **commented out** (lines 50-103). **`SendToCondemnateController.java`** similarly implements only `/dropdowns` and nothing else (no commented-out remainder, suggesting this one was never built past a stub rather than built-then-disabled). Both `rejectedLinenAcceptance`/`rejectedLinenAcceptanceDetails` and `sendToCondemnation`/`addNewSendToCondemnation`/`sendToCondemnationDetails` ui-router states (`app.js` lines ~15261-15312) point to real `.html` templates but are wired to the shared generic `"MasterController"`, not a dedicated controller — confirmed via grep, no JS file anywhere in the codebase references either `sendToCondemnate` or `reject/linen/acceptance` API paths. Net effect: both the "Rejected Linen Acceptance" and "Send to Condemnation" flows are non-functional beyond populating their dropdowns, on both the frontend and backend — not a regression, but a confirmed, currently-incomplete feature area. A path that resolves to no handler at all (rather than one with a mismatched HTTP verb) would normally produce an HTTP 404 rather than 405 from Spring's default dispatcher behavior, so this finding does not conclusively explain a reported 405 specifically — flagging it as the strongest concrete lead found, pending live reproduction.

**Recommendation for a future dedicated fix session:** treat this as two separate slices — (a) BR-086's original 5 bugs in the Linen Indent flow (well-understood, already scoped, smallest fix surface), and (b) a much larger scoping decision on whether "Rejected Linen Acceptance"/"Send to Condemnation" should be completed (uncommenting + finishing the disabled controller code, writing the missing frontend controllers) or intentionally left as future/out-of-scope work — this is a product-scope call for the owner, not something to guess at.

Classification: Neither (investigation only, no schema/data/code change, no test written). Owner Decision: Pending — needs owner triage on (a) prioritizing BR-086's 5 confirmed-still-present bugs for a fix session, and (b) whether Rejected Linen Acceptance/Send to Condemnation should be completed or explicitly deferred.

**Notes:** No compile check applicable (no files changed). Isolated worktree `/tmp/legacy-modernization-batch3`, branch `investigate-fix/br094-096-linen`, not merged.

## BR-PENDING-razorpay-signature-verification — Razorpay client-trusted-amount + missing signature verification fix (2026-07-15)

**What changed:** implements `docs/REVIEW_DRAFT_payment-fixes-FINAL.md` sec 1/2a/2b/3/4 in full (sec 2c, Payment Links signature verification, deliberately excluded — parked pending owner-confirmed payload format). `confirmAppointment` and `confirmPayment` (`RazorPaymentController.java`) now verify the Razorpay signature and re-fetch the order from Razorpay's own API before creating a bill or marking a payment-link log paid, using Razorpay's amount/status as the source of truth instead of a client-supplied field that had no independent verification. `confirmAppointment1` (an unauthenticated debug endpoint hardcoding one specific order ID, zero callers found anywhere) was removed. A new shared `IRazorPayService.verifyRazorpayPayment(...)` method (signature check + `Orders.fetch`) backs all of the above plus `saveBillFromKiosk`/`saveBillFromKioskFutureDate` (`BillingMasterController.java`), which previously trusted a client's entire `paymentReceiptDetailsDtosList` — amount, transaction number, payment mode — with zero Razorpay involvement of any kind for any payment claiming `paymentModeId == 8`. This required an additive `PaymentReceiptDetailsDto` extension (`razorpayOrderId`/`razorpaySignature`) and a matching frontend change (`selfRegistrationController.js`'s checkout handler now sends both — Razorpay's own callback already provided them, only one of three fields was being read before) — shipped together deliberately, not staged, since the backend alone would reject every real Razorpay payment until the frontend caught up.

**Why this is Behavioral Change / Financial Correctness, not a pure security patch:** for any request that previously succeeded with a spoofed or mismatched amount, this fix now correctly rejects it — output changes for that class of input, same rationale RULES.md's Behavioral Change definition covers, and the same "Financial Correctness" subtype used for BR-018 (the fix corrects a payment-integrity gap, not just an internal-structure refactor).

**Non-Razorpay payment modes (cash/card/cheque, CCAvenue) are deliberately unchanged** — see the review doc sec 4 for the reasoning (no external gateway to verify against, and a companion `JwtTokenFilter` auth-requirement change carried the same "might break a legitimate flow" risk that already caught out an earlier draft of this fix this same session — held back rather than rushed).

## BR-PENDING-documentupload-hardcoded-pdf-owner-password — Hardcoded PDF owner-password in document-upload utilities, logged as a known issue, deliberately NOT fixed this phase (2026-07-15)

**Owner instruction:** log the hardcoded password in `DocumentUploadUtility.java` as a known issue and do **not** change it in this phase (Phase 1, billing-accuracy work). This entry records the finding only — no code, schema, or data was changed.

**Finding:** `stamper.setEncryption(patientMasterDto.getMrnNumber().getBytes(), "clinivantage".getBytes(), ...)` — an iText `PdfStamper` password-protects patient PDFs using the patient's MRN as the *user* password (first arg) and the string literal `"clinivantage"` as the *owner* password (second arg). The owner password is hardcoded in source, identical across the codebase, and would let anyone who knows it bypass the PDF's permission restrictions on any patient document. Same class of issue as the hardcoded-secret findings already remediated in BR-019/BR-020 (see `docs/SECRETS_REMEDIATION.md`, `docs/SECRETS_REMEDIATION_BATCH2.md`).

**Two occurrences, both confirmed currently unreachable:**
1. `legacy/ADT/src/main/java/com/param/adt/common/DocumentUploadUtility.java:315` — the occurrence the owner flagged. **Dead code:** it sits entirely inside a block comment spanning lines 305–410 (block-comment markers verified at line 305 `*//*` open and line 410 `}*/` close). Not compiled, not callable.
2. `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/common/DocUploadUtility.java:1457` — a near-duplicate of the same `getFileFromUrl`/`uploadDocumentWithPassword` pair. This copy is **live (uncommented) source**, but a full-tree grep found **no external caller** of either `getFileFromUrl` or `uploadDocumentWithPassword` anywhere outside these two utility classes (the public `uploadDocumentWithPassword` entry point has zero references; `getFileFromUrl` is only called internally by it). So this path is effectively unreachable dead code as well, not an active runtime exposure.

Related but out of scope for this entry (flagged only): `legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/common/SmsUtility.java` hardcodes `String user = "clinivantage"` as an SMS-gateway username in 7 places — a separate hardcoded-credential item for a future security sweep, not the PDF-password issue logged here.

**Severity: Low** — the secret is present in source (a real hygiene/defense-in-depth concern, and it would become a live exposure if either method is ever re-enabled or wired to a caller), but no reachable execution path uses it today.

**Recommended future fix (deferred, not for this phase):** when a dedicated security-hygiene / dead-code session is scheduled, either delete the two dead `getFileFromUrl`/`uploadDocumentWithPassword` methods outright, or — if the password-protected-PDF feature is to be revived — externalize the owner password to configuration (same `CHANGE_ME_SET_LOCALLY_NOT_IN_GIT` convention already used for `jdbc.password` and the Duo/OAuth secrets sanitized in the git baseline) rather than a source literal. Changing the literal in place would be a Behavioral Change (PDFs encrypted with the old owner password would no longer open with it) requiring owner sign-off, which is a further reason it is out of scope for the billing-accuracy phase.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code/schema/data change this session). Owner Decision: Pending — owner has explicitly deferred the fix to a later phase; no action requested now beyond recording it.

**Notes:** No compile check applicable (no files changed). Discovered during the pre-commit secret scan of the git baseline (2026-07-15); the git history itself is clean of this value only in the sense that it is a functional PDF-permission password, not one of the connection/API secrets sanitized before the baseline commit — it remains in the committed source at the two locations above by design, pending the deferred fix.

## BR-PENDING-java21-track-a-source-changes — Java 21 upgrade (Track A), 6 financially-isolated modules: pom.xml + mechanical source modernization (2026-07-15)

**What changed:** Ran OpenRewrite's `UpgradeToJava21` recipe against the 6 modules `docs/JAVA_UPGRADE_MODULE_DEPENDENCY_MAP.md` confirmed have no Billing/Global/Entity financial-code dependency: AngularSpring, BloodBank, CSSDMaster, DietAndKitchen, LinenLaundry, RabbitMQ. Re-verified the map's isolation claims first (fresh import/pom greps matched the map byte-for-byte; no drift since it was written). Branch: `upgrade/java21-track-a-safe-modules`.

Every module's `pom.xml` now targets Java 21 (`<release>21</release>` where an explicit compiler-plugin block existed; `maven.compiler.source/target` properties bumped directly where the module only used bare properties — 3 of the 6 modules used the latter style, which OpenRewrite's plugin-config recipe silently left at 17; caught by re-verifying the actual `javac` invocation after each module rather than trusting `BUILD SUCCESS` alone, and one instance of this gap — CSSDMaster — was initially missed and had to be corrected in a follow-up commit, `638db7e`). `commons-codec` was bumped 1.9 → 1.17.2 in BloodBank and DietAndKitchen (transitive dependency version pin only, not directly imported by either module's own code).

Source-level changes across 15 files (LinenLaundry ×4, CSSDMaster ×10, DietAndKitchen ×3, BloodBank ×5 — some files touched by more than one recipe rule): `list.get(0)` → `list.getFirst()` (SequencedCollection modernization), removal of stray `;` inside empty `finally { ; }` blocks (no-op), and one `new Integer(x)` → `Integer.valueOf(x)` (BloodBank's `DonorRegistrationServiceImpl`, inside an `int == Integer` comparison).

**Why this needed a manifest entry despite being tooling-driven, not hand-written:** per RULES.md's definition, a change is only "Refactoring (Safe)" if output is *provably* byte-identical for all inputs. `getFirst()` and `get(0)` differ in exception type on an empty list (`NoSuchElementException` vs `IndexOutOfBoundsException`) — reviewed every catch block in all 15 changed files and confirmed none catch that specific type (all are broad `catch (Exception e)` / `catch (HibernateException he)`), so this is not observable anywhere in the current codebase, but it is not literally byte-identical in the empty-list edge case, so it doesn't meet the strict "Safe" bar either. The `Integer.valueOf` change was separately verified: the comparison is `int == Integer`, so Java auto-unboxes either way — genuinely no behavioral difference, including for cached vs. non-cached values, since the primitive side forces unboxing regardless of which factory produced the object.

No local `try/catch` structure was removed or altered anywhere (only the source *inside* existing try blocks changed, or a no-op `;` was removed from a `finally`) — RULES.md's centralized-exception-handler rule is not implicated. No database, schema, or business term changed.

**Verification:** all 6 modules compile clean under a real JDK 21 (`javac ... target 21` / `release 21` confirmed per-module, not inferred from `BUILD SUCCESS` alone). BloodBank's one existing test (`AppTest.java`, JUnit 3 archetype placeholder) passes 1/1. No other module in this batch has any existing test source. No file outside these 6 modules was modified. `main`/`origin` untouched — all work is local commits on the dedicated branch, not pushed.

No try/catch structure removed/altered. No DB/schema/business-term change. Owner sign-off requested only because this doesn't meet the strict letter of "provably byte-identical," not because a real behavioral risk was found. |

## BR-PENDING-java21-track-b-source-changes — Java 21 upgrade (Track B), AssetManagement + LIS: re-verified financially-isolated, pom.xml + mechanical source modernization (2026-07-15)

**What changed:** Following up on Track A, re-verified (not re-read from the old map) all 3 modules the map had flagged "needs owner review": Entity, AssetManagement, LIS.

**Entity — left blocked, not upgraded.** Mapped its actual financial-entity blast radius rather than trusting the map's formal-dependency answer alone: Entity physically hosts 156 real financial JPA entity classes (deposits, payments, refunds, doctor shares, tariffs, discounts, credit/debit notes, cost estimates, package consumption). Traced every module's real (not package-prefix) usage: Billing (itself) and Global (58 imports — the shared DAO/service layer nearly every blocked module's billing logic runs through) have substantial real read/write access; Pharmacy (5 of 6 real) and HIS-WAR (1 real) also genuinely read/write these classes for deposit/refund and doctor-share logic respectively. ADT's and Abdm's financial-class imports, previously ambiguous, are now confirmed fully dead code (import line only, zero references) — a refinement of the original map, not a reversal. Given this blast radius, Entity was not touched — any tooling-driven change to these 156 classes' source directly touches the persistence layer live billing transactions in Billing/Global run through, both under separate active correctness remediation.

**AssetManagement and LIS — re-verified genuinely safe, upgraded.** AssetManagement: re-confirmed 25 unique imports, all non-financial `Global` utility/DTO classes, zero `billing`-prefixed. Re-traced the one real nuance (`IWalkInSalesDao`, a Global interface mixing real billing methods with inventory methods) to its actual implementation: the only method AssetManagement calls, `saveStockTransaction()`, is a one-line Hibernate persist with no billing DAO/service involvement. LIS: re-confirmed 52 unique imports, zero `billing`-prefixed; both of the map's flagged nuances (a `BillingMasterDto` import, an `IBillingCommonConstants` implementation) reconfirmed as non-issues — the import is genuinely dead code, and the constants interface is 100% non-executable (0 methods) with only its document-numbering constants actually referenced, never anything billing-calculation-related.

Ran OpenRewrite's `UpgradeToJava21` recipe on both. AssetManagement: pom.xml (`<release>21</release>`, plugin-block style, no manual fix needed) + 9 source files — 8 `getFirst()` modernizations plus one `new Long(x)` → `Long.valueOf(x)` in the exact `IWalkInSalesDao`-calling file (`CorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleServiceImpl.java`), verified safe: the resulting value is only ever passed as a method argument, never `==`-compared. LIS: pom.xml (bare-properties style, needed the same manual fix as several Track A modules; `commons-codec` 1.9→1.17.2) + 61 source files — `getFirst()` modernizations, no-op double-semicolon removal, one `instanceof` pattern-match rewrite (textbook-equivalent), and 4 `new Double(x)` → `Double.valueOf(x)` changes (unlike `Integer`/`Long.valueOf`, `Double.valueOf` never caches — unconditionally behaviorally identical, not even an edge case). Ran a full symmetry cross-check on LIS's 61-file diff (every removed line has an exact added counterpart, or is a pure no-op) — confirmed nothing outside the documented patterns. Every catch block across both modules' changed files is broad (`Exception`/`HibernateException`), none type-specific.

One nuance flagged for transparency, not because it changes the safety verdict: AssetManagement's `saveStockTransaction()` call persists a `StockTransaction` entity that carries a `rate` field (inventory cost/valuation) — financial-*adjacent*, but it's asset/inventory cost tracking, not patient-billing logic, and touches none of Entity's 156 flagged financial JPA entities.

**Verification:** both modules compile clean under a real JDK 21 (`javac ... release 21` / `target 21` confirmed per-module via a clean-`target/` recompile, not inferred from `BUILD SUCCESS` alone). AssetManagement's one existing test (`AppTest.java`, JUnit archetype placeholder) passes 1/1. LIS has no test source. No file outside these 2 modules was modified; Entity, and all 9 originally-blocked modules, remain untouched. Branch `upgrade/java21-track-b-safe-modules`, not merged, not pushed.
