## BR-PENDING-java21-entity-source-changes — Java 21 upgrade (Step 3), Entity: 156 real financial JPA entities upgraded now that shadow-test characterization of Billing/Global is substantially complete (2026-07-16)

**What changed:** Entity was the module Track B left explicitly blocked (156 real financial JPA entities, substantial blast radius via Billing/Global/Pharmacy/HIS-WAR). Revisited now that the shadow-test harness has characterized the money-calculation core it feeds (`BillingCommonMethods.java`, `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`) and 8 real bugs found there are fixed and pinned by characterization tests — the condition Track B's blocking note was waiting on.

Ran OpenRewrite's `UpgradeToJava21` recipe scoped to Entity alone. 369 files touched, overwhelmingly one pure pattern: 361 files got `import java.io.Serial;` + `@Serial` added directly above an **already-existing** `serialVersionUID` field (confirmed via a per-file added/removed line-count diff — every file's `serialVersionUID`-related addition has an exact matching removal; zero files gained a *new* `serialVersionUID` value that didn't already exist). This is a compiler-visibility annotation only, not a behavioral change — it does not alter the field's value or Java's serialization format. Beyond that: one `instanceof UserDetails` → pattern-match rewrite in `Util.java` (textbook-equivalent, confirmed by inspection), one `new Double(x)` → `Double.valueOf(x)` in `GlobalCommonDateUtils.java` (unconditionally identical — `Double.valueOf` never caches, same reasoning already applied to LIS in Track B), and a handful of no-op stray-semicolon removals. `pom.xml` gained one new `provided`-scope dependency (`jakarta.annotation-api` 1.3.5) that nothing in Entity currently imports (existing `javax.annotation.PostConstruct`/`Generated` usages were left untouched by the recipe) — additive only, no behavior change.

**Recipe gap, same class as Track A/B:** Entity's `pom.xml` sets the Java version via bare `<maven.compiler.source>`/`<maven.compiler.target>` properties, not an explicit compiler-plugin block, which OpenRewrite's plugin-rewrite recipe doesn't touch. Caught by re-verifying the actual `javac` invocation rather than trusting `BUILD SUCCESS` alone (`javac [debug target 21]` after a manual property bump to 21, versus a silent `target 17` before the fix).

**Verification:** compiles clean under a real JDK 21, confirmed via a clean-`target/` recompile with the literal `javac` invocation showing `target 21`. Full reactor test suite (`mvn clean test`, all 18 modules) re-run after this change: 141/141 pass, 0 failures/errors, `BUILD SUCCESS` — identical to the pre-upgrade baseline. No file outside `legacy/Entity/` was modified. Branch `upgrade/java21-entity`, off `main`, not merged, not pushed.

## BR-PENDING-java21-global-source-changes — Java 21 upgrade (Step 3), Global: the shared billing/co-pay/tax-calculation module, upgraded now that its own money-math bugs are characterized and fixed (2026-07-16)

**What changed:** Global hosts `BillingCommonMethods.java` and `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` — the shared co-pay/cap-split/tax-calculation core this session's shadow-test harness has spent most of its effort characterizing and fixing (8 real money bugs found and fixed, all pinned by characterization tests). Upgraded now that this core is substantially de-risked, same reasoning as the Entity upgrade earlier the same day.

Ran OpenRewrite's `UpgradeToJava21` recipe scoped to Global alone. 147 files touched (738 insertions, 814 deletions — net negative, mostly from stray-semicolon and boilerplate removal). Breakdown by pattern, each independently verified:

- **407 `list.get(0)` → `list.getFirst()`** modernizations. Verified class-wide: every catch block across all touched files is broad (`Exception`/`HibernateException`/etc.) — grepped the full touched-file set for `catch (...Exception`, found zero narrow catches of `IndexOutOfBoundsException`/`NoSuchElementException` in any file that also received a `getFirst()` change (one pre-existing, unrelated `NoSuchElementException` catch exists in `URLConnectionResponse.java`, in a method the diff didn't touch). Includes the 2 shadow-test characterization test files (`BillingCommonMethodsCharacterizationTest.java`, `ContractConsumptionServiceImplCharacterizationTest.java`) — both changed lines are guarded by a preceding `hasSize()`/non-empty assertion, so the empty-list edge case this pattern can differ on is unreachable at that point either way.
- **89 `new Integer/Long/Double(x)` → `valueOf(x)`** modernizations, including 4 inside a primitive `int ==` comparison (`response.getStatusLine().getStatusCode() == Integer.valueOf(...)`) — safe because Java unboxes the `Integer` operand for a primitive `==` comparison regardless of caching, same reasoning already applied in Track A/B. No boxed-vs-boxed `==` comparison found anywhere in the diff.
- **11 string-concatenation → text-block (`"""`) conversions**, across `GlobalDao.java` (2), `ProductionReleaseEmailNotificationConfigDaoImpl.java` (1, an HQL query), `AiAppointmentQueueDaoImpl.java` (1, a native SQL query), `UserRoleMasterDaoImpl.java` (1, a native SQL query), `AbcdeAssessmentDaoImpl.java` (1, a native SQL query), `IMailConstants.java` (5, email template strings) — a new pattern not seen in Track A/B, using OpenRewrite's `\` line-continuation and `\s` explicit-trailing-space escapes to preserve exact original content. **Not just eyeballed**: wrote a standalone Java verification program pairing the exact pre-recipe concatenated string against the exact post-recipe text block for all 11 conversions and asserting `.equals()` — all 11 came back byte-identical (see this session's scratch verification, not committed). Two of these are live HQL/SQL queries; confirming exact-string equality rules out any whitespace-driven query-behavior change.
- **9 `@Serial`** additions over already-existing `serialVersionUID` fields (same pure-annotation pattern as Entity), a handful of `instanceof` pattern-match rewrites (textbook-equivalent) including two in actual contract-consumption cast logic (`PackageConsumptionServiceDetailDto`/`TPackageConsumptionCapDetailsDto` in `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`'s support code — confirmed unchanged semantics, purely inlines the existing cast), one `Math.random()` → `ThreadLocalRandom.current().nextDouble()` (a coupon-code random-index generator, non-financial, statistically equivalent), and no-op stray-semicolon removal.
- **`pom.xml`**: 2 new dependencies (`jakarta.xml.bind-api` 2.3.3, `jakarta.annotation-api` 1.3.5 `provided`-scope) — both additive, needed for JAXB/annotation APIs no longer bundled in newer JDKs that this module's existing `javax.xml.bind`/`javax.annotation` code already relied on; confirmed the module compiles clean with them present. One dependency version bump, `modelmapper` 3.1.1 → 3.2.6 — checked actual usage: confined to `com.param.global.ticketofsystem.*` (the support-ticket subsystem), zero use in any billing/contract-consumption code path.

**Recipe gap, same class as Entity/Track A/B:** bare `<maven.compiler.source>`/`<maven.compiler.target>` properties, manually bumped 17→21.

**Verification:** compiles clean under a real JDK 21, confirmed via a clean-`target/` recompile with the literal `javac [debug target 21]` invocation (2,670 source files). Full reactor test suite (`mvn clean test`, all 18 modules, with Entity's Java 21 changes NOT yet merged in — this branch is off `main`) re-run after this change: 141/141 pass, 0 failures/errors, `BUILD SUCCESS` — identical to the pre-upgrade baseline. No file outside `legacy/Global/` was modified. Branch `upgrade/java21-global`, off `main`, not merged, not pushed.

Two others (`pharmacyTariffs.getFirst()`, initially concerning given an adjacent "Don't throw" comment) are both fully guarded by a preceding `!= null && !isEmpty()` check — the comment refers to a separate business decision (whether to error on *multiple* tariffs found), unrelated to the empty-list case. **No manual pom.xml fix needed** — Pharmacy uses the explicit `<maven-compiler-plugin>` block style (like Inventory/Procurement), and the recipe correctly rewrote it to `<release>21</release>`.

## BR-FIXED-globalexceptionhandler-null-customexception-crash — FIXED: the centralized exception handler no longer crashes on its own generic catch-all path (2026-07-17)

**Found during Step 5's live wave-1 smoke pass** (deploying the current `main` build to the local Tomcat + Postgres dev environment and driving the 5 wave-1 entry points via real HTTP requests, not just unit tests) — hitting `PATCH /api/procurements/quotation/grn/approve` (GRN approval, the tax-computation entry point) produced a plain `HTTP 500` with no useful correlation-tracked detail, unlike the other 4 wave-1 entry points which all failed cleanly (missing seed data, correctly wrapped by the centralized handler with a correlation ID).

**Root cause:** `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/java/com/param/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java:134`, the generic catch-all branch, constructs `new ErrorResponse("HIS-1001", ErrorCorrelationUtil.clientMessage(correlationId), null)` — passing a literal `null` where the code expects a `CustomException`. `ErrorResponse`'s matching 3-arg constructor (`legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/common/dto/ErrorResponse.java:37-48`) unconditionally calls `ex.printStackTrace(new PrintWriter(writer))` on that argument with no null check — so the exception handler itself throws a `NullPointerException` while trying to report the *original* error, losing the original error's detail and correlation-tracked logging for this path. The client still receives some 500 response (an outer Spring/Tomcat fallback catches the secondary crash), so this isn't a hard outage, but the intended `HIS-1001` error detail and structured logging is silently lost for any request that hits this exact branch.

**Confirmed pre-existing, unrelated to this session's work:** `git log --all -- legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/common/dto/ErrorResponse.java` shows the file has been touched only once, in the very first commit in this repo's history (`96b75c4 Baseline before upgrade`) — long before any track of this session's Java 21 work. Not introduced, not touched, not adjacent to anything modified this session.

**Blast radius, checked rather than assumed:** this specific `new ErrorResponse(..., null)` call is the **only** call site anywhere in the reactor that passes a null exception argument — confirmed via `grep -rn "new ErrorResponse("` across `legacy/*/src/main/java/`: roughly 80 other call sites exist (concentrated in `Global/.../ItemsController.java`, `GlobalController.java`, several Procurement controllers), and every one of those passes a real, non-null exception from its own local `catch` block. The bug is confined to the centralized handler's own generic fallback. That fallback is reachable from any of the **195 controller methods across the reactor that return `ResponseEntity<...>`**, whenever an exception isn't caught by a local try/catch or one of `GlobalExceptionHandler`'s other, more specific `@ExceptionHandler` branches (`BillingException`, `MethodArgumentNotValidException`, etc.) — so the exposure, while real, is narrower than "195 endpoints are broken": it only fires for genuinely-uncaught exceptions on that specific subset of endpoints.

**FIXED, owner-approved 2026-07-17** — proposal doc `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_GlobalExceptionHandlerNullCustomExceptionCrash.md` reviewed and approved; fix applied same day. Root-cause fix applied at `ErrorResponse.java`'s two constructors (`Exception`-typed and `CustomException`-typed), not at the `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` call site — both constructors now null-guard the exception argument before calling `.printStackTrace()`/`.getMessage()`/`.getErrors()` on it, leaving `summary`/`stackTrace`/`errors` unset (not populated) when no exception is supplied, instead of throwing. This protects all ~80 call sites in the reactor (all of which currently pass a real, non-null exception) against ever repeating this exact mistake, not just the one call site that triggered it. `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` itself was not touched — the correlation-ID hardening's security intent (never returning a raw exception/stack trace to the client) is fully preserved.

Before:
```java
public ErrorResponse(String code, String message, com.param.common.dto.CustomException ex ) {
    super();
    StringWriter writer = new StringWriter();
    ex.printStackTrace(new PrintWriter(writer));   // NPE when ex is null
    this.code = code;
    this.message = message;
    this.summary = ex.getMessage();
    this.stackTrace = writer.toString();
    this.errors = ex.getErrors();
}
```

After:
```java
public ErrorResponse(String code, String message, com.param.common.dto.CustomException ex ) {
    super();
    this.code = code;
    this.message = message;
    if (ex != null) {
        StringWriter writer = new StringWriter();
        ex.printStackTrace(new PrintWriter(writer));
        this.summary = ex.getMessage();
        this.stackTrace = writer.toString();
        this.errors = ex.getErrors();
    }
}
```
(The `Exception`-typed constructor received the identical guard.)

5 new tests added (`ErrorResponseNullExceptionFixTest`, Entity module): both constructors confirmed to no longer throw on a null exception; a test reproducing the exact production call shape (`new ErrorResponse(code, message, null)`, letting Java's own overload resolution pick the `CustomException` constructor, same as `GlobalExceptionHandler.java:134`) confirms it no longer crashes; two regression tests confirm non-null-exception behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (summary/stackTrace/errors still populate exactly as before).

**Side effect worth noting**: this also fixes the double-counting side effect flagged in the Tier 0+1 circuit-breaker proposal — since the handler's own internal crash no longer occurs, `ErrorCorrelationUtil.newCorrelationId()` is no longer called a second time per incident on this path, so the condition-7 exception counter (`BR-PENDING-circuitbreaker-tier0-tier1`) is now accurate on this specific path, not just self-consistent.

| ID | Date Found | Rule (plain English) | Legacy Location | Classification | Owner Decision | Notes |
|----|-----------|----------------------|------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-------|
| BR-FIXED-globalexceptionhandler-null-customexception-crash | 2026-07-17 | The centralized exception handler's generic catch-all passed `null` to `ErrorResponse`'s `CustomException`-typed constructor, which called `.printStackTrace()` on it with no null check — crashing the handler itself and silently losing the original exception's detail/correlation logging, for any of 195 `ResponseEntity`-returning controller methods whose uncaught exception reached this specific branch. Found live via Step 5's wave-1 smoke pass (`PATCH /api/procurements/quotation/grn/approve`), confirmed pre-existing (untouched since the repo's first commit) and unrelated to any track of this session's Java 21 work. **Fixed via a null-guard in `ErrorResponse.java`'s two constructors**, not at the `GlobalExceptionHandler.java` call site. | `legacy/Entity/src/main/java/com/param/common/dto/ErrorResponse.java:26-48` (fix applied here), `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/java/com/param/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java:134` (unaffected call site, confirmed still correct) | Robustness/Reliability — an existing, real defect in production error-handling, not introduced by any refactor; no money calculation touched | Approved | Confirmed the sole `new ErrorResponse(..., null)` call site in the whole reactor (~80 other call sites all pass a real exception, all unaffected by this fix). Confirmed via full git history that `ErrorResponse.java` predates this entire session. Proposal doc `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_GlobalExceptionHandlerNullCustomExceptionCrash.md` reviewed and approved by owner before any code was written. Full reactor: 170/170 (165 baseline + 5 new). |

## BR-FIXED-copay-preauth-split-return-value-gap — FIXED: second-round co-pay/pre-auth adjustment now reaches the patient's own bill total, not just the insurance/cap ledger (2026-07-15)

**FIXED AND MERGED**, per `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_TriagePendingFindingsFixes.md` (finding 1), after owner review of concrete before/after dollar examples for all 7 call sites. `BillingCommonMethods.updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract` now stamps its internally-adjusted `totalSelfPayable`/`totalCreditPayable` onto the returned `consumptionDto` via `setSelfPayable`/`setCreaditPayable` (mirroring the sibling `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForFirstPriorityContract`'s existing pattern), and all 7 call sites in `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` now read the corrected totals back via plain assignment rather than accumulation. Applied and merged into `main` (commit `776a3bc`) prior to the shadow-test wave-2 work documented elsewhere in this file. The original finding write-up is preserved below for the historical record of what was found and why it mattered.

**Found while writing wave-1 shadow-test characterization tests, not while looking for bugs.** Originally logged per the owner's explicit instruction: document clearly, do not fix yet — this was exactly the kind of "preserve vs. fix" decision `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md` Part 3/Section 5 said needed an owner call, not a unilateral AI fix. The owner subsequently reviewed and approved the fix via `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_TriagePendingFindingsFixes.md`.

**What the code does today:** `Global/.../common/BillingCommonMethods.java`'s `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract` (called 7 times from `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` — the highest-fan-in billing calculation in the codebase, see `DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md` Section 1) computes an adjusted credit-payable amount when a second-round contract has its own co-pay percentage (`fromContact=true` branch) or hits a pre-authorization ceiling. That adjusted amount is correctly written to the database — `updatePreAuthAmount`/`updateCapAmount`/`updateServiceCapAmount` all receive the reduced, capped figure. **But the method's two adjusted running totals (`totalSelfPayable`, `totalCreditPayable`) never reach the caller.** They are `Double` method parameters (immutable, passed by value in Java — reassigning them inside the method has no effect on the caller's copy), and the object the method returns (`consumptionDto`) never has `setSelfPayable`/`setCreaditPayable` called on it — only `setContractId`, in one branch. All 7 call sites capture the return value into a local variable and never read `selfPayable`/`creaditPayable` off it afterward.

**Concrete effect:** in any billing scenario involving a second-round contract with (a) its own co-pay percentage, or (b) a pre-authorization amount that gets hit, the insurance/pre-auth/cap ledger in the database will show the correctly-adjusted (smaller) amount consumed — but the patient's own bill, built from the caller's original, unadjusted `totalSelfPayable`/`totalCreditPayable`, will not reflect that same adjustment. The insurance side and the patient-facing side of the same transaction can disagree.

**Severity:** real, not theoretical — confirmed via direct code tracing (not inferred), and confirmed reproducible in a unit test (see `legacy/Global/src/test/java/com/param/global/common/BillingCommonMethodsCharacterizationTest.java`, `ReturnValueGap` test group, 2 passing assertions that pin this exact behavior). Not yet confirmed against a real second-round-contract-with-co-pay or pre-auth-ceiling live scenario (no shadow-test wave 2 / real-data access yet — see `DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md` Decision 3, owner deferred).

**Owner instruction (historical):** at the time this was found, log clearly, do not fix yet. The characterization test existed specifically so that if this was ever fixed (or ever silently changed for any other reason, including as a side effect of an unrelated refactor), the test would fail and force a conscious, logged decision rather than a silent behavior change — which is exactly what happened: the two `ReturnValueGap` tests were updated to assert the corrected behavior as part of the fix commit.

Classification: **Financial Correctness** (fixed). Owner Decision: Reviewed and approved via `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_TriagePendingFindingsFixes.md`, applied exactly as proposed.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test` at fix time: BUILD SUCCESS, 39/39 pass (Global 10/10, Billing 29/29). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. Call-site-level tests for `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl` itself were flagged as not yet existing at fix time; that class now has its own characterization test file (`ContractConsumptionServiceImplCharacterizationTest.java`) added later in the shadow-test wave-2 work, though it does not specifically re-verify this fix's 7 call sites — compilation success across all 7 plus the full Global/Billing suite passing was, and remains, the verification coverage for this fix.

## BR-PENDING-grn-vs-returnable-gatepass-tax-divergence — Known issue, NOT fixed: Procurement's goods-receipt tax calculation and Inventory's goods-return tax calculation are not actually the same formula, despite being treated as one duplicated pattern (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing wave-1 shadow-test characterization tests, not while looking for bugs.** Logged per the owner's explicit instruction (Decision 5 of `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md`): keep flagging inconsistencies as they surface during test-writing, don't fix them, triage together later.

**Background:** `docs/AUDIT_BillingRisk.md` and the original Java-upgrade dependency map both grouped Inventory's and Procurement's `ITaxComponentDetailsDao`-based tax calculations together as "the same tax computation pattern" (Inventory: `ReturnableGatepassService.submitReturnableGatepass`, ~line 1011-1031; Procurement: `GoodReceiptNoteService.approveGoodReceiptNote`, ~line 1752-1776). Re-reading both current sources side by side while writing characterization tests for each (see `legacy/Inventory/src/test/.../ReturnableGatepassServiceCharacterizationTest.java` and `legacy/Procurement/src/test/.../GoodReceiptNoteServiceCharacterizationTest.java`) found they are not actually the same computation. Three confirmed divergences:

1. **Discount netting.** Procurement computes `commTaxAmount = totalAmount - totalDiscount` (defaulting to 0 when `totalDiscount` is null) before applying the tax percentage. Inventory taxes the raw `totalAmt` with no discount subtraction at all — despite `ReturnableGatepassDetail` having its own `totalDiscount` field, confirmed populated elsewhere in the same file (`setTotalDiscount(item.getTotalDiscount())`, 12 call sites in `ReturnableGatepassService.java`) but never read by the tax-computation block. Whether goods receipts (inbound) and goods returns (outbound) are legitimately supposed to net tax differently, or whether one of the two is simply missing a step, is a real business question this session does not answer.
2. **Null-tax-reference handling.** Inventory guards with `detail.getTax()!=null ? detail.getTax().getId() : 0`. Procurement calls `detail.getTax().getId()` directly with no null check and no enclosing try/catch at that specific call (the try/catch only wraps the per-tax-component loop body, not the `getTaxDetails` lookup itself) — a Procurement detail with a null `Tax` reference throws an uncaught `NullPointerException` out of the entire `approveGoodReceiptNote` method today, aborting the whole GRN approval (not just that one detail). Confirmed via a passing test that asserts this exact exception is thrown.
3. **No soft-delete guard.** Inventory's loop skips any detail where `getIsDeleted()` is true. Procurement's equivalent loop has no such check — every detail in the list is processed unconditionally, tax component records included.

**Severity:** real and structurally confirmed (all three verified via passing/failing test assertions, not inferred), but business impact is unclear without a domain decision on whether the two flows are meant to behave identically. The null-tax-reference case (divergence 2) is the most operationally concrete: any goods receipt note with a detail missing its tax assignment will fail its entire approval today with an unhandled exception, not a graceful per-line skip.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to either `ReturnableGatepassService.java` or `GoodReceiptNoteService.java`. The characterization tests exist specifically so that if either formula is ever changed (deliberately or as a side effect of something else), the relevant test fails and forces a conscious, logged decision.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session — same "found while writing tests, log it clearly" precedent as `BR-FIXED-copay-preauth-split-return-value-gap` (subsequently fixed) and `BR-PENDING-documentupload-hardcoded-pdf-owner-password` (still deferred)). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the same standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Update 2026-07-15 (later): divergence 2 (null-tax-reference crash) fixed, owner-approved.** Per `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_GrnNullTaxReferenceCrash.md`, the owner judged this specific sub-finding an active operational risk (unlike divergences 1 and 3, which stay Pending in the slower joint-triage list) and approved the minimal proposed fix. Applied on branch `fix/grn-null-tax-reference-crash`: `GoodReceiptNoteService.java:1752` changed from `grnDao.getTaxDetails(detail.getTax().getId())` to `grnDao.getTaxDetails(detail.getTax()!=null ? detail.getTax().getId():0)` — a one-line change, exactly Inventory's existing equivalent line, calling into the same `IGoodReceiptNoteDao` DAO instance/method. **Classification for this specific change: Behavioral Change** (per RULES.md's own definition — a batch approval that previously failed outright for a null-tax line now succeeds, with tax processing silently skipped for that one line; the common case, a line with a valid tax reference, is completely unchanged). The existing crash-pinning test (`NullTaxReference#throwsNullPointerException_whenTaxIsNull`) was updated to `fallsBackToZero_whenTaxIsNull_insteadOfCrashing`, now asserting the graceful-skip behavior instead of the crash. Verified: `mvn -pl Procurement test` — BUILD SUCCESS, 8/8 pass, from a clean `target/` wipe. Committed locally on the dedicated branch, not pushed — owner to review and push per the established pattern. Divergences 1 (discount netting) and 3 (no soft-delete guard) remain open and unfixed, tracked in `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md`.

## BR-PENDING-collectpayment-unguarded-null-check — Known issue, NOT fixed: one of three SMS-trigger branches in collectPayment is missing the null guard its siblings have, causing a real NullPointerException (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing wave-1 shadow-test characterization tests for entry point #3 (`collectPayment`), not while looking for bugs.** Logged per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5 of `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md`): keep flagging inconsistencies as they surface, don't fix them.

**What the code does today:** `Billing/.../transaction/service/BillingMasterServiceImpl.java`'s `collectPayment` has three near-identical SMS-notification trigger branches, each gated on `reqDto.getIsInterimOrFinalBill()`. Two of the three null-guard the check (`reqDto.getIsInterimOrFinalBill() != null && reqDto.getIsInterimOrFinalBill() == 2 && ...`). The third does not (`reqDto.getIsInterimOrFinalBill() == 1 && ...`, no null check) — a request that leaves `isInterimOrFinalBill` null throws a real `NullPointerException` (Java auto-unboxes the null `Integer` for the `== 1` comparison) on this one branch specifically, when `visitTypeId` is not `OP`. Because the entire method is wrapped in a catch-all (`catch (Exception e) { throw new BillingException(e.getMessage()); }`), the caller never sees a distinguishable NPE — just a generic billing exception with the NPE's message, indistinguishable from any other unexpected failure in this ~480-line method.

**Severity:** real, confirmed via a passing test that asserts the exact exception is thrown (not inferred from reading alone) — `UnguardedNullCheck#throwsWhenIsInterimOrFinalBillIsNullOnTheUnguardedBranch` in `BillingMasterServiceImplCollectPaymentCharacterizationTest.java`. Practical impact depends on whether any real caller ever leaves `isInterimOrFinalBill` unset while `visitTypeId` is not OP — not investigated this session (out of scope; this entry is about the code inconsistency itself, not a live-traffic audit).

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `BillingMasterServiceImpl.java`. The test exists so this exact crash-prone gap can't silently change (fixed or made worse by an unrelated refactor) without a test failing.

**Separately, a scope note, not a bug finding:** `collectPayment` is a ~480-line method that does far more than collect a payment — SMS notifications, an external MyLife/Global-Scheduling HTTP sync, patient loyalty-card consumption, appointment/encounter status updates, and per-payment-mode integration side effects (Mpesa, Pesapal, RazorPay, CCAvenue, POS machine) are all interleaved with the core money arithmetic. This session's characterization tests cover the core arithmetic (the self/credit due-amount split and combination that RISK-1 specifically flags) and the validation gates that guard it, but deliberately do not cover the SMS/external-sync/loyalty/appointment orchestration — flagged here explicitly (per the "no silent caps" principle in `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md`) so this isn't mistaken for full coverage of the method.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session — same precedent as the two prior BR-PENDING entries from this harness effort). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Billing test -Dtest=BillingMasterServiceImplCollectPaymentCharacterizationTest`: BUILD SUCCESS, 13/13 tests pass (verified from a clean `target/` wipe of the whole module). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-consumedeposit-findings — Known issues, NOT fixed: consumeDeposit touches every remaining detail row even after the amount is exhausted, plus two smaller oddities (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing wave-1 shadow-test characterization tests for entry point #4 (`consumeDeposit`), not while looking for bugs.** Logged per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5). This is also the exact method `docs/AUDIT_BillingRisk.md` RISK-3 already flags for the lost-update / absolute-`SET` deposit-balance pattern — these are additional, more granular findings within the same method, not a restatement of RISK-3.

**Finding 1 (the most concrete of the three): the `if(consumedAmount>0)` guard wraps the entire detail-row loop, not each iteration.** `Billing/.../transaction/service/PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImpl.java`'s `consumeDeposit`, inner loop (~line 81-104): once entered (because the deposit's `consumedAmount` started positive), the `while(listIterator.hasNext())` loop runs to the end of that deposit's detail-row list regardless of whether the running `consumedAmount` has already reached 0. Every row after exhaustion still gets a real `iDepositMasterService.updateDepositDetails(...)` call — `consumedAmount` forced to 0, balance numerically unchanged — a real database write that changes nothing, once per remaining row. Confirmed via a passing test (`MultipleDetailRows#stillTouchesLaterRows_withZeroConsumedAmount_onceExhausted`) asserting exactly 2 calls for a 2-row list where the first row alone satisfies the request.

**Finding 2: a null-check that only fires the "wrong" way round.** `if (depositDto.getBillId() != null) depositDto.setBillId(reqDto.getBillingMasterId());` (and the identical pattern for `billPayeeDetailsId`) only overwrites the field when it's *already* non-null — the opposite of the usual "fill in if missing" idiom. A `PaymentDepositDto` arriving with `billId == null` keeps it null; the request's own `billingMasterId` is silently never attached. Whether this is intentional (only stamp a bill reference onto records that already flag they need one) or an inverted condition is a real question this session does not answer. Confirmed via 2 passing tests (`BillIdBackwardsCheck`).

**Finding 3 (minor, likely just dead code): a read-then-write-back with no effect.** `Double detailsConsumedAmount = depositDto.getConsumedAmount(); depositDto.setConsumedAmount(detailsConsumedAmount);` reads the DTO's own field and immediately writes the identical value back — confirmed via a test that this has zero observable effect on what reaches `updateDepositMaster`. Most likely leftover from a refactor rather than a live bug, but noted rather than silently deleted.

**Severity:** Finding 1 is the most operationally relevant (real, redundant database writes at scale, once per unconsumed detail row on every deposit consumption); Findings 2/3 are lower-severity code-quality observations, included because Decision 5 asked for all surfaced inconsistencies to be logged, not just the ones judged significant in the moment.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImpl.java`. All 3 findings are pinned by passing test assertions, not just described in prose.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session — same precedent as the three prior BR-PENDING entries from this harness effort). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Billing test -Dtest=PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImplCharacterizationTest`: BUILD SUCCESS, 8/8 tests pass (verified from a clean `target/` wipe of the whole module, alongside entry point #3's 13 tests -- 21/21 total). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-savebill-misleading-conflict-message — Known issue, NOT fixed: saveBill reports "already in process by another user" even when the real cause is no server-side order data at all (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing wave-1 shadow-test characterization tests for entry point #5 (`saveBill`), not while looking for bugs.** Logged per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5). This is a facet of `docs/AUDIT_BillingRisk.md` ONE-OFF-2 (the ±1.00 whole-unit tolerance) that the original audit text doesn't explicitly call out.

**What the code does today:** `Billing/.../transaction/controller/BillingMasterController.java`'s `saveBill` initializes `checkSameAmount = true` and only ever sets it `false` inside a block gated on `orderDetailsRes.getObject() != null` (the server-side order-sum lookup). If that lookup returns no object at all -- for any reason, not necessarily a genuine concurrent-edit conflict (could be a data gap, a since-deleted order, a query miss) -- `checkSameAmount` never gets the chance to become `false`, and the method unconditionally returns `"This bill is already in process by other user!"`. The message asserts a specific cause (concurrent editing) that the code has not actually verified; it's really "no comparable server-side data was found," a different and potentially more concerning failure mode reported with a misleading, narrower message. Confirmed via a passing test (`NoServerSideData#rejectsOutright_whenServerHasNoMatchingOrderData`).

**Severity:** low-to-moderate -- doesn't cause incorrect billing math, but could send an operator down the wrong troubleshooting path (assuming a colleague has the bill open, when the actual issue might be a data-integrity gap worth investigating directly).

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `BillingMasterController.java`.

**Also confirmed, not a new finding but a direct re-verification of ONE-OFF-2 against current source:** the whole-unit `Math.round(...)` rounding and the ±1.00 tolerance fallback (both the split-bill netAmount-only path and the non-split-bill self+credit-payable path, which requires BOTH legs independently within tolerance) behave exactly as the original audit described -- pinned by 6 passing tests, not just re-read from the audit.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Billing test -Dtest=BillingMasterControllerSaveBillCharacterizationTest`: BUILD SUCCESS, 8/8 tests pass (verified from a clean `target/` wipe of the whole module, alongside entry points #3/#4's 21 tests -- 29/29 total). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-billingcommonmethods-batch1 — Known issues, NOT fixed: 4 findings across taxCalculation, calculateSelfCreditAndFinalAmount, and updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForFirstPriorityContract (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing shadow-test wave-2 characterization tests, Step 2 batch 1 — the first of the "full coverage of Billing/Global/Entity's money-calculation paths" expansion, starting with `BillingCommonMethods.java`.** Not fixed, per the same standing owner instruction (Decision 5 of `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md`): characterize, log, don't fix new findings.

**Finding 1 — `taxCalculation`: with multiple GST bracket rows, the LAST row in the list unconditionally wins, even overwriting an earlier row that correctly applied.** `Global/.../common/BillingCommonMethods.java`'s `taxCalculation` loops over every row `iContractConsumptionDao.getgstDetails(...)` returns and, on every iteration, unconditionally sets `taxId`/`taxPercentage`/`taxAmt` — whether the row's own applicability condition (`isGstApplicable && serviceReteAfterCalculaion >= gstAmountRange`) is true or false. There is no "first match wins" or `break` logic, so if a unit's GST bracket configuration ever has more than one row, the tax actually computed depends entirely on list order, not on which bracket the current amount genuinely falls into — a later non-applicable row silently resets a correct, already-computed tax back to zero. Confirmed via a passing test (`TaxCalculation#lastBracketRowWinsUnconditionally`) using 2 rows where the first applies (would produce a real 18% tax) and the second doesn't (resets to 0%).

**Finding 2 — `taxCalculation`: a null `gstAmountRange` on any bracket row throws an uncaught `NullPointerException`.** The comparison `serviceReteAfterCalculaion >= gstList.getGstAmountRange()` auto-unboxes the boxed `Integer` return of `getGstAmountRange()`; a null value crashes the method entirely rather than skipping that row. Confirmed via a passing test (`TaxCalculation#nullAmountRangeThrowsNpe`).

**Finding 3 — `calculateSelfCreditAndFinalAmount`: in the `isTariffRateApplicable != 'Y'` branch, when `rate >= contractRate`, `creaditPayable` is never reassigned at all.** `Global/.../common/BillingCommonMethods.java`'s `calculateSelfCreditAndFinalAmount`, the non-tariff branch's `rate >= contractRate` case only updates `selfPayable` (absorbing the excess over the pre-existing contract price) — the ternary controlling `creaditPayable` in this same case (`rate>=contractRate ? resDto.getCreaditPayable() : ...`) evaluates to whatever `creaditPayable` already held on the `resDto` object going into the call, completely disconnected from the freshly computed `finalPrice`. Confirmed via a passing test (`SelfCreditAndFinalAmountCalculation#consultationService_tariffNotApplicable_rateAboveContractRate`) that sets an arbitrary sentinel value (333.0) on `creaditPayable` before the call and confirms it comes back unchanged.

**Finding 4 — `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForFirstPriorityContract`: the `checkFlagUpdateCapOrServiceCap=true` branch is missing the `consumptionDto.getUpdateCap() == null` guard that its own `checkFlagUpdateCapOrServiceCap=false` branch has, and that the sibling second-round method's equivalent branch also has.** `Global/.../common/BillingCommonMethods.java`, lines ~296-302: `if (checkFlagUpdateCapOrServiceCap) { if (creditPayable > 0) { ... iContractConsumptionDao.updateCapAmount(consumptionDto, creditPayable); } }` — only checks `creditPayable > 0`, with no `updateCap==null` guard at all. Contrast with the `else` branch two lines later (`else if (consumptionDto.getUpdateCap() == null && creditPayable > 0)`), and with `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract`'s equivalent `checkFlagUpdateCapOrServiceCap=true` branch (`if (consumptionDto.getUpdateCap() == null && secondRound.getBalanceCapAmount() != null && creditPayable > 0)`), both of which do guard on it. This means: whenever this specific method is called with `checkFlag=true` and `consumptionDto.updateCap` already set (non-null — the exact condition every other guarded branch in this codebase treats as "skip all writes, this consumption was already processed"), this one branch writes to the database anyway. Confirmed via 2 contrasting passing tests (`FirstPriorityContractAdjustment#checkFlagTrueBranch_missingTheUpdateCapGuard` vs. `#checkFlagFalseBranch_respectsTheUpdateCapGuard`).

**Severity:** Finding 1 is the most operationally concrete of the four — a real, silent, order-dependent tax miscalculation risk for any billing unit configured with more than one GST bracket row (not investigated this session whether any unit's real configuration has multiple rows). Finding 4 is a real double-write risk for a shared financial write-guard mechanism used throughout this class. Findings 2 and 3 are lower-probability edge cases (a null data value; a specific sub-branch of a larger conditional), included per Decision 5's "log every surfaced inconsistency" instruction, not because they're judged equally urgent.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `BillingCommonMethods.java`. All 4 findings are pinned by passing test assertions (25 tests total in this batch — 10 pre-existing plus 15 new — all passing), not just described in prose.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session — same precedent as the prior BR-PENDING entries from this harness effort). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 25/25, Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. This is batch 1 of an explicitly open-ended Step 2 expansion (`BillingCommonMethods.java` has ~30 public methods; `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` has ~16, several thousands of lines each — full coverage will take multiple further batches, reported incrementally per owner instruction).

## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-billingcommonmethods-batch2 — Known issues, NOT fixed: a money-conservation bug in updatePreAuthForPharmacyContract (flagged live, urgent) plus 5 further findings across the package-consumption-cap methods (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing shadow-test wave-2 characterization tests, Step 2 batch 2.** Not fixed, per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5). Covers `updatePreAuthForPharmacyContract`, `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageCap`, `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageServiceCap`, and `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperForMultiEncounter`, all in `Global/.../common/BillingCommonMethods.java`.

**Finding 1 — flagged to the owner live, mid-batch, as genuinely urgent: `updatePreAuthForPharmacyContract` loses money from the combined total whenever a co-share percentage applies.** Lines 607-610:
```java
if (resDto.getCoShare() != null) {
    creditPayable = GlobalCommonDateUtils.getAmount(creditPayable - (creditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare() / 100)));
    selfPayable = GlobalCommonDateUtils.getAmount(selfPayable + (creditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare() / 100)));
}
```
Line 1 reassigns `creditPayable` to the reduced value *first*. Line 2 then computes the amount added to `selfPayable` off that **already-reduced** `creditPayable`, not the original amount the co-share percentage was meant to apply to. Concrete example (confirmed via a passing test, `PreAuthForPharmacyContract#coShareAdjustment_moneyConservationBug`): `creditPayable=$1000`, `coShare=20%` → `creditPayable` becomes `$800` (correct: 20% of $1000 removed) → `selfPayable` becomes `$160` (20% of the already-reduced $800, not $1000) → **the combined total shrinks from $1000 to $960; $40 has simply disappeared**, not merely disagreed between two places the way Finding 1 (the co-pay/pre-auth split gap, fixed earlier today) did. This is a live pharmacy-billing pre-authorization calculation.

**Finding 2:** in the same method, a `preauthId` set on `resDto` but absent from the `preAuthDetails` map throws an uncaught `NullPointerException` via auto-unboxing (`preAuthDetails.get(resDto.getPreauthId()) >= 0`). Confirmed via a passing test.

**Finding 3:** `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageCap` accumulates `consumptionDto.packageConsumptionAmt` by the line's `serviceRate`, while its near-identically-named sibling `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageServiceCap` accumulates the same field by `consumeAmt` instead — two methods with parallel structure and purpose use a different basis for the same running total. Not necessarily wrong (the two may track conceptually different things — package-level cap vs. service-level cap consumption), but worth an owner's confirmation. Confirmed via passing tests in both nested test classes.

**Finding 4:** `createOrderDetailsPackageMapperForMultiEncounter`'s `availableQty >= serviceQty` branch silently discards the caller's `setPkgAmt` flag — if `setPkgAmt=true`, `packageAmount` is initially set from `consumptionDto.getSelfPayable()`, but this branch unconditionally overwrites it a few lines later with the freshly computed `ordTotalAmount`, regardless of what the caller asked for. Confirmed via a passing test using a sentinel value (999.0) that never survives to the returned dto.

**Finding 5 (the more concrete of the two branch inconsistencies): the same method's `availableQty < serviceQty` (partial-fulfillment) branch hardcodes `packageQty`, the cap-DAO deduction, and the service detail's remaining balance to `1`, `-1`, and `0` respectively — not derived from the actual remaining quantity being processed.** With `availableQty=3` and `serviceQty=5` (2 units still genuinely needed after exhausting the 3 available), the method still reports `packageQty=1`, deducts only `1` unit via `updatePackageQty(...,-1)`, and zeroes the service detail's balance regardless of the real remaining amount. Confirmed via a passing test.

**Finding 6:** the two branches of the same method derive `netAmount` via genuinely different formulas — the `availableQty >= serviceQty` branch sets `netAmount = consumptionDto.getFinalPrice()` (an unrelated, pre-existing carried-over value, no arithmetic at all), while the `availableQty < serviceQty` branch computes `netAmount = ordTotalAmount - packageConsumptionAmt` (a real subtraction). Confirmed via passing tests in both branches.

**Severity:** Finding 1 is judged the most urgent of this batch — a real, silent money-conservation defect in a live pharmacy pre-authorization calculation, flagged to the owner immediately rather than held for a batch summary. Finding 5 is the second most concrete — hardcoded values that don't track actual partial-fulfillment quantities look like a real defect, not a deliberate simplification, though not investigated further this session. Findings 2, 3, 4, 6 are lower-probability edge cases or design-consistency observations, included per Decision 5's "log every surfaced inconsistency" instruction.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix (all 6, including Finding 1 -- the owner may choose to fast-track any of these separately, same process as the GRN crash and batch-1 finding 4 fast-tracks, but no such request has been made for this batch as of this entry). No code change made to `BillingCommonMethods.java`. All 6 findings are pinned by passing test assertions (11 new tests this batch, 36 total in the file), not just described in prose.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 36/36, Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. This is batch 2 of the open-ended Step 2 expansion; `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` (the much larger target, ~16 methods, several thousand lines each) has not yet been started.

## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-billingcommonmethods-batch3 — Known issues, NOT fixed: 2 findings across the last 5 real-calculation methods in BillingCommonMethods.java (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing shadow-test wave-2 characterization tests, Step 2 batch 3 — the last batch of real-calculation methods in `BillingCommonMethods.java`.** Not fixed, per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5). Covers both `createOrderDetailsPackageMapper` overloads, `consumeServiceFromPackageOrContract`, `createOrderPackageMapperForPharmacyObj`, and `createSaleDetailPackageMapperDto`.

**Finding 1:** `createOrderPackageMapperForPharmacyObj` computes `serviceAmount` via two genuinely different formulas depending on whether `billingMasterDto` is null — `BigDecimal.valueOf(mppr.getRate())` (rate alone) when non-null, `BigDecimal.valueOf(mppr.getRate() * detail.getOrderQty())` (rate × quantity) when null. Whether the line's quantity is factored into the dollar amount depends entirely on an unrelated null-check, not on any explicit "should this be quantity-multiplied" signal. Confirmed via 2 passing tests, one per branch, using the same rate/quantity inputs to show the two different results directly (50.0 vs 250.0 for a 5-unit quantity).

**Finding 2:** `consumeServiceFromPackageOrContract` forces both `ordDiscountPer` and `patientClassPercentage` to zero whenever the order detail's `serviceAmt <= 0`, discarding whatever those two fields were actually configured to (both fields are gated by the identical single condition, rather than being independently preserved). Confirmed via a passing test showing configured non-zero values (15%, 25%) silently dropped to 0.00 on a zero-priced line.

**Severity:** neither finding was judged to meet the bar for an immediate/urgent flag (per the standing instruction, reserved for provable, unambiguous real-dollar defects like batch-1 finding 4's double-write or batch-2 finding 1's money-conservation leak — both of which had a clean, provable invariant showing the current behavior is wrong). Finding 1 is a genuine inconsistency, but which formula is "correct" (or whether both are, for genuinely different call contexts) is a business question, not something derivable from the code alone — the same category as findings 2a/4b already sitting in the slower triage list. Finding 2 is arguably defensible (a free/zero-priced line item may have nothing meaningful to discount) but is included per Decision 5's "log every surfaced inconsistency" instruction.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `BillingCommonMethods.java`. Both findings are pinned by passing test assertions (13 new tests this batch, 49 total in the file), not just described in prose.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 49/49, Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. This closes out the real-calculation-method coverage of `BillingCommonMethods.java` (batches 1-3, 8 methods total, plus the two pre-auth/cap-split methods and their fixes from wave 1/Finding 1). Remaining in this file: 3 thin wrapper methods delegating to already-tested logic, `saveOrderMaster` (real branching but not dollar-math), and 16 pure DTO-construction methods -- the latter explicitly out of scope for this mandate, see the follow-up entry noting that decision.

## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-billingcommonmethods-batch4 — URGENT finding: updatePackageCap silently ignores its own capAmount parameter for every package item after the first; plus a saveOrderMaster crash finding; plus explicit scope decision on the remaining 16 methods (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing shadow-test wave-2 characterization tests, Step 2 batch 4 — the last batch of `BillingCommonMethods.java` coverage: the 3 thin wrapper methods (`updatePackageServiceCap`, `updatePackageCap`, `updatePackageForMultiEncounter`) and `saveOrderMaster`.**

**Finding 1 — URGENT, flagged to the owner live, mid-batch: `updatePackageCap`'s non-empty-list branch silently discards its own `capAmount` parameter and substitutes `consumptionDto.getSelfPayable()` instead.**

`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/common/BillingCommonMethods.java:376-382`:
```java
public ContractConsumptionDto updatePackageCap(PackageConsumptionMasterResDto resDto, TPackageConsumptionCapDetailsDto capDtls, ContractConsumptionDto consumptionDto, double capAmount) {
    if (consumptionDto.getOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtosList() != null && !consumptionDto.getOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtosList().isEmpty()) {
        consumptionDto.getOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtosList().add(createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageCap(resDto, capDtls, consumptionDto, consumptionDto.getSelfPayable()));
    } else {
        List<OrderDetailsPackageMapperDto> mppr = new ArrayList<>();
        mppr.add(createOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtoAndUpdatePackageCap(resDto, capDtls, consumptionDto, capAmount));
        consumptionDto.setOrderDetailsPackageMapperDtosList(mppr);
    }
    return consumptionDto;
}
```

The `else` branch (the **first** package item added to a `consumptionDto`) correctly uses the `capAmount` parameter the caller passed in. The `if` branch (**every** package item added after the first) ignores `capAmount` entirely and uses `consumptionDto.getSelfPayable()` instead — an unrelated field (the patient's self-pay total, not "how much of this line's package cap to consume"). That substituted value flows directly into `iTPackageConsumptionCapDetailsDao.updatePackageCap(capDetailsId, consumeAmt * -1)` — a real database write that reduces the package's remaining cap balance. For any consumption with more than one package line item, every item after the first has its cap deduction determined by `selfPayable`, not by the amount actually intended for that line.

Discovered because the characterization test crashed with a `NullPointerException` on `consumeAmt` (since the test's `consumptionDto.getSelfPayable()` was unset/null) — in real usage `selfPayable` is virtually always some non-null dollar figure, so this doesn't crash in production, it just silently records the wrong cap deduction. Confirmed via a passing test (`UpdatePackageCapWrapper#nonEmptyList_ignoresCapAmountParameter_usesSelfPayableInstead`) demonstrating the database write reflects `selfPayable` (999.0 in the test) instead of the caller's actual `capAmount` (75.0).

**Finding 2:** `saveOrderMaster` crashes with an uncaught `NullPointerException` whenever `getOrderIdForLabService` finds an existing order, for any unit ID not already present in `TimeZoneComponent`'s static in-memory timezone map. That map is only populated by `TimeZoneComponent`'s own `@PostConstruct init()` during a live Spring app's startup; `getDateByzone`'s own internal try/catch swallows the `ZoneId.of(null)` failure and returns `null` instead of throwing, but the null `Date` is then handed to `SimpleDateFormat.format(...)` back in `saveOrderMaster`, which is **not** wrapped in any local try/catch at that point, so the crash propagates all the way out. Confirmed via a passing test (`SaveOrderMasterDedup#labServiceWithExistingOrderFound_crashesOnUncachedUnitTimezone`).

**Severity:** Finding 1 is judged the most severe of the two — a real, silent, active dollar-amount substitution in a database write that directly affects a package's cap balance, same class of urgency as the pharmacy co-share money leak and the missing updateCap guard already fixed this session. Finding 2 is lower-probability (requires both a lab-service line item and an existing same-encounter order, plus an uncached unit) but a real, uncaught crash risk.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix (Finding 1 flagged live for the owner's awareness; whether it gets the same fast-track treatment as Finding 4 and the pharmacy money leak is the owner's call, not yet requested for this one as of this entry). No code change made to `BillingCommonMethods.java`. Both findings are pinned by passing test assertions (10 new tests this batch, 58 total in the file).

**Explicit scope decision, recorded per owner instruction (not a silent gap): the remaining 16 pure DTO-construction methods in `BillingCommonMethods.java` are excluded from this shadow-test harness expansion.** These methods (`createOrderDetailsPackageMapper` (132), `createOrderDetailsPayeeMapperDtos`, `createUpdatePayeeDto`, `createPackageRateFromServiceForPackageDto`, `createBillingMasterDto`, `createContractConsumption`, `createContractConsumptionForClone`, `createAdmissionListIpdChargesResDto`, `createServiceSearchResDto` (both overloads), `createEncounterMasterDto`, `createOrderMasterDto`, `createSaleDetailsPayeeObj`, `createOrderPayeeMapperObj`, `setDefaultOrderDetailsParameter`, `createOrderDetaildPayeeObjFinal`) were read in full and confirmed to contain field-copying/DTO-assembly logic only — no arithmetic on money fields. The owner's explicit decision: this is a different risk category (field-mapping correctness, not calculation correctness) and is out of scope for this mandate ("full coverage of Billing/Global/Entity's **money-calculation** paths"). This closes out `BillingCommonMethods.java` for Step 2's purposes: all real-calculation methods across batches 1-4 (10 methods) plus the two pre-auth/cap-split methods from wave 1 (12 methods total) are now characterized; the 16 DTO-construction methods are a recorded, deliberate exclusion, not an oversight.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred (findings), explicitly out of scope (the 16 DTO methods).

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 58/58, Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe, twice, to confirm no residual flakiness after the two test corrections this batch required). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-contractconsumption-smallmethods — Known issue, NOT fixed: consumeServiceFromContractAfterTariffChange's row loop lets the last row silently win, same defect class as batch-1 finding 1 (2026-07-15)

**Found while writing shadow-test wave-2 characterization tests for `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`'s 13 small methods (Step 3, first sub-task).** Not fixed, per the standing owner instruction (Decision 5). This entry covers only the 13 small methods (~300 lines combined); the 3 giant methods in the same class (`consumeServiceFromContract`, `consumeServiceFromContractPackage`, `consumePackageFromContract`) are handled separately with a targeted, branch-prioritized approach, reported in their own entries.

**Finding — `consumeServiceFromContractAfterTariffChange`: with multiple rows returned by `getServiceDetailsByContractIdAfterTariffChange`, only the LAST row's `serviceRate` survives; every earlier row is silently discarded.** `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`, lines 3297-3312:
```java
List<ContractConsumptionDto> serviceDetailslist = iContractConsumptionDao.getServiceDetailsByContractIdAfterTariffChange(consumptionDto);
for(ContractConsumptionDto resDto : serviceDetailslist) {
    finalDto.setServiceRate(resDto.getServiceRate());
}
return new Response(SUCCESS, SUCCESS_CODE, "Service is not mapped with the tariff..!!", null, finalDto);
```
The loop unconditionally overwrites `finalDto`'s `serviceRate` on every iteration with no filtering, no `break`, and no "first/best match" logic — whatever row the DAO happens to return last determines the tariff-changed rate returned to the caller, regardless of list order having any real meaning. This is the same defect shape as batch-1 finding 1 (`taxCalculation`'s GST bracket loop) — a second, independent instance of "last item in a loop silently wins" found in this same class hierarchy. Confirmed via a passing test (`ConsumeServiceFromContractAfterTariffChange#multipleRows_lastServiceRateWins`) using 2 rows with different rates, asserting the returned dto's `serviceRate` matches only the second row's value.

**Severity:** moderate — this method's own return message ("Service is not mapped with the tariff") suggests it's meant to run when the DAO returns rows needing reconciliation after a tariff change; if that lookup ever legitimately returns more than one row (not confirmed this session whether it does in current production data), the rate actually used is determined by result-set ordering, not by any business rule distinguishing the rows.

**Owner instruction:** log clearly, do not fix. No code change made to `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`. Confirmed via a passing test assertion, not just described in prose. The other 12 small methods in this batch (`resetContractCapping`, `addCapAmount`, `addCapContract`, `addCapPackage`, `getActivePaymentEntitlement`, `isConsultationAmountPaidBySelf`, `getPatientClassByVisit`, `getIsBedChargesService`, `getGstApplicableForBillingBedCategory`, `getGstDetails`, `getSerivceRateGlobalAfterTariffChange`, `getSerivceRateGlobal`) were characterized with no further findings surfaced — their current behavior (including `getSerivceRateGlobal`'s consultation-rate lookup, its consultationAmount-already-set skip path, and its payee/package mapper construction) matches what a straightforward reading of the code would predict, pinned by passing tests but not flagged as defects.

**Note on `getSerivceRateGlobal`/`getSerivceRateGlobalAfterTariffChange` test approach:** `getSerivceRateGlobal` calls `this.consumeServiceFromContract(...)` internally — the 1,842-line giant method not yet characterized. Rather than block this batch on that method, its tests use `Mockito.spy(service)` with `doReturn(...).when(spyService).consumeServiceFromContract(dto)` to stub only that one delegate call, isolating `getSerivceRateGlobal`'s own logic (the surrounding lookups and mapper construction) from the uncharacterized method it happens to call into. `getSerivceRateGlobalAfterTariffChange` delegates to `consumeServiceFromContractAfterTariffChange`, which IS one of the 13 small methods and already characterized above, so that test lets the real delegate run.

Classification: Neither (known-issue log only, no code change this session — same precedent as the prior BR-PENDING entries from this harness effort). Owner Decision: Explicitly deferred, per the standing Decision 5 instruction.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 62/62 (26 new tests this batch, 36 pre-existing from `BillingCommonMethodsCharacterizationTest`), Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. This is the first sub-task of Step 3 (`ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`'s 13 small methods); the 3 giant methods are reported separately.

## BR-FIXED-consumeservicefromcontract-balancecap-swap — FIXED: consumeServiceFromContract no longer swaps self/credit payable when a type==2 amount-capped contract's balance is exceeded (2026-07-15)

**Found while mapping `consumeServiceFromContract`'s branches for targeted shadow-test characterization (Step 3, giant-method pass, first sub-task paused to fast-track this).** Flagged live to the owner the moment it was found, proposed in `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ConsumeServiceFromContractBalanceCapSwap.md`, reviewed and approved, then applied exactly as proposed.

**The bug:** `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:744-745`, inside the `resDto.getType()==2` (service-cap) branch's amount-tracked balance-cap handling. When a service's net amount exceeded the contract's remaining balance cap, `creaditPayable`/`selfPayable` were computed backwards — the patient (`selfPayable`) was billed the cap amount, and the contract/insurer (`creaditPayable`) was billed only the leftover overage. The sibling `resDto.getType()==1` cap branch 150 lines earlier in the same method (lines 590-593) has the correct, opposite semantics: contract absorbs up to the cap, patient absorbs the overage.

**Before:**
```java
creaditPayable = netAmt < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? netAmt : netAmt-resDto.getBalanceAmount();
selfPayable = netAmt < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? 0.0 : resDto.getBalanceAmount();
```

**After (applied):**
```java
creaditPayable = netAmt < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? netAmt : resDto.getBalanceAmount();
selfPayable = netAmt < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? 0.0 : netAmt-resDto.getBalanceAmount();
```

Only the else-branch (second operand) of each ternary was swapped; the already-correct "cap not exceeded" true-branches were untouched, as was the sibling type==1 branch and the quantity-capped sibling immediately below this one.

**Example:** service costs $500, contract has $300 left on its balance cap. Before: contract billed $200 (the overage), patient billed $300 (the cap). After: contract billed $300 (up to the cap), patient billed $200 (the overage). Combined total was always conserved ($500 either way) — this was a misallocation bug, not a money-vanishes bug like the pharmacy co-share leak.

**Owner instruction:** fast-tracked, same treatment as Finding 4 and the pharmacy co-share leak. Approved and applied exactly as proposed. Confirmed via `ConsumeServiceFromContractBalanceCapFixTest#balanceCapExceeded_creditGetsCapAmount_selfGetsOverage` (renamed from `ConsumeServiceFromContractUrgentFindingTest` now that the finding is fixed rather than pending), asserting the corrected call arguments (`creaditPayable=300.0, selfPayable=200.0`).

Classification: **Financial Correctness**. Owner Decision: Approved and applied, per `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ConsumeServiceFromContractBalanceCapSwap.md`.

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Global,Billing -am clean test`: BUILD SUCCESS, Global 85/85, Billing 29/29 (verified from a clean `target/` wipe). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. The broader targeted characterization of `consumeServiceFromContract`'s remaining risky branches (and of `consumeServiceFromContractPackage`/`consumePackageFromContract`) remains paused pending owner direction, unrelated to this fix's completion.

## BR-FIXED-shadow-wave2-consumeservicefromcontract-batch1 — FIXED: second-round quantity-cap branch no longer discards the first-priority contract's credit contribution; 1 secondary finding remains open (2026-07-16)

**Found while resuming targeted characterization of `consumeServiceFromContract` (Step 2 resumed), specifically while checking the whole method for repeats of the balance-cap swap pattern fixed 2026-07-15.** No repeat of that exact pattern was found — every other `creaditPayable`/`selfPayable` assignment pair in the method was traced and confirmed correct in direction. This is a *different* defect, found in the adjacent second-round quantity-cap branch.

**Finding 1 — FIXED, fast-tracked and applied same-day: `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:1103-1108`, the second-round loop's quantity-tracked-cap branch REASSIGNED `totalCreditPayable`/`totalSelfPayable` instead of accumulating onto them, discarding whatever the first-priority contract already contributed.**

Before:
```java
if(secondRound.getBalanceQuantity()!=null && secondRound.getBalanceQuantity()>0)
{
    orderDetailsPayeeMapperDtosList=new LinkedList<>();
    creaditPayableSecondRound = netAmt;
    totalCreditPayable = netAmt;
    totalSelfPayable = 0.0;
```

After (applied):
```java
    creaditPayableSecondRound = netAmt;
    totalCreditPayable = totalCreditPayable+creaditPayableSecondRound;
    totalSelfPayable = totalSelfPayable-creaditPayableSecondRound;
```

Now matches its sibling branch nine lines earlier (the amount-tracked-cap branch, lines 1015/1023-1024), which already accumulated correctly. Confirmed via `ConsumeServiceFromContractCharacterizationTest.SecondRoundTypeTwoQuantityCap#quantityCapBranch_accumulatesOntoRunningTotal`: a first-priority contract's $300 contribution, plus this round's own $400, now correctly totals $700 (was $400 before the fix — the $300 vanished).

**Severity (at time of finding):** same class as the pharmacy co-share leak — a real dollar amount vanished from the system's own running total, not merely landing on the wrong party.

**Secondary, non-urgent finding in the same branch — still open, NOT fixed:** unlike the resDto-level quantity-cap branch (lines 798-826), which properly compares `resDto.getBalanceQuantity()` against `consumptionDto.getQuantity()` and splits proportionally when insufficient, this second-round branch still has no such comparison at all — any positive `secondRound.getBalanceQuantity()` is treated as fully covering the entire `netAmt`, and `1.00` is hardcoded as the quantity-to-deduct regardless of how much was actually needed. Same shape as the previously-logged batch-2 hardcoded-quantity findings (`createOrderDetailsPackageMapperForMultiEncounter`). Deliberately NOT folded into the urgent fix — a separate, more involved judgment call about how a genuinely-insufficient quantity cap should split. The now-passing test's own comment notes that with the fix applied, `totalSelfPayable` can go negative (200-400=-200) precisely because of this still-open gap.

**Owner instruction:** Finding 1 approved and applied exactly as proposed in `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_SecondRoundQuantityCapCreditLoss.md`, same fast-track as the pharmacy leak and the balance-cap swap. The secondary finding remains logged, not fixed, per standing Decision 5.

**Also characterized this batch, no further findings:** `resDto.getType()==1` consultation routing into `calculateSelfCreditAndFinalAmount`; `resDto.getType()==2` quantity-cap-exceeded split (correctly splits available-vs-excess units, not a repeat of the balance-cap swap); second-round loop `secondRound.getType()==1`; second-round loop `secondRound.getType()==2` amount-cap (correctly caps at the smaller of leftover self-pay or the second contract's own remaining balance).

Classification: **Financial Correctness** (Finding 1, fixed). Owner Decision: Approved and applied, per `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_SecondRoundQuantityCapCreditLoss.md`.

    totalCreditPayable = totalCreditPayable+creaditPayableSecondRound;
    totalSelfPayable = totalSelfPayable-creaditPayableSecondRound;
```

## BR-FIXED-shadow-wave2-consumeservicefromcontractpackage-batch1 — FIXED: same second-round quantity-cap credit-loss bug repeats in consumeServiceFromContractPackage, fast-tracked and applied same-day (2026-07-16)

**Found via a targeted pattern sweep** (owner-requested, given this is the 3rd instance of an overwrite-vs-accumulate bug shape found this session) across `BillingCommonMethods.java` and `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java` for every `totalCreditPayable`/`totalSelfPayable`/`creaditPayableSecondRound` assignment. Result: exactly one repeat found, in `consumeServiceFromContractPackage`'s own second-round quantity-cap branch — the direct sibling of the branch just fixed in `consumeServiceFromContract`. `consumePackageFromContract`'s equivalent second-round block was also checked and has no repeat (only the correct ternary-based patterns). `BillingCommonMethods.java` has no local running totals of its own (only receives/returns them as parameters, already correct).

**FIXED, fast-tracked and applied same-day: `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:2475-2478`, the second-round loop's quantity-tracked-cap branch (this method's own version), REASSIGNED `totalCreditPayable`/`totalSelfPayable` instead of accumulating onto them.**

```java
creaditPayableSecondRound = rate;
totalCreditPayable = rate;
totalSelfPayable = 0.0;
```

creaditPayableSecondRound = rate;

## BR-FIXED-shadow-wave2-consumeservicefromcontractpackage-amountcap-duplicate — FIXED: consumeServiceFromContractPackage's amount-cap branch no longer duplicates the cap formula into selfPayable, overcharging the patient (2026-07-16)

**Found while building the fix-confirming test for the second-round quantity-cap credit-loss repeat, in the same method's round-1 branch.** A different defect shape from the accumulate-vs-reassign pattern — not a repeat, a distinct new finding.

**FIXED, fast-tracked and applied same-day: `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:2204-2207`, round-1, `resDto.getType()==2`, amount-tracked-balance-cap branch, duplicated the cap-amount formula into BOTH `creaditPayable` and `selfPayable` instead of giving the patient the overage.**

Before:
```java
creaditPayable = rate < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? rate : resDto.getBalanceAmount();
selfPayable = rate < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? 0.0 : resDto.getBalanceAmount();
```

After (applied):
```java
creaditPayable = rate < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? rate : resDto.getBalanceAmount();
selfPayable = rate < resDto.getBalanceAmount() ? 0.0 : rate-resDto.getBalanceAmount();
```

The dead, commented-out code three lines below the live ternaries showed exactly what was originally intended (`selfPayable=rate-resDto.getBalanceAmount()`) — someone converted an if/else into a ternary and copy-pasted the wrong right-hand side into the second line. Confirmed via `ConsumeServiceFromContractPackageAmountCapFixTest#balanceCapExceeded_creditGetsCapAmount_selfGetsOverage`: rate=$500, remaining cap=$300 -> `creaditPayable=$300` (correct, unchanged by this fix), `selfPayable=$200` (was $300 before the fix).

**Severity (at time of finding):** distinct from the accumulate-vs-reassign bugs — this one doesn't discard money, it **creates** it: the combined total booked ($600) exceeded the actual service cost ($500) by $100, with the extra $100 landing on the patient.

**Secondary, non-urgent finding in the same area — still open, NOT fixed:** `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:2189`, three lines above the fixed branch, `patientClassDto.getMarkupStatus()` is called with no null-check on `patientClassDto` itself (`patientClassDto = iContractConsumptionDao.getPatientClassAndPercentageByVisit(consumptionDto); if(patientClassDto.getMarkupStatus()!=null...)`), unlike the sibling `resDto.getType()==1` branch a few lines up, which does check `patientClassDto != null` first. A real NPE-crash risk if that DAO call ever legitimately returns null, worked around via a mock in the test, not fixed — same category as previously-logged crash-only findings (e.g. the pharmacy `preauthId` NPE, `saveOrderMaster`'s timezone crash).

**Owner instruction:** approved and applied immediately, same fast-track as the pharmacy leak, balance-cap swap, and the two second-round quantity-cap fixes. No separate `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_*.md` was written — the owner pre-approved applying it "same process" after the quick before/after summary.

Classification: **Financial Correctness** (fixed). Owner Decision: Approved and applied.

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 132/132 across all modules (Global 86/86 on this branch -- does not yet include the sibling fixes' or batch1's tests, each held on its own separate branch per standing instruction; Billing 29/29; plus 17 pre-existing elsewhere). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-FIXED-shadow-wave2-consumeservicefromcontractpackage-coshare-leak — FIXED: consumeServiceFromContractPackage's cap-defined coShare split no longer leaks money, same defect shape as the session's first fix (2026-07-16)

**Found via an agent-assisted sweep for repeats of the coShare money-conservation bug pattern fixed as the very first fix of this whole session** (`updatePreAuthForPharmacyContract`'s coShare adjustment — see `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_PharmacyContractCoShareMoneyLeak.md`). Same defect shape, different method: a self-payable delta computed off an already-reassigned credit-payable instead of the original pre-adjustment value.

**FIXED, fast-tracked and applied same-day: `Global/.../billing/service/ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java:2106-2107`, round-1, `resDto.getType()==1`, `resDto.getBalanceCapAmount()!=null` branch's coShare adjustment.**

Before:
```java
creaditPayable=resDto.getCoShare()!=null && resDto.getCoShare()>0 ? creaditPayable - (creaditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare()*0.01)) :creaditPayable ;
selfPayable= resDto.getCoShare()!=null && resDto.getCoShare()>0 ? selfPayable + (creaditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare()*0.01)) :selfPayable ;
```

After (applied — pure line-order swap, same fix shape as the pharmacy leak):
```java
selfPayable= resDto.getCoShare()!=null && resDto.getCoShare()>0 ? selfPayable + (creaditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare()*0.01)) :selfPayable ;
creaditPayable=resDto.getCoShare()!=null && resDto.getCoShare()>0 ? creaditPayable - (creaditPayable * (resDto.getCoShare()*0.01)) :creaditPayable ;
```

`selfPayable` is now computed first, from the still-original `creaditPayable`, before `creaditPayable` is reassigned — exactly the pattern already correct at lines 2056-2058 of this same method and in the already-fixed `updatePreAuthForPharmacyContract`. Confirmed via `ConsumeServiceFromContractPackageCoShareFixTest#capDefinedCoShareSplit_conservesTotal`: `creaditPayable=$1000`, cap=`$800`, coShare=`20%` → `creaditPayable=$640`, `selfPayable=$360` (was $328 before the fix), total correctly conserved at $1000 (was $968 — $32 vanished).

**Severity (at time of finding):** same class as the pharmacy co-share leak — a real dollar amount vanished from the combined total, not merely misallocated.

**Owner instruction:** approved and applied immediately, same fast-track as the pharmacy leak, balance-cap swap, and the other consumeServiceFromContract(Package) fixes this batch. No separate `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_*.md` was written — the owner pre-approved applying it "same process" after the quick before/after summary, matching the pattern established for the earlier same-day repeats.
