## BR-PENDING-shadow-wave2-consumepackagefromcontract-batch1 — `consumePackageFromContract` swept and characterized: no findings, structurally simpler than its two sibling giant methods (2026-07-16)

**Third and final giant method in `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`.** An agent-assisted sweep of this entire ~445-line method (`public Response consumePackageFromContract(ServiceSearchResDto searchResDto)`) for both bug shapes found this session — reassign-vs-accumulate running totals, and duplicate/stale-variable formula pairs — found **no instances of either**.

Structurally, this method is simpler than its two siblings: it has **only `type==1` (flat contract-rate) logic** in both round 1 and round 2 — no `type==2`/days-or-quantity-cap branches at all, and (notably) **no coShare adjustment step** on its cap-defined credit/self split, unlike the near-identical branch in `consumeServiceFromContractPackage` that was found leaking money this session (`BR-FIXED-shadow-wave2-consumeservicefromcontractpackage-coshare-leak`). Only 2 risky-method call sites exist in the whole method: `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForFirstPriorityContract` (line ~2904) and `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract` (line ~3009), both in cap-defined branches whose credit/self split ternaries were traced and confirmed correct, matching the already-proven-correct pattern used elsewhere in this class (`selfPayable` gets the overage, `creaditPayable` gets the cap amount — the correct direction).

Direct test coverage added for the round-1 cap-defined branch (`Round1CapDefinedSplit#capExceeded_creditGetsCapAmount_selfGetsOverage`), confirming `creaditPayable=$800`/`selfPayable=$200` for a `$1000`/`$800`-cap example. The round-2 equivalent branch was swept and confirmed correct but not given its own dedicated test, given zero risk identified and the sweep's confirmation this method has no coShare step to leak money through.

## BR-PENDING-circuitbreaker-tier0-tier1 — new infrastructure: circuit-breaker feature flag (Tier 0) and its two cheapest RULES.md trip conditions, automated (Tier 1) (2026-07-17)

**Implements Tier 0 and Tier 1 exactly as scoped in `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_CircuitBreakerMonitoringGap.md`, the owner-reviewed proposal for the RULES.md circuit-breaker/monitoring gap.** This is new, additive infrastructure — no existing behavior, calculation, or code path changes. Nothing here is wired into any request-routing decision yet (there is no legacy-vs-new-path split anywhere in the codebase to route between); it only makes the flag and two of the seven trip conditions real for the first time.

**Tier 0 — the flag mechanism:**
- New table `public.m_circuit_breaker_state` (`docs/br099_circuit_breaker_state.sql`), single row (id=1, seeded `NORMAL`), system-wide (deliberately not org/unit-scoped — a breaker trip is about the shared codebase's health).
- New `CircuitBreakerState` entity (extends `CommonEntity`, not `BaseEntity` — no org/unit scoping), DTO, DAO (`ICircuitBreakerStateDao`/`CircuitBreakerStateDaoImpl`), service (`ICircuitBreakerStateService`/`CircuitBreakerStateServiceImpl`), and REST controller (`CircuitBreakerController`: `GET /global/circuitbreaker/state`, `POST /global/circuitbreaker/flip`, `POST /global/circuitbreaker/reset`) — all following this codebase's existing DAO/Service/Controller/`Response<>` conventions exactly (see `UnitApplicationBillingConfigurationDaoImpl`/`ReferalDetailsController` as the templates used).

**Tier 1 — condition 3 (shadow test failure rate), automated:**
- New `.github/workflows/reactor-tests.yml`: runs the full 141-test (now 146, +5 from this change) reactor suite (`mvn -f legacy/pom.xml clean test`) on every push/PR to `main`. Any test failure fails the CI job — that IS the trip signal for condition 3. This does not auto-flip the Tier 0 flag (CI has no access to the running app's database); it's a red build, same as any other CI failure, for a human to act on.

**Tier 1 — condition 7 (unhandled exception count > 5 / 24h), automated:**
- `ErrorCorrelationUtil.newCorrelationId()` (already called at all 11 existing call sites across ADT/Global/HIS-WAR's `GlobalExceptionHandler`) now also records the occurrence in a rolling 24h window (`ConcurrentLinkedDeque<Instant>`, pruned on read); new `countInLast24Hours()` query method. Correlation IDs are generated before any downstream handler logic runs, so this correctly counts the original error event even in the one path where the handler itself later crashes (`BR-FIXED-globalexceptionhandler-null-customexception-crash`, fixed 2026-07-17 — logged as `BR-PENDING-...` under this same ID at the time this Tier 0+1 work was written) — see the proposal doc's note correcting an earlier claim that this bug would undermine the counter.
- New `CircuitBreakerMonitorScheduler` in HIS-WAR (`@Scheduled(fixedRate = 60000)`, matching RULES.md's 60-second interval literally): reads the count, and if it exceeds 5 AND the flag is currently `NORMAL`, flips it to `LEGACY` with a reason and logs a distinctly-tagged `CIRCUIT_BREAKER_TRIP` line. Never auto-resets — returning to `NORMAL` is a deliberate owner action via the `/reset` endpoint, not something the scheduler decides. No external alert channel (email/Slack) is wired — explicitly named as an open decision in the proposal doc's summary, not invented here.

**Tier 1 — condition 6 (try/catch removed without centralized routing), automated:**
- New `scripts/check_trycatch_removal.py`: diff-based heuristic PR check (same "candidate for manual verification" spirit as this project's other heuristic scripts, e.g. `find_swallowed_return_pattern.py`) — scans the diff for removed `catch (` lines; fails if none of the diff or PR body references `GlobalExceptionHandler`/`ErrorCorrelationUtil`/"centralized handler"/"routed to". Wired into `reactor-tests.yml` on `pull_request` events.

**Not implemented (explicitly out of scope for this change, per the proposal doc's Tiers 2/3):** condition 1 ($0.01 divergence — blocked on the same real-data-access decision as `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ShadowTestHarnessApproach.md`), condition 2 (feature-flag-service health check — trivial once needed, not built since nothing depends on flag availability yet), conditions 4/5 (DB replication lag / CPU — infrastructure provisioning, not application code).

**Owner instruction:** implement Tier 0+1 exactly as scoped in the proposal doc; full reactor suite must still pass; commit on its own branch, do not merge/push without review.

Classification: Neither (new infrastructure, no existing calculation/behavior changed). Owner Decision: Pending.

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 146/146 (141 baseline + 5 new — `ErrorCorrelationUtilCircuitBreakerCounterTest` (2), `CircuitBreakerStateServiceImplTest` (3)). Verified via literal `javac [debug target 21]`/`[debug release 21]` invocation across all recompiled modules (Global's 2676-file compile includes all new Tier 0+1 source). One local `try/catch`-adjacent change: none — no existing try/catch blocks removed or altered anywhere in this change. One new database table added (`m_circuit_breaker_state`), applied via `docs/br099_circuit_breaker_state.sql`, additive only, Expand-Migrate-Contract compliant. No business term used outside `docs/GLOSSARY.json`'s existing vocabulary — this is infrastructure terminology (circuit breaker, feature flag), not a new billing/clinical business term.

## BR-PENDING-pharmacy-billlistservice-savebilldetails-batch1 — `BillListService.saveBillDetails` characterized (outstanding-amount + overpayment-deposit branches); scope of the "5 live financial calls" corrected to 14; 2 findings logged, neither fixed (2026-07-17)

**First batch of Pharmacy's characterization pass**, re-verifying and extending a prior session's list of "5 live financial-calculation calls" flagged as outside this project's characterization scope. An agent-assisted re-verification (grep + read, not trusted from memory) confirmed the original 5 (`saveDeposit`, `savePaymentDepositDetails`, `getBillByEncounterId`, `getPreAuthAmutByVisitId`, `updatePOSMachineResponseMapperFlag`) are real, but the list was **incomplete**: **14 live financial-calculation call sites** exist across Pharmacy into Global's billing DAOs/services, not 5. `BillListService.saveBillDetails` alone accounts for 9 of the 14 (including 2 originally-listed calls, `saveDeposit`/`savePaymentDepositDetails`, plus 7 more: `ccAvenueCheckvalidation`, `updateDepositMaster`, `getDepositDetailsByDepositId`, `updateDepositDetails`, `updateDueAmountByBillBilPayeeId`, and `updatePOSMachineResponseMapperFlag` called twice). The remaining 5 call sites are in `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` (`postServicesIntoExistingBill` — the single most financially substantial call of all 14, computing tax/net/self/credit-payable per order line), `SalesReturnService.salesReturnSave` (`getTaxDetail`), `PatientConsumableIssueService.patientConsumableIssueDetails` (`getTariff`), and `ReturnAcceptanceConsumableService.returnAcceptanceDetails` (`getActualRateFromOpeningBalance`) — none of these 5 are covered by this batch, logged as open scope for follow-up batches, not silently dropped. Two commented-out/dead call sites in `SalesReturnService.java` (`getTaxDetail` at line 643, `getTaxDetailBP` inside a fully-commented `salesReturnSaveBP` method) were explicitly ruled out as non-live.

**This batch characterizes `BillListService.saveBillDetails`'s outstanding-amount computation and overpayment-creates-a-deposit branches only** (production source lines ~320-452) — deliberately not the existing-deposit CONSUMPTION loop (~454-574) or the full payment-mode dispatch logic inside the private `buildSalePaymentModes` helper (mpesa, POS-card, loyalty-card, cheque, e-transfer, razorpay/ccavenue branches), both logged as deferred to a follow-up batch. 8 pinning tests added (`BillListServiceSaveBillDetailsCharacterizationTest`), all against the real production method via Mockito, no logic reimplemented in the tests.

**Findings (both characterized, neither fixed, neither judged an urgent active money-loss finding — see reasoning below):**

1. **Asymmetric outstanding-amount formula between the invoice-generated Y and N branches for the identical paid>balance shape.** When `getIsInvoiceGeneratied()=="Y"` and `paidAmount > balanceAmount`, `outstandingAmount` is hardcoded to `0.00` regardless of its prior value. When `getIsInvoiceGeneratied()=="N"` for the exact same paid/balance/outstanding inputs, `outstandingAmount` instead becomes `outstanding - balanceAmount` (a real subtraction, not a reset to zero). Confirmed via two tests using identical fixture values (outstanding=800, balance=500, paid=700): the Y branch yields `0.00`, the N branch yields `300.00`. Not fixed — this may be intentional (invoice-generated implies the balance is already fully invoiced/settled elsewhere), but it's a real, material difference in the same paid/balance shape and worth an owner read.
2. **`balanceAmount` can be omitted by the caller and silently defaults to `sale.getTotalSelfPayable()`; when `paidAmount` equals that default exactly, `outstandingAmount` can go negative** (test: outstanding=800, totalSelfPayable/defaulted-balance=1000, paid=1000 → resulting `outstandingAmount = -200.00`), and no deposit or credit record is created for the difference, because the overpayment-deposit block requires `paidAmount` to be *strictly greater than* `balanceAmount` (not `>=`), so an exact match never triggers it. Not flagged as urgent: a negative outstanding balance is a plausible intentional representation of "patient has a credit," and no evidence was found that this loses track of real money (no live production trace, unlike the fast-tracked fixes earlier this session) — logged as a business-intent question for the owner, not a proven defect.
3. **(Secondary, noted in the test but not separately numbered as a top-level finding.)** If `iDepositMasterDao.saveDeposit(...)` returns a non-success `Response` (e.g., `getObject()==null`), the method does not stop or throw — it silently skips creating the compensating `PaymentDepositDetails` record and proceeds to save the bill as if nothing were wrong. This is a failure-path gap (only reachable if the underlying deposit save itself fails), not a happy-path bug, so it's logged as a moderate, non-urgent finding for the same follow-up review, not elevated to the two numbered findings above.

**Owner instruction:** characterize Pharmacy's live financial calls with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/gain or misallocation immediately rather than holding for a batch summary. Neither finding above met that immediate-flag bar (see reasoning per finding); both are reported here, at the end of this batch, as ordinary characterization findings. Findings 1 and 2 above are tracked as **6a** and **6b** in `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md`, in the same plain-language, single-sitting format as findings 2a/4b — both need an owner/business-workflow answer, not a coding decision.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch). Owner Decision: Pending — both findings need an outside business/workflow answer (see `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md` §6).

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 154/154 (146 baseline + 8 new, all in `BillListServiceSaveBillDetailsCharacterizationTest`). Pharmacy's `pom.xml` test dependencies modernized (mockito-core bumped 1.10.19→5.11.0, matching every other module's shadow-tested version; `junit-jupiter`, `mockito-junit-jupiter`, `assertj-core` added) — additive, test-scope only, no production dependency touched. No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-pharmacy-postservicesintoexistingbill-batch2 — `postServicesIntoExistingBill` characterized (the single most financially substantial of Pharmacy's 14 calls); 1 structural finding logged, not fixed, not confirmed reachable via the traced call site (2026-07-17)

**Second batch of Pharmacy's characterization pass**, covering the call this session's research flagged as the single most financially substantial of the 14 live cross-module financial calls: `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` → `globalBillingService.postServicesIntoExistingBill(ids)` (fired when a walk-in sale is added to an existing bill), landing in `BillingServiceImpl.postServicesIntoExistingBill` (Global module), which computes tax/net/self/credit-payable per order-detail line and aggregates them onto the bill's running totals.

**11 pinning tests added, split across two files** (branch `characterize/pharmacy-financial-calls-batch2`, off `main`, independent of batch 1's branch):
- `WalkInSalesControllerSaveBillCharacterizationTest` (Pharmacy, 3 tests): pins the controller-level trigger condition — `postServicesIntoExistingBill` is called only when `addToBill==true` AND the save produced a non-empty ids list; never called otherwise.
- `BillingServiceImplPostServicesIntoExistingBillCharacterizationTest` (Global, 5 tests): pins the actual money math — non-package `totalAmount` uses `ordTotalAmt`; the package-consumption branch instead uses `packageMapper.serviceAmount * orderQty` (confirmed via a fixture where using the wrong formula would produce a materially different, easily-detected result); existing `BillingMaster` totals are correctly **accumulated** (`+=`), not overwritten — explicitly checked because this is exactly the "reassign instead of accumulate" bug shape found repeatedly earlier this session in `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl`, and this method does NOT have that bug; the 3-way `billStatusId` derivation (0/exact-match/else) is pinned for all three branches; a 2-order-detail-same-bill test confirms both lines' contributions are summed, not last-one-wins.

**One structural finding, logged but not elevated to urgent:** `billId` and the five running-total accumulator variables (`taxAmount`/`netAmount`/`totalAmount`/`selfPayable`/`creditPayable`) are all scoped to the whole method, not per-bill. If `orderDetailsIdsList` ever contains order-details belonging to **different** bills in one call, only the **last** order-detail's bill gets its `BillingMaster` aggregate fetched and updated — but that one bill's update carries the **summed** total of every order-detail in the whole call, including ones that actually belong to other, never-touched bills. Pinned with a dedicated test (`twoOrderDetailsDifferentBills_onlyLastBillUpdated_withBothAmountsSummed`). **Not confirmed reachable via the one call site this session traced**: `WalkInSalesController.saveBill` passes `walkinResponse.getIds()` from a single `WalkInSalesRequest` (one walk-in sale transaction), which in ordinary usage resolves to a single bill — no evidence was found of a real path that batches order-details from multiple bills into one call. Logged for the owner's awareness given `postServicesIntoExistingBill` is a public, reusable service method other callers could invoke differently, not escalated as an urgent active-money-loss finding absent that evidence.

**Deliberately out of scope this batch, deferred:** the payee-priority/sponsor-consolidation branch (production lines ~371-451, reached only when an order-detail's payee-mapper list is non-empty; every fixture here used an empty list to isolate the accumulation arithmetic) and the EHC multi-service-package branch (`isPackage()==true` plus OP visit type). Remaining 3 of the original 14 call sites (`SalesReturnService.getTaxDetail`, `PatientConsumableIssueService.getTariff`, `ReturnAcceptanceConsumableService.getActualRateFromOpeningBalance`) also remain open for a future batch.

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/gain or misallocation immediately. The multi-bill structural finding was evaluated against that bar and judged not to meet it (no evidence of reachability), so it's reported here rather than flagged mid-batch.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch).

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 154/154 (146 Tier-0/1 baseline + 5 new in Global + 3 new in Pharmacy). This branch is independent of batch 1's branch (both off `main`); Pharmacy's `pom.xml` test-dependency modernization (same as batch 1) was reapplied here since branches don't share uncommitted work. No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-pharmacy-salesreturn-batch3 — `SalesReturnService`'s tax-refund calculation characterized (3 tests); a suspected data-integrity bug disproven by the test itself; `getTariff`'s 5 call sites and `getActualRateFromOpeningBalance` explicitly scoped out this batch, not silently dropped (2026-07-17)

**Third batch of Pharmacy's characterization pass**, covering the third of the original 14 live financial calls: `SalesReturnService.salesReturnSave` (called from public `saveSalesReturn`) → `GlobalDao.getTaxDetail(...)`, which back-calculates the tax portion embedded in a sales-return refund amount, one `SaleReturnDetailTaxComponenet` per tax component (e.g. a CGST+SGST split).

**3 pinning tests added** (`SalesReturnServiceCharacterizationTest`, Pharmacy): (1) pins the exact formula `calTaxAmt = (refundAmount * component's own taxPercentage) / (100 + item's taxPercentage)`, and confirms the request item's own `discount` field is read into a local variable but has zero effect on the result (dead-variable characterization, not a fix); (2) confirms a multi-component `TaxDetail` list (e.g. CGST 9% + SGST 9%) adds a component for EVERY row, not just the first; (3) confirms two-item requests correctly attribute each tax component to its OWN item's `ReturnDetail`, not a shared/wrong one.

**A suspected bug, disproven by the test itself before being written up — recorded here as a methodology note, not a finding.** Reading the source, each tax component looked like it was being constructed with the WRONG `ReturnDetail` reference (`new ReturnDetail(returnObject.getReturnDetailList().getFirst().getId())` — literally "whichever return-detail was added first", not the current item's own). A first version of test (3) asserted exactly that mismatch and the assertion **failed** — investigating why showed `ReturnDetail.addSaleDetailTaxComponenet(...)` (the method that actually attaches the component, called immediately after construction) unconditionally calls `saleReturnDetailTaxComponenet.setReturnDetail(this)`, overwriting whatever the constructor was given. The mismatched constructor argument is discarded, dead construction — the final persisted state is correct. Rewritten to assert the real (correct) behavior instead. Kept in the manifest as a reminder that a plausible-looking read of the source is not the same as a verified one — write the test.

**Two of the original 14 call sites explicitly scoped out of this batch, not silently dropped:**
- **`getTariff`** (5 call sites, all inside two large ~300-line, non-decomposed methods — `patientConsumableIssueDetails` and `patientConsumableIssueAcceptanceDetails` in `PatientConsumableIssueService.java`): each call site sits ~200 lines deep behind a long chain of nested object-graph prerequisites (`issueDetail.getPatientIndentDetail().getItem().getGeneric().getProductCategory()...` and similar). Building a fixture to reach any one of them would cost substantially more than the ~15-line markup-lookup logic it contains is worth characterizing in isolation. Deferred to a future batch if the owner wants this specific logic covered; the logic itself (silently takes the first matching tariff row if more than one exists, per an explicit `// Don't throw - get first object and set data` comment in the source, and defaults `markUpDown=1`/`markUpDownPer=0.00` when no tariff row is found) was read and is noted here for awareness even though it isn't pinned by a test.
- **`getActualRateFromOpeningBalance`** (`ReturnAcceptanceConsumableService.returnAcceptanceDetails`): also embedded behind a similarly deep nested object graph. Additionally, and more decisively: the call itself is a pure pass-through (confirmed via its `GlobalDao` implementation — a native SQL `select rate from inventory.t_batch where id=... and unit_id=...`, no arithmetic at all) whose result is assigned directly to a response field (`item.setMrp(getActualRate)`) with no transformation. Given there is no calculation to get wrong, the cost of an elaborate fixture to test this specific call was judged not worth it.

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/gain or misallocation immediately. Nothing in this batch met that bar — the one suspected issue was disproven, and the two explicitly-deferred call sites carry no evidence of miscalculation (one wasn't reached at all, the other has no calculation to miscalculate).

**This closes out explicit examination of all 14 originally-flagged Pharmacy live financial-calculation calls.** Batches 1 and 2 covered `saveBillDetails` (9 calls) and `postServicesIntoExistingBill` (1 call, the `WalkInSalesController`→`BillingServiceImpl` pair); this batch covers `getTaxDetail` (1 call) and explicitly scopes out `getTariff` (5 calls, deferred) and `getActualRateFromOpeningBalance` (1 call, deferred, low-value). 8 of 14 fully characterized with pinning tests; 6 explicitly deferred with documented reasoning, not silently skipped.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch).

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 149/149 (146 Tier-0/1 baseline + 3 new). Pharmacy's `pom.xml` test-dependency modernization (same as batches 1/2) reapplied here since this branch is independent (off `main`). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-adt-financial-calls-batch1 — ADT's 3 originally-flagged live financial calls characterized; 2 confirmed CRASH bugs found (more severe than any prior Pharmacy finding), both silently swallowed by their ADT callers (2026-07-17)

**Characterizes all 3 of ADT's originally-flagged live financial calls**: `convertServicesFromOPToIP` (re-prices OP order-details onto a new IP order when a patient is admitted; triggered from both `BedAllocationServiceImpl.acceptNursingAcceptanceRequet` and `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission`), `ccAvenueCheckvalidation` (duplicate-transaction gate in `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission`, structurally identical to the already-characterized Pharmacy version), and `addDepositAgainstAdmission` (creates a deposit record during admission, triggered from `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission`). 9 pinning tests added across 3 files (`BillingServiceImplConvertServicesFromOPToIPCharacterizationTest`, `DepositMasterServiceImplAddDepositAgainstAdmissionCharacterizationTest` — both Global — and `BedAllocationControllerCcAvenueGateCharacterizationTest`, ADT). ADT's `pom.xml` had **no test dependencies at all** before this batch (not even an old Mockito version) — added the same modern `junit-jupiter`/`mockito-core`/`mockito-junit-jupiter`/`assertj-core` set used everywhere else, test-scope only.

**Two findings, both CONFIRMED CRASH BUGS via passing tests — more severe than any Pharmacy finding from batches 1-3, since those were ambiguous-behavior questions, not crashes:**

1. **`convertServicesFromOPToIP` throws an uncaught exception (wrapped as `BillingException`) whenever a service being converted has no matching package/contract** — a plausible, non-exotic condition (e.g. a self-pay patient, or a service simply not covered by any active package). Root cause: production code reassigns its own local `consumptionDto` variable to the result of `checkServiceAvailableInPackageContract(...)` (`res.getObject()`), correctly null-checks it before the field-copy block, but then **unconditionally dereferences the same now-possibly-null variable two lines later** (`consumptionDto.getOrderDetailsPayeeMapperDtosList()`) to build the DAO save call — with no guard for the not-found case. Confirmed via a passing test asserting `BillingException` is thrown. **This is called from `BedAllocationServiceImpl.acceptNursingAcceptanceRequet`, whose own try/catch is entirely empty (`catch (Exception e) { }`, no logging at all) — meaning this crash is completely silent to anyone watching logs, and the caller returns a generic `COMMON_ERROR_MESSAGE` response even though the underlying nursing-acceptance step (`iBedAllocationDao.acceptNursingAcceptanceRequet(admission)`) may have already committed successfully before the crash.** A second trigger point exists in `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission`, also wrapped in one large silent catch block.
2. **`addDepositAgainstAdmission` throws an uncaught exception (wrapped as `BillingException`, an NPE underneath) whenever the deposit-master save doesn't return a success code with a non-null object** — production declares `DepositMaster depositMaster = null;` up front, only assigns it inside the save-success branch, then **unconditionally dereferences it on the method's own success return path** (`depositMaster.getDepositMasterId()`) with no guard for the failure case. This is a **more severe version of the analogous gap already logged for Pharmacy's `saveBillDetails`** (`BR-PENDING-pharmacy-billlistservice-savebilldetails-batch1`, finding 3/"secondary") — Pharmacy's version silently continues and skips creating a compensating record; this ADT version crashes outright, with no response returned at all. Confirmed via a passing test asserting `BillingException` is thrown.

**Why these are flagged as more urgent than the Pharmacy 6a/6b findings**: those needed a business-workflow judgment call about ambiguous-but-defined behavior. These two are unambiguous crash bugs on a plausible, ordinary path through core admission workflows, both swallowed silently by their own callers with zero logging — squarely the CLAUDE.md #1 priority concern ("excessive local try/catch blocks that silently swallow errors, particularly around billing"). Flagged to the owner immediately upon confirmation (not held for this batch summary), consistent with the standing "flag anything urgent immediately" instruction.

**Not fixed** — characterization only, per standing instruction. Not proposed for a fix yet either; unlike the `GlobalExceptionHandler` crash (which already has a `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_*.md` proposal), these are logged here first for the owner to decide whether they warrant the same proposal-doc treatment, given their severity.

**Deliberately out of scope this batch:** the rest of `BedAllocationController.savePatientAdmission` and `BedAllocationServiceImpl.savePatientAdmission` (bed-booking checks, patient-sponsor priority, transfer creation, medico-legal details, waiting-list updates) — both are 270+/600+ line methods behind one try/catch each with many prerequisites; this batch calls the Global-side destinations of the 3 flagged calls directly (same strategy used for Pharmacy's `postServicesIntoExistingBill`), rather than driving the full ADT controller/service methods end-to-end.

**Owner instruction:** characterize ADT's live financial calls, don't fix anything, flag anything urgent immediately. Both findings above are flagged here as urgent given they are confirmed, reproducible crashes on ordinary paths, not merely theoretical risks.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch). Owner Decision: Pending — both crash findings need the owner's decision on whether to fast-track a fix (same track as `GlobalExceptionHandler`) or hold for a later pass.

## BR-PENDING-pharmacy-savebilldetails-depositconsumption-paymentmodes-batch4 — two more `saveBillDetails`-adjacent branches characterized: the deposit-consumption loop (same defect class as `consumeDeposit`) and the payment-mode dispatch helper (a confirmed dangling-`else` dead-code bug) (2026-07-17)

**Fourth batch of Pharmacy's characterization pass**, covering two remaining pieces of `BillListService` not reached by batch 1: the deposit-consumption loop inside `saveBillDetails` (draws down a patient's deposit records against the bill being saved) and the private helper `buildSalePaymentModes` (dispatches a `BillSaveRequest`'s payment entries into `SalePaymentMode` records by mode: cash/card/gateway/cheque/eTransfer/loyalty-card/M-Pesa).

**Deposit-consumption loop (`BillListServiceDepositConsumptionCharacterizationTest`, 4 tests):** confirms this loop has the **exact same defect shape already logged for Billing's `consumeDeposit`** (`BR-PENDING-consumedeposit-findings`) — the "is there still an amount left to consume" guard is checked ONCE before the loop starts, not per iteration, so once the amount is fully consumed by an early row, every subsequent deposit record in the list still receives a real (no-op) `updateDepositDetails` database write. Also confirms the same "fill in the blank only fires backwards" `billId` null-check pattern as `consumeDeposit`'s finding 4b: it only overwrites `billId` when the field is **already non-null**, never filling it in when blank. Both confirmed via passing test assertions. This is now the **second independent occurrence** of this exact defect pair — worth treating as a pattern, not a one-off, whenever either gets addressed.

**`buildSalePaymentModes` (`BillListServiceBuildSalePaymentModesCharacterizationTest`, 8 tests, private method invoked via reflection):**

1. **MAJOR FINDING (dead code, not a calculation bug): the loyalty-card mailing/notification call is structurally unreachable.** Production source has `for(...mailList...) { iMailingService.consumeLoyaltyAmount(...) }` written directly after an `else` clause with no braces — by Java's dangling-`else` grammar rule, only the `for` loop's own first statement binds to that `else`; the loop itself is NOT conditional on the intended branch and, once traced fully, the loop body can never execute on the path that has a non-empty `mailList` to notify. Confirmed via a test that first asserted the call WAS made (failed — "zero interactions with this mock"), then was corrected after re-reading the exact source lines to assert the real, confirmed behavior instead: `verify(iMailingService, never()).consumeLoyaltyAmount(any())`. A loyalty-card payment is still correctly added to the returned payment-mode list; only the notification side-effect is dead.
2. Gateway mode routes by provider ID (including a dedicated CCAvenue path); cheque and eTransfer modes map their fields correctly; the loyalty-card branch's own list-object null-check is confirmed to run in a "backwards" order relative to its balance-sufficiency check (pinned as-is, not flagged as urgent — no evidence of an unreachable or lost-payment condition in ordinary use, unlike finding 1).

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/gain or misallocation immediately. Neither finding here is a money-calculation error — the deposit-consumption loop's no-op writes are pure waste (same as the already-logged `consumeDeposit` twin), and the dead mailing call only affects whether a notification is sent, not any amount. Not elevated to urgent.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch).

**Notes:** `mvn -pl Procurement -am test` / `mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am test` style targeted runs plus a full reactor pass: 12 new tests, all passing. No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-pharmacy-postservicesintoexistingbill-ehc-payeepriority-batch5 — the two branches deferred out of batch 2 now characterized; MAJOR FINDING: EHC add-on service amounts are excluded from the bill's own total/tax/net accumulators even though the add-on line items ARE saved and marked billed (2026-07-17)

**Fifth batch of Pharmacy's characterization pass, closing out `postServicesIntoExistingBill`** (`BillingServiceImpl`, Global) — the two branches batch 2's test class explicitly deferred: the EHC multi-service-package fan-out (production lines ~282-334, gated on `orderDetailsMasterDto.isPackage() && vistDetailsObj.getVisitTypeId()==OP`) and the payee-priority/sponsor-consolidation branch (production lines ~371-451, reached once per payee on an order-detail whenever that payee's `updatePayeeDetailsByPayeeAndBillId` call reports "not found" for this bill). 6 new pinning tests (`BillingServiceImplPostServicesIntoExistingBillEhcAndPayeePriorityCharacterizationTest`, Global).

**MAJOR FINDING (money-accounting gap, flagged to the owner — see `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md` §7): EHC add-on service amounts never reach the bill's aggregate totals.** When a package order-detail on an OP visit has EHC ("add-on") child services attached, each child is correctly saved as its own `BillingDetailsDto` row (so it appears as a line item) and its order-detail ID IS folded into the set passed to `updateIsBilledStatus` (so it gets marked billed) — but the method's running `totalAmount`/`taxAmount`/`netAmount`/`selfPayable`/`creditPayable` accumulators, which feed directly into the `BillingMaster` aggregate update at the end of the method (`netAmount`/`totalBillAmount`/`gst`/`outstanding`), are populated ONLY from the ONE parent order-detail (production lines 346-362, which execute once per parent, entirely outside the EHC child loop at lines 289-333). Confirmed via a passing test: a parent contributing 100.00/5.00/105.00 (total/tax/net) alongside one EHC child worth 2331.00 (777.00 base price × 3 quantity) results in a `BillingMaster` update reflecting only the parent's 100.00/5.00/105.00 — the child's 2331.00 is present in `billing_details` but absent from the bill's own headline total/outstanding figures. **Not yet confirmed how often this combination occurs in real billing history** (access to that is separately on hold); not proven as lost money, but a genuine line-items-vs-total disagreement, flagged per the standing "any active money loss/misallocation gets flagged immediately" instruction out of caution.

**Payee-priority/sponsor-consolidation branch, 4 more tests, no findings beyond the structural notes below:**
- The insert path (first time this payee appears on this bill, `updatePayeeDetailsByPayeeAndBillId` returns `0`) correctly re-syncs priority/payment-entitlement on all of the bill's existing payee rows AND applies the same sponsor-match logic to the newly-inserted row — confirmed both when a sponsor match is found (by `payeeId` alone, when the payee has no associate company; also separately by `payeeId`+`associateCompanyId` when it does) and when no match is found (priority/entitlement correctly stay `null`, not defaulted to anything).
- The update path (`i != 0`, payee already exists on this bill) correctly skips the entire insert/sponsor-sync path — `getPayeeDetailsByPayeeAndBillId` and `saveBillPayeeDetails` are never called.
- **Structural note, not a bug:** `iPatientSponsorDetailsDao.getSponsoredDetailsForUpdatePayee(...)` is called **unconditionally once per order-detail**, sitting BEFORE the per-payee `if(i==0)` check rather than inside it — so this sponsor lookup still runs even when every payee on that order-detail already exists and the result goes completely unused. A wasted, harmless DB call; pinned as-is, not elevated.
- **Duplication risk, noted for awareness, not itself a current bug:** the 15-line sponsor-matching stream logic (match by `payeeId`+`associateCompanyId` if present, else by `payeeId` alone) is written out twice, verbatim, in the same method (once for existing payee rows, once for the newly-inserted row) rather than shared. Both copies are currently identical and correct; the risk is purely a future-maintenance one — a fix applied to one copy and not the other would silently diverge. No test needed since there is no current behavioral difference between the copies to pin.

**`getTariff` and `getActualRateFromOpeningBalance` remain explicitly deferred**, consistent with batch 3's documented reasoning (buried behind deep nested-object-graph fixtures; `getTariff` has a small but low-value first-match-wins/default-markup rule, `getActualRateFromOpeningBalance` is a pure single-row SQL pass-through with no calculation at all) — re-confirmed via research this batch, not silently dropped.

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/misallocation immediately. The EHC totals-exclusion finding above is flagged per that instruction; the payee-priority branch produced no findings meeting that bar.

**This closes out Pharmacy's remaining deferred characterization scope from batches 1-3** (item 8 of the session's consolidated tracking list) — `saveBillDetails`, `buildSalePaymentModes`, and both branches of `postServicesIntoExistingBill` are now all characterized; only the two explicitly-deferred low-value call sites (`getTariff`, `getActualRateFromOpeningBalance`) remain untested, by documented choice.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch). Owner Decision: Pending — the EHC totals-exclusion finding needs the owner's read on whether it's an active gap worth fixing, tracked in `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md` §7.

**Notes:** 6 new tests, all passing (`mvn -pl Global -am test`). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-hiswar-tdoctorshare-getdoctorshareservice — HIS-WAR's `TDoctorShareImpl.getDoctorShareService` characterized; confirms the "mark as posted" step is commented out (re-processing risk on repeat manual runs) and an unusual growing-page-size pagination pattern (2026-07-17)

**Characterizes `TDoctorShareImpl.getDoctorShareService()`** (`legacy/HIS-WAR`), item 9 of the session's consolidated tracking list. This method is reachable ONLY via a manual internal developer endpoint (`GET /doNotHit/docShare`, wired in `ExecuteBatchForcefully.java`) — **it is NOT the live, nightly-scheduled doctor-share batch**, which is a separate class (`ITDoctorShareService.getProcessDocShare`, called from `MidNightBatchWithTimeZone.java`) outside this batch's scope. The sibling method on the same class, `getDoctorReferralShareService()`, is confirmed to have zero callers anywhere in the codebase (fully dead code) and is intentionally not characterized. `legacy/HIS-WAR/pom.xml` had only an ancient pre-JUnit4 `junit:junit` dependency (via parent `dependencyManagement`, no annotations available) before this batch — added the same modern `junit-jupiter`/`mockito-core`/`mockito-junit-jupiter`/`assertj-core` set used everywhere else, test-scope only. 4 pinning tests added (`TDoctorShareImplCharacterizationTest`).

**Findings, none elevated to urgent given how rarely this manual-only path is actually exercised (see `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md` §8):**

1. **The "mark this record as already processed" call is commented out in the source** (`//iBillingDetailsDao.updateBillDetailsIsDoctorSharePostedByBillId(...)`), confirmed via a passing test asserting it is never invoked even immediately after a successful save. This means nothing stops the same billing records from being calculated and saved again if this manual endpoint is ever triggered a second time over an overlapping window — a real double-posting risk, though one that depends on someone actually re-running this rarely-used manual endpoint.
2. **Pagination requests a growing page size rather than a fixed one**: page 1 asks for up to 1,000 rows (`startPoint=0, maxResults=1000`), page 2 asks for up to 2,000 (`startPoint=1000, maxResults=2000`), page 3 up to 3,000, and so on — `maxResults` grows by +1,000 each round while `startPoint` only advances by the PREVIOUS round's `maxResults`. Confirmed via a passing test capturing the exact arguments sent across 3 sequential calls. Noted for awareness as an unusual pattern; not itself proven to cause double-processing independent of finding 1 above (this session did not have visibility into the DAO's own query semantics to confirm or rule out overlap).
3. `unitId` is hardcoded to the literal `0` on every call, regardless of any real unit context — confirmed via the same test.
4. Rows with a `null` "share percentage" are silently skipped (no save, no log) — confirmed via a passing test; this is an existing, unconditional guard in the source (`if(tDoctorShareRespDto.getSharePer()!=null)`), not something introduced by null-coalescing elsewhere.
5. Share calculation formula pinned as-is: `amount = totalAmount - discount`; `docShare = (sharePer/100) * amount`; `hospShare = amount - docShare`; `dueShareAmt = docShare`.

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/misallocation immediately. Finding 1 is flagged (in the triage document, not as an urgent same-day item) given its double-posting implication, but downgraded from "urgent" given the endpoint's manual-only, rarely-exercised nature — a materially different risk profile than the ADT crash bugs (`BR-PENDING-adt-financial-calls-batch1`), which sit on ordinary, frequently-exercised paths.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch). Owner Decision: Pending — tracked in `docs/TRIAGE_PendingFindings.md` §8.

**Notes:** 4 new tests, all passing (`mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am test`). `legacy/HIS-WAR/pom.xml` test-dependency modernization only (no production code touched). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

## BR-PENDING-procurement-vendorrate-passthrough — Procurement's originally-flagged live financial call characterized; confirms no vendor-rate selection/comparison logic exists on the Java side, no findings (2026-07-17)

**Characterizes `ServiceWorkOrderCreationServiceImpl.getDetails`** (`legacy/Procurement`), item 10 of the session's consolidated tracking list — the call to `IVendorServiceRateConfigDao.getVendorRatesListForService` originally flagged as one of this codebase's live cross-module financial calls. 3 pinning tests added (`ServiceWorkOrderCreationServiceImplCharacterizationTest`); no `pom.xml` change needed (Procurement's test dependencies were already fully modernized in an earlier session).

**No findings — confirmed via research and pinning tests that this call has no arithmetic or selection logic on the Java side at all.** The DAO's returned vendor-rate list (vendor name, rate, etc.) is placed onto the response DTO completely unmodified — confirmed via a test using a deliberately cheaper second vendor to prove nothing in Java picks the lower rate (any comparison/selection the user sees happens in the AngularJS frontend, out of this characterization's scope). The only real Java-side logic in this method is a fallback ternary: if a pending-service row doesn't already have a `billingBedCategoryId` set, it falls back to the request's `opBillingBedCategoryId`; if the row already has one, it's kept as-is and not overwritten. Both branches confirmed via passing tests, along with the `unitId` field being unconditionally overwritten from the request on every row regardless of its prior value.

**Owner instruction:** characterize with pinning tests, don't fix anything, flag any active real money loss/misallocation immediately. Nothing found meeting that bar — this is a straightforward read/pass-through path.

Classification: Neither (characterization only, no code change this batch).

**Notes:** 3 new tests, all passing (`mvn -pl Procurement -am test`). No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced.

**Notes:** `mvn clean test` (full reactor, all 19 modules): BUILD SUCCESS, 174/174 (149 baseline + 7 new in Global + 2 new in ADT). Verified via literal `javac [debug target 21]` invocation. No local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed, no business term introduced. ADT's `pom.xml` modernized with test-scope-only dependencies (previously had none).
